# Hive architecture

The service side is the `hive` binary and the `hive-protocol` crate (Stage 0); the app side is the Tauri app (`src-tauri`) and its React UI (`src`). On Windows the service runs inside WSL or on Windows itself, the user's choice ([App mode](#app-mode-1254)); on macOS it runs natively (5.2). The macOS app can also link to services on remote Linux hosts over ssh (Stage 26, [Remote hosts](#remote-hosts)), one link per host, beside its local one.
Decisions live in `docs/hive.md` (Portuguese). This file describes how the code implements them.

## Pieces

```
Windows                         WSL
┌────────────┐  wsl.exe   ┌──────────────┐  Unix socket  ┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ Tauri app  │◄──stdio───►│ hive bridge  │◄─────────────►│ hive daemon                  │
└────────────┘  (frames)  └──────────────┘               │  terminals (PTY + fish)      │
                                                          │  hook events → app           │
             Claude Code in a Hive terminal               │  unhooked-claude watcher     │
             └─ hooks ─► hive hook <event> ──────────────►└─────────────────────────────┘
```

On Windows without WSL, or when the user chooses it (native mode, 12.5), the same pieces run on Windows: the app runs `hive.exe bridge` itself, the bridge and the daemon meet over a named pipe instead of the socket, and the terminals are pseudoconsoles running PowerShell, Command Prompt or Git Bash ([Native Windows service](#native-windows-service-1252)).

| Command | Role |
|---|---|
| `hive daemon` | The service. It owns the PTYs, receives hook events and lives exactly as long as the app connection. `--remote` (a remote host's, started by `hive bridge --remote`): terminals run the user's login shell (26.14), and an app connection that gave `keep_secs` and drops leaves it running for that long (26.15). |
| `hive bridge` | stdio ↔ socket relay run by the app through `wsl.exe` (directly on macOS, and on Windows in native mode, over a named pipe there). It starts the daemon when needed. On a remote host the app runs it over ssh as `hive bridge --remote` (`--replace` first ends a running service of another version, Q8). |
| `hive hook <event> [--record FILE]` | Called by Claude Code hooks. Forwards one event and always exits 0. |
| `hive statusline` | Claude Code's `statusLine` in Hive's terminals: reports the 5-hour usage to the service, then runs the user's own statusline and prints what it prints (see [Session usage](#session-usage-121)). |
| `hive sheets <format> <budget>` | Hidden; run by the service only (23.7, `hive::workbook`): reads an `xlsx` / `xls` / `ods` workbook on stdin, writes `{"Ok": [sheets]}` or `{"Err": "why"}` on stdout, within a memory and time limit. |
| `hive badge <text…>` / `hive badge --clear` | Run inside a Hive terminal: sets or clears that terminal's label on its tab and agent row. Exits 2 without a valid `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID`, 1 when the service cannot be reached. |
| `hive worktree create/list/remove/hook-create/hook-remove` | Worktrees following Claude's convention. `list --json` prints them as JSON ([CLI output](#cli-output-2810)). |
| `hive agents [--json]` | 28.10: the running service's live agents, read only (`hive::query`), for scripts and status bars: it never starts, stops or talks to an agent. Exits 3 when no service is running, 1 on any other failure ([CLI output](#cli-output-2810)). |

### CLI output (28.10)

`hive worktree list --json` and `hive agents --json` print one line of JSON and a newline; these shapes are stable (a field may be added, none renamed or removed without a note here).

- `hive worktree list --json` (no service needed): `{"worktrees": [{"path", "branch", "bare", "prunable"}]}`, the main worktree first. `branch` is the short name, null when detached or bare; `prunable`: its folder is gone (git keeps it until `git worktree prune`). A path that is not UTF-8 has its odd bytes replaced (U+FFFD). Without `--json`: `path<TAB>branch` per line, `(detached)` / `(bare)` for no branch.
- `hive agents --json`: `{"agents": [...]}`, in terminal order, each `{"id", "terminal", "project", "worktree", "cwd", "title", "state", "pending", "since_ms", "activity", "subagents", "usage"}` (`agent_listed` in the [Message catalog](#message-catalog)): `id` the session id; `terminal` its `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID`; `project` and `worktree` the ids (paths) of the followed project and worktree containing its `cwd`, null outside every followed project; `title` its session name, null until known; `state` one of `ended`, `idle`, `working`, `with_subagents`, `waiting_you`, `error`, `waiting_answer`, `waiting_plan`, `waiting_permission` (see [Agent states](#agent-states)); `pending` whether it needs the user (as the app's counter); `since_ms` when `state` began (ms since the Unix epoch); `activity` its current tool call, or null; `subagents` its live subagents as in `agent_state` (`{id, agent_type, teammate_name, state, worktree, activity, since_ms}`); `usage` `{context_tokens, context_limit, output_tokens}`, null until its transcript was read. Only agents in Hive's terminals are listed. Without `--json`: one line per agent, tab-separated: terminal, state, worktree (else `cwd`), context used as a percent of its window, title, activity; `-` for what is not known, control and invisible characters (bidi marks, zero-width spaces) as spaces; nothing when there are none.
- **No service running** (`hive agents`): nothing on stdout, `hive: no Hive service is running (<why>)` on stderr, exit code 3. Any other failure (a service of another version, a refusal, no answer within 10 s, a broken answer, more than 4096 agents): a message on stderr, exit code 1. The CLI connects only to its own user's service (`Paths::connect`, as `hive hook`) and never starts one.

### Remote hosts

```
macOS                                   Linux host (x86_64 or aarch64)
┌────────────┐ local link: /bin/sh  ┌─────────────┐   ┌──────────────────────────┐
│ Tauri app  │◄──────stdio─────────►│ hive bridge │◄─►│ hive daemon (this Mac's) │
│ React UI   │                      └─────────────┘   └──────────────────────────┘
│            │  one link per host: /usr/bin/ssh -T … -- <alias> <constant command>
│ Remotes ◄──┼──────stdio (frames)───────────────► ┌──────────────────────┐ socket ┌─────────────────────────┐
│ limits     │                                     │ hive bridge --remote │◄──────►│ hive daemon --remote    │
└────────────┘                                     │ (~/.hive/bin/hive)   │        │  terminals ($SHELL -l)  │
                                                   └──────────────────────┘        │  rings, hooks, git      │
                                                                                   └─────────────────────────┘
```

- **One link per host** (`src-tauri/src/remote.rs`, `Remotes`): the local service's link, plus one per alias of the local service's `remote.hosts` setting. A remote link is `/usr/bin/ssh -T -o BatchMode=yes -o StrictHostKeyChecking=yes -o ClearAllForwardings=yes -o ForwardAgent=no -o ForwardX11=no -o ForwardX11Trusted=no -o ServerAliveInterval=10 -o ServerAliveCountMax=3 -o ConnectTimeout=15 -- <alias> "exec /bin/sh -c '<script>' hive <version>"`, each a separate argument: the alias (`check_host`) is the only part that varies, and the script is compile-time text. Everything else (`HostName`, `User`, `ProxyJump`, keys, the agent's keys, `ControlMaster`) is the user's `~/.ssh/config`; neither the agent nor X11 is ever forwarded (`ForwardAgent=no`, `ForwardX11=no` and `ForwardX11Trusted=no` win over a `yes` there; `ClearAllForwardings` clears neither).
- **Install:** the script runs `~/.hive/bin/hive bridge --remote` when its `--version` is the app's, else prints `hive-needs <uname -s> <uname -m>`. A Linux x86_64 or aarch64 host then gets the app's bundled static `hive-linux-<cpu>` (26.13) on a second constant command's stdin, its first line `<byte count> <sha256>`: checked on the host by size and `sha256sum` before it ever runs, then by `--version`, and only then renamed over `hive`. Every other OS or CPU is refused, and nothing is uploaded.
- **Keep and reattach:** the remote `hello` carries `keep_secs` (`remote.keep_minutes` × 60; default 1440 minutes, 1–10080) and a per-launch `app_id`. A remote service whose link drops without `bye {end: true}` keeps its terminals that long, each writing into a 1 MiB ring (`hive::ring`) with its PTY never paused. A new link of the same `app_id` takes over a stale one; the service lists `terminals_kept`, and the app's `attach_terminal {from}` gets the missing bytes, or `terminal_reset` and the whole ring ([App disconnect](#app-disconnect-or-sigterm), [Reattach](#reattach-after-a-dropped-link-2616)).
- **Trust boundary:** a remote is someone else's machine, a less trusted peer than the local service. Its frames meet the codec's limits as every link's do, then the app's own (`src-tauri/src/limits.rs`, 26.19): message rates, list sizes, unacknowledged terminal output and the write queue are capped, and its text is clipped; past a cap the link is dropped (`disconnected {kind: limit}`) and not retried. Of its control messages, the link acts itself on `REMOTE_LINK` (`welcome`, `version_mismatch`, `terminals_kept`, `terminal_reset`), never passed on, and passes the UI only the types in `REMOTE_TO_UI` (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`), each read again as its `Control` type; every other one is dropped before the pump acts on it. Nothing a remote names is ever opened or revealed on this Mac, its files get no `hive-file:` URL, and URLs open only as `https://` (or, from Markdown and terminals, `http://` and `mailto:`). The full rules: [Remote hosts over ssh](#remote-hosts-over-ssh-2617), items 1 and 7.

## Module map

| Crate / module | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `hive-protocol` | Frame codec, `Control` messages, handshake constants, internal event model (`AgentEvent`, `EventKind`), `AgentState`. Shared with the app. |
| `hive::cli` | clap subcommands. Runs each async command on a runtime that is dropped without waiting for a pending stdin read. |
| `hive::paths` | Runtime dir (`$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/hive`, or `/tmp/hive-<uid>`, checked to be ours and mode 0700), socket, lockfile, `daemon.log` (in `logs`: `~/Library/Logs/Hive` on macOS, D18.6), Hive's folder (22.1, D22.16: `$HIVE_HOME`, absolute only, else `~/.hive`; both `data` and `config` on Unix) with its bin dir, hooks settings, `spaces.json`, `projects.json`, `ports.json`, `offered-pulls.json`, `usage.json`, `accounts/` (22.3) and `settings.json`; `Paths::legacy` finds the pre-22.1 XDG folders (none when `HIVE_HOME` is set). `paths::canonical` resolves a path without Windows' `\\?\` prefix (what the service compares and hands git). |
| `hive::legacy` | Unix: when the service starts (with the lock, so after the previous service and its terminals ended), moves everything in the pre-22.1 data folder (`$XDG_DATA_HOME/hive` or `~/.local/share/hive`) and the old config folder's `settings.json` into Hive's folder (made 0700): a rename, else (another disk) a copy then the original removed; never over something already there, which stays in the old place (the old `bin/` once the app installed into `~/.hive/bin`). A failure puts back what moved, keeps the old folders in use for this run and says why in `daemon.log` and `welcome`'s `warning` (the status bar's "Old folders in use"). A `~/.local/bin/hive` link to the old `bin/hive` is pointed at the new one. |
| `hive::uninstall` | Uninstalling Hive (23.5, see [Uninstall](#uninstall-235)): what goes (`plan`, from the `HOME` and the service's `Paths`), removing it through `Remove` (the Trash on macOS, deleted elsewhere), and `hive uninstall`'s list and `[y/N]` prompt. |
| `hive::daemon` | Lockfile, socket (0600), the service's `State`, projects/health/files-panel work, shutdown. Split by job (9.20): `daemon::app` (handshake, app and hook connections, the app's frames, prioritized writer), `daemon::terminals` (open, input, output pump, close, the unhooked-`claude` tick), `daemon::agents` (detection from hooks, events, titles, the silence/usage tick, the snapshot for a new app, subagents' worktrees), `daemon::usage` (the 5-hour windows `hive statusline` reports, 12.1, kept in `usage.json`, 21.8). Also runs on native Windows (12.5.2), where no terminal opens yet. |
| `hive::terminal` | Spawns `fish -C 'set -gx PATH <bin> $PATH'` (on macOS and on a remote Linux service, `hive daemon --remote` (26.14), `terminal::login`: the user's `$SHELL` as a login shell, the bin dir first once its login files ran; zsh through Hive's `ZDOTDIR`, bash through Hive's rc file, fish with `-C`, `sh` and `dash` also with Hive's `ENV` file (not set when its path holds a `$`, a backtick or a backslash), any other shell only with the bin dir first on the `PATH` it starts with; on Linux `$SHELL` runs by the absolute path found for it, and a relative one is not used; `/bin/sh` on Linux when `$SHELL` is unset or cannot run, `/bin/zsh` on macOS when it is unset) on a PTY with `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID`, its space's environment entries and its worktree's `HIVE_*` entries. Handles input and resize, and ends process groups. Its `Output` carries a terminal's output to the app under 9.19's window; a remote service's (26.15) also fills a `hive::ring::Ring`, and `Output::detach` (the app dropped: only the ring, the PTY never paused), `Output::reattach` (an app back, 26.16: the bytes it missed, or a reset and the whole ring, then the window afresh) and `Output::kept` (read the ring) move it between apps. `ENXIO` from opening a PTY is reported as no pseudo-terminal left, naming macOS's `kern.tty.ptmx_max` (17.6). |
| `hive::scripts` | Project scripts (6.8): each worktree's block of 10 ports (`<data>/hive/ports.json`), the `HIVE_*` environment, and running the archive script (`sh -c`, on native Windows the terminals' shell; time limit, process group or job killed). |
| `hive::procs` | Minimal `/proc` reader (pid, pgrp, session, comm; skips zombies; `libproc` on macOS, `sysinfo` on native Windows), and the Claude session each `claude` process runs (8.16). |
| `hive::watch` | Pure state machine for the unhooked-`claude` warning. |
| `hive::osc` | Shell marks and terminal alerts (17.25): the OSC sequences a terminal's output carries that the service acts on, scanned across reads, size-limited and cleaned; each terminal's command timing, notification spacing and progress changes; the long-command rule. See [Shell marks](#shell-marks-and-terminal-alerts-1725). |
| `hive::states` | Pure agent state machine: hook events → state per agent and subagent, "the most urgent wins", PTY-silence reconciliation (the clock is passed in). |
| `hive::adapter` | `Adapter` trait and `ClaudeCode` adapter: raw hook payload → `AgentEvent` (raw payload kept). |
| `hive::anchor` | Line hashes, a bounded Myers diff of hash vectors, mapping a range through it, and finding a run again by its text and context (27.5; shared with 27.3), see [Line provenance](#line-provenance-275). |
| `hive::background` | An agent's background commands (28.8, see [Background commands](#background-commands-288)): their launches, Claude Code's `background_tasks` list, their end and whether it failed; the clock is passed in. |
| `hive::slim` | What `hive hook` forwards of a `PostToolUse`: the fields the service reads, and a file edit as line hashes (`hive_edit`, 27.5). |
| `hive::provenance` | Edit records and line maps per repository, re-sync, `who_wrote` (live, else `git blame` and the records before the commit), `session_files`, the store and its caps (27.5). |
| `hive::hook` | `hive hook`: reads stdin (512 KiB limit; 32 MiB for `PostToolUse`, slimmed by `hive::slim`), optionally records JSONL, sends with a 200 ms timeout, never to a socket in a runtime dir that is not ours (9.7). `hive badge` sends through the same hook-role connection. |
| `hive::statusline` | `hive statusline` (12.1): its input read and parsed, the report, the user's statusline found in their settings and run (`sh -c`; on Windows the shell Claude Code picks, 12.5.5). The service's side, the latest 5-hour window per Claude config folder, is `daemon::usage`. |
| `hive::bridge` | Relay plus detached daemon start (`setsid --fork`, stderr to `daemon.log`; on native Windows `CREATE_NO_WINDOW \| CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP \| CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB`, without the breakaway when the bridge's job forbids it). |
| `hive::wrapper` | Installs `<data>/hive/bin/claude` (sh wrapper; on Windows `claude.exe` and `hive.exe`, copies of `hive`, 12.5.5), `<data>/hive/hive-hooks.json` and the shared plugin's folder when the daemon starts; `real_claude` (`PATHEXT` on Windows) and the user's `PATH`. |
| `hive::logins` | Whether a Claude account has logged in (21.6): its Claude Code config file found as Claude Code finds it, and only whether it has an `oauthAccount` (16 MiB limit). |
| `hive::settings` | The user's settings (`<config>/hive/settings.json`): read at start (256 KiB limit; missing: the defaults; invalid: the defaults plus a warning, the file left alone), range checks, saved whole (temporary file + rename, 0600). The Claude accounts (12.2): their rules, the folder a new terminal gets (asked, else its space's, 22.5), the migration of the spaces' old Claude folders, and of the account selected before 22.5 into every space. |
| `hive::browser_cleanup` | What the agent's browser (removed in 26.11) left in an install that had it, cleaned when the service starts: see [Daemon start](#daemon-start) item 3. The only code naming it. |
| `hive::shared` | The plugin every Claude account shares (21.9): makes `<data>/hive/shared` when missing and lists its skills, agents, commands and MCP servers (`shared`). |
| `hive::query` | `hive agents` (28.10): asks the running service for its live agents on a hook-role connection (`list_agents`, 10 s for the whole answer, at most 4096 agents) and prints them as text or JSON; and `hive worktree list --json`'s JSON. Control characters in the text output print as spaces. See [CLI output](#cli-output-2810). |
| `hive::projects` | The projects the app follows, grouped in spaces, and groups of them (folders of repositories, 14.1): validation, `<data>/hive/spaces.json` (migrated from `projects.json`), worktrees per project for the sidebar (kept from the last `git worktree list` until forgotten, 9.14: on any change of the spaces, the app's create/remove/rename of a worktree, `State::worktrees_changed` (hooks, git's registry and each worktree's `HEAD`, 19.5)), placing an agent's `cwd` in a worktree, a terminal's space environment. |
| `hive::spaces` | Spaces (6.14): the rules (names, environment checks, one space per project, only an empty space deleted, never the last) and the environment entries a space's terminals get. |
| `hive::files` | The files panel's worktree: `git ls-files` listing (sorted, capped) and its ignored entries (14.2: one level of each open ignored folder), `notify` watches by `Strategy` (17.6: inotify on Linux, a watch per listed directory; FSEvents on macOS, one recursive watch on the worktree) with a `Filter` that keeps the listed directories, the open ignored folders and the git dir's `HEAD`/`index`, debounce (the clock is passed in). |
| `hive::fds` | Raises the soft `RLIMIT_NOFILE` to `min(hard, 10 240)` at service start (17.6); the app does the same (`hive_lib::fds`). |
| `hive::session_watch` | Claude's session logs and records of running `claude`s, watched with `notify` (D19.4, `files::watch_config`): each account's projects folder recursively (on Linux non-recursively, with each followed project's log folder in it, since inotify would add a watch per subfolder), its `sessions/` records folder beside it (only files) the same way; a watched folder removed or renamed is unwatched and followed again on the next pass. A change counts when it is a record, or a followed project's log folder or an entry directly in it (the folder name, as Claude encodes it, starting with a followed repository's or group's encoded folder); a subagent's log or another project's does not. A burst is one change, 500 ms after its last event (at most 2 s after its first, so a streaming agent updates the list at most every 2 s). The daemon's `watch_sessions` follows the accounts' folders again when the app sends `list_sessions`, and after each change sends the list (`State::sessions_changed`) when the app has one. An agent detected or ended (`SessionStart`, `SessionEnd`, its terminal's exit) pokes the same burst (`State::sessions_poke`), so a burst of them is one listing. |
| `hive::ring` | A kept terminal's last output (26.15): `Ring`, the last `RING` (1 MiB) bytes, the oldest dropped, with byte offsets counted from the terminal's first byte (`start`, `end`, and `since(from)`: the bytes from `from` on while it is still held, else `None`), for 26.16's replay. |
| `hive::registry` | Git's worktree registry of the current space's projects (9.36): the common git dir (`git rev-parse --git-common-dir`), its `worktrees/` and each entry in it, each watched non-recursively with `notify`, and each group's folder once, recursively (14.1, D19.2; on Linux the folder and the folders directly in it, non-recursively, as inotify watches every folder of a recursive watch); the watcher's thread drops events deeper in a group than an entry's `.git` (but the registries' and the worktrees' `HEAD`), so churn in a group's repositories never fills the queue; only `worktrees/` appearing or going, an entry in it, or a worktree's `HEAD` written (a branch switched by hand, 19.5) counts, or an entry of a group's folder made, removed or renamed, or a `.git` in one of its folders; a burst is one change, 300 ms after its last event (at most 1 s after its first). The daemon's `watch_registry` follows the projects (added, removed, a space switch) and, after each change or re-follow (so what changed while a registry was not watched is not lost), sends `projects` through `State::worktrees_changed`. |
| `hive::git` | Every git call: `git -C <dir>` with separate arguments and `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, stdout size-limited, stderr in the error; an optional time limit (`output_within`) that kills git and its children; `read_limited` for any size-limited read; `limited` runs any prepared command the same way (`hive::gh` uses it), keeping its stdout up to a limit (`Stdout::Max`, more is an error) or only its last bytes (`Stdout::Tail`, `read_tail`: a log of any length in bounded memory). Which git runs is resolved once (D18.3, 18.19: `git::resolved`, asked on a thread of its own at the service's start): on macOS `xcrun -f git` (`macos::apple_git`, 2 s, 4 KiB), whose path replaces Apple's `/usr/bin/git` stub (about 5 ms a call) when the stub is the first `git` on the `PATH` git gets; a git of the user's first on that `PATH` (Homebrew's, 17.5) still runs. When xcrun says the Command Line Tools are missing, no git call starts (each is a `NotFound` error, so macOS never asks to install them) and Diagnostics' `git_error` says why; any other xcrun failure keeps `git` on `PATH`. Elsewhere `git` on `PATH`, as before. |
| `hive::gh` | The GitHub CLI (9.30): `gh` on the user's `PATH` with separate arguments, stdin closed, the service's own `GH_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_TOKEN`/`GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`/`GITHUB_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`/`GH_HOST`/`GH_CONFIG_DIR`/`GH_REPO` removed, prompts, colour, pager and update notices off, `core.fsmonitor` and `log.showSignature` off for the git it runs (`GIT_CONFIG_*`), 30 s limit (5 s for the local `auth token` and `auth switch`). Lists the accounts (`gh auth status` parsed), gets an account's token (`gh auth token --hostname --user`), switches `gh`'s active account (`gh auth switch --hostname --user`), and `Gh::run`: any `gh` command as a space's terminal would run it. |
| `hive::checkout` | Worktrees from GitHub (17.24): a pull request checked out into `.claude/worktrees/pr-<number>` and an issue's worktree on `<number>-<title>`. `gh pr view` / `gh issue view --json` read as untrusted (the number must match); a pull request's branch must pass `git check-ref-format --branch` unchanged and fit 255 bytes, a title (or a fork's branch) becomes lowercase ASCII words, the whole branch name, prefix included, at most 64 bytes (`NAME_LIMIT`). A branch of the repository is fetched into its remote-tracking branch (`+refs/heads/<b>:refs/remotes/<remote>/<b>`) and checked out as a tracking branch of the same name; a fork's comes from the base remote's `refs/pull/<n>/head` onto `pr-<n>-<its branch>` at the head commit (no fork URL ever reaches git). A local branch that already exists is checked out as it is, with a note. Fetches carry the space's identity and token (`worktree::fetch`). Hive only prepares the worktree; the human starts `claude`. |
| `hive::pulls` | The pull requests view (9.31): the project's GitHub repository from its remotes (`gh`'s order: the `gh repo set-default` one, `upstream`, `github`, `origin`, the first; only on the space account's host, else github.com), the list in one GraphQL request (`pulls.graphql`), details (`gh pr view --json`), actions (`gh pr ready/close/merge/create`; a checkout through `hive::checkout`) through `Gh::run`, `gh`'s JSON read as untrusted `serde_json::Value` with size limits, and the list's `Cache` (at most one fetch per 2 minutes per project and account unless forced, longer after GitHub's rate limit). |
| `hive::offers` | Offers to remove a worktree once its pull request merged or closed (17.27): checks each `pulls` list the daemon sends against the project's worktrees and raises `pull_ended` once per pull request, kept in `<data>/hive/offered-pulls.json` (0600, the latest 1000 that fit its 256 KiB read limit; a reopened one is forgotten). Never removes anything. |
| `hive::commit` | The Changes panel's git actions (27.6, D27.3): staging (`git --literal-pathspecs add` / `reset`, the paths on stdin), `git commit --message=` with the space's identity (21.5), the push's target (`symbolic-ref`, `branch.<name>.remote`/`.merge`, `origin` with `--set-upstream` when there is none) and `git push` built by `push_args` (never forced, a test checks it), with the space's token (`remote_env`), never prompting (`worktree::quiet`). Git's output, hooks' included (`Stdout::Shown`), is the error shown. |
| `hive::workflows` | Running a workflow by hand (27.6): the `workflow_dispatch` workflows of a worktree's `HEAD` (`git ls-tree`, `git cat-file`, untrusted YAML through `serde_norway`), `gh workflow run --ref= --raw-field` through `pulls::run`, then the new run looked for (`gh run list --event=workflow_dispatch`) and answered as `actions` answers a re-run. |
| `hive::actions` | The Actions view (9.32), on `hive::pulls`' pieces (repository lookup, `run` helper, text helpers, `Cache`): the runs (`gh run list --json`, `--branch=` when filtered), a run's jobs and steps (`gh run view --json …,jobs`), a job's log tail (`gh api repos/<owner>/<name>/actions/jobs/<job>/logs`, read as `Stdout::Tail`), re-run and cancel (`gh run rerun [--failed]`, `gh run cancel`). |
| `hive::health` | A worktree's status for the sidebar (changed files and their lines, ahead/behind the main worktree's branch, merged, last commit) and the last status sent per worktree. |
| `hive::worktree` | Worktrees in `.claude/worktrees/<name>` on branch `worktree-<name>`; `.worktreeinclude` copy. `create_on(dir, name, Head)` makes one on `Head::New {branch, start}` (`-b`) or `Head::Existing(branch)` (as it is; a failed copy deletes only a new branch); `create` is it with `worktree-<name>`. `check_branch` refuses a name from outside over 255 bytes or one `git check-ref-format --branch` rejects or rewrites; `has_branch` tells a local branch; `fetch` is `git fetch --no-tags -- <remote> <refspec>` with an environment, never prompting, within 30 s (`fetch_base` uses it). |
| `hive::changes` | A worktree's changes against its base (`HEAD`, the merge-base with the main worktree's branch, a chosen commit or an agent's last turn, 27.2) for the files panel: the base (`against`), `git status`, `git diff --raw` and `git diff --numstat` parsers, untracked line counts, the `changes` message. |
| `hive::snapshot` | Snapshots of a worktree at the start of each agent turn (27.2, `docs/spike/diff-bases.md`): a tree written through a snapshot index of Hive's into Hive's own object store per repository (`<data>/snapshots/<key>/store.git`, the repository's objects as its alternate), refs `refs/turns/<session>/<n>` and `turns.json`; the caps (10 turns a session, 7 days, 1 GiB a store), the prune, and the listing since a turn through the warm snapshot index. See [Changes](#changes) item 6. |
| `hive::transcript` | An agent's transcript (see [Transcripts](#transcripts)), only read: its path from a hook payload, the checks (inside Claude's projects folder, a regular file) and the tail read as it grows, for its tokens and interrupts (no conversation is shown since 9.35). Token usage (6.9): `Tokens` counts a conversation's usage records (also for the Sessions panel), `Usage` reads an agent's transcript as it grows and says when it ends on an interrupt. |
| `hive::dirs` | Browsing folders for the folder fields (`list_dirs`; `FolderField.tsx`: Add project, a new account's folder, a gh config folder in Settings → Accounts, 22.2, 24.13): a Linux path, or a Windows one converted with `wslpath -u`; the home folder (`$HOME`, or `%USERPROFILE%` through `cmd.exe`) for an empty one; subfolders sorted ignoring case, capped, hidden ones only when asked or when the last name starts with `.`; whether the path is a Claude config folder. On native Windows every path is a Windows one taken as it is (either separator), the home folder is `%USERPROFILE%`, and a name without a separator lists the drives. |
| `hive::sessions` | Claude Code's session logs of the followed projects for the Sessions panel (`<claude dir>/projects/<encoded cwd>/<id>.jsonl` whose `cwd` lies in a followed worktree): each log read once, then from where it stopped (9.12); the summary and the state from its ending; finding, locating and deleting one (its subagents' logs too); an agent's session name (`agent_title`); the sessions kept in `open-sessions.json` to resume when the app opens again (`restore_sessions`). Logs are only read, except when the user deletes one. |
| `hive::seen` | Files marked seen in a worktree's diff (27.1): each mark with the file's diff identity (blob ids of both sides), `seen` or `changed` on each listing, kept in `<data>/seen.json`. See [Seen files](#seen-files-271). |
| `hive::worktree_store` | Per-worktree data in one JSON file of Hive's folder (never inside `.git`): atomic, `0600`, size-capped, a worktree's data dropped with it or once its folder is gone. Used by `hive::seen` and `hive::comments`. |
| `hive::cow` | Copy-on-write copies of `.worktreeinclude` files (28.9): `copy_new` tries the `Copier`'s clone (`System`: `fclonefileat` on macOS, in `hive::macos`; `FICLONE` on Linux; none on Windows) and falls back to its plain copy on any error but `AlreadyExists`; from a source the caller opened (`hive::worktree` through `file::PlainFiles`: no link in any part of its path, the last file's folders kept open), never over an existing path, the source's mode kept (a plain copy's new file has it from its creation and is removed when the copy fails). |
| `hive::comments` | Review comments (27.3): kept per worktree in `<data>/comments.json`, moved with their code on every listing (`hive::anchor`), outdated when it is gone. See [Review comments](#review-comments-273). |
| `hive::search` | The Files panel's "Contents" search (`search_files`): `git grep` as a fixed string, ignoring case, over tracked and untracked files that are not ignored, binary ones skipped; capped per file, in all and per line. |
| `hive::macos` | Only on macOS: the kernel's process table (`libproc`) for `hive::procs`, each process named after its `argv[0]` (`KERN_PROCARGS2`; a native `claude` is a link to `versions/<x.y.z>`, which the kernel names it after) or `claude` for npm's `node …/cli.js` (`terminal::conpty::comm`), its arguments and open files, native file paths (where WSL asks `wslpath -w`) , the no-replace rename (`renamex_np(RENAME_EXCL)`) and `hive::cow`'s clone (`fclonefileat`). Tested by `macos.yml`; left out of Linux coverage. |
| `hive::windows` | Only on native Windows (12.5): every Windows body, which portable files only `pub use` (a Windows body elsewhere would be a Linux mutant nothing can catch). The paths (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\hive`, `%APPDATA%\hive`), the service's named pipe (`Listener`, `accept`, `Paths::connect` with the server-SID check), the detached daemon start (`spawn_detached`), the QPC clock, the process table (`sysinfo`) and the job of a program run with a time limit (`Job`), the archive script's shell, `gh` found on the user's `PATH` only, the `claude` wrapper and the statusline's shell, command and job (`windows::claude`, 12.5.5), the files (`rename_new` through `MoveFileExW`, `PlainFiles` and `open_parts` for `file::open_plain`, `clone_file` (none: `hive::cow` copies), `native_path`, `in_use`, `drives`, git's paths with `\`), and the terminals (12.5.3). Tested by `windows.yml` (100% lines, no surviving mutant); left out of Linux coverage and mutants. |
| `hive::file` | One file of a worktree for the viewer and diff: path checks, the text on disk and at the Changes panel's base (`git ls-tree`, `git cat-file`), the version token, the `file` message and an image's or a PDF's `media` (24.2), and the ranges `read_file` reads for the app's `hive-file:` scheme (`read_range`, under the size caps); saving it (temporary file + rename, version check), creating, renaming and moving files and creating folders (never over an entry), and its Windows path for an external editor. `file::answer` has the answer to each request on a worktree's files (search, open, save, create, rename, move, delete, editor target), after checking the worktree is followed. |
| `hive::table` | A CSV or TSV file of a worktree as a table (23.6): read on disk through `file::open_inside` (the viewer's path checks) with the `csv` crate, whatever the file's size; the first 10 000 rows (`TABLE_ROWS`) that fit in one frame, the total counted over at most 64 MiB (`COUNT_LIMIT`), a row over 1 MiB (`RECORD_LIMIT`) ending the reading as that limit does (the csv reader's memory grows with a row), the `table` message. A workbook (23.7, `hive::workbook`) answers it too. |
| `hive::table_edit` | A CSV or TSV text changed through its table (25.4): `edit_table` gets the text being edited and one edit and answers the new text and its table; nothing on disk. `scan` finds each cell's bytes by the `csv` reader's rules, and an edit is a list of splices on them, so every other byte stays (see [Editing](#editing-35-31), item 12). |
| `hive::workbook` | An `.xlsx`, `.xls` or `.ods` workbook as a table (23.7), with `calamine` (no default features): its worksheets in order (chart, dialog and macro sheets left out, hidden ones kept), each from A1 with its values as last computed (formulas never shown), the rows sharing one frame (`table::TABLE_ROWS` a sheet; a sheet whose record does not fit, and those after it, left out). Values: numbers to 15 significant digits, dates `YYYY-MM-DD`, times `HH:MM:SS`, both `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS` (`.mmm` when it has milliseconds), durations `H:MM:SS`, booleans `TRUE` / `FALSE`, errors as Excel writes them; an empty cell empty. `calamine` sizes memory by what the file claims (an `.xls` sheet's dimensions reserve rows × columns at once, sparse sheets are stored dense, zip members inflate uncapped), so the service never parses a workbook itself: at most 32 MiB of it (`WORKBOOK_LIMIT`, checked before reading) goes on stdin to its own binary run as `hive sheets <format> <budget>` (`Worker`, separate arguments), which answers JSON on stdout (at most 8 MiB read, `OUTPUT_LIMIT`), one reading at a time (`READING`). The child ends within 1 GiB (`MEMORY_LIMIT`: its own `RLIMIT_AS` on Linux, with core dumps off; on macOS the service polls its footprint, `macos::footprint`, every 20 ms and kills it past the limit) and 30 s (`TIME_LIMIT`, then killed); a crash, a kill or a bad answer is the error "the workbook could not be read", and the service goes on. A native Windows service (not one in WSL, which is Linux) can cap only the time, so it reads no workbook (23.14): `Worker::supported` is false there (`SUPPORTED`), and `workbook::read` answers "Spreadsheets are not supported on Windows yet" (`UNSUPPORTED`) without starting the child. |

## Wire protocol

Every message is a frame, big-endian: `[type: u8][channel: u32][length: u32][payload]`.

- **type** `0` = control: the payload is one JSON `Control` message, tagged by `"type"` in snake_case. **type** `1` = terminal: the payload is raw PTY bytes. **type** `2` = data (24.2), service → app only: the bytes a `read_file` asked for, on the request's `id` as channel, after its `file_range`; one from the app is refused as an unexpected message.
- **Contract (9.25).** The app's TypeScript mirror of the messages it receives is `ServiceMessage` in `src/protocol.ts`, reduced by `apply` in `src/reduce.ts`. `crates/hive-protocol/tests/app_messages.rs` writes one sample of every `Control` variant the app receives to `tests/app-messages.json` (checked in; the test rewrites it and fails when it was stale), and `src/protocol.test.ts` runs each through `apply` and checks that its type is known and its top-level fields are exactly the ones `ServiceMessage` gives it (plus what the Tauri pump adds: `channel`, and `app_*` and `bundled` on `version_mismatch`). A variant or field renamed, added or removed on one side fails the other's tests. The same test file sorts every variant into sent and received (an exhaustive match: a new variant does not compile until it is sorted), checks that every received one has a sample, and checks the [Message catalog](#message-catalog) below against the code (9.26): one row per `type`, none that no longer exists, each in the direction it is sorted into (a received one's ends at the app or a client).
- **channel** `0` is the connection itself. Channels from `1` up are terminals, and the channel number is also the terminal's `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID`.
- **Size limit.** The maximum payload is 4 MiB (`MAX_PAYLOAD`) in both directions. The decoder never panics: it fails with `Oversized`, `UnknownType` or `Json` errors. A message too big to encode never ends a connection: the app's Rust side refuses the command with the `Oversized` error (e.g. saving a huge paste), and the service's writer drops the frame with a warning in `daemon.log`.
- **Priority.** The service's writer always drains queued control frames before terminal frames. Terminal frames wait in one queue per terminal, and the writer takes the terminals in turn (channel order, wrapping), so a keystroke's echo waits for at most one frame of each other terminal.
- **Flow control (9.19).** The app acknowledges each terminal's output once xterm.js has written it (`ack {bytes}`). The service stops reading a terminal's PTY while more than 512 KiB of its output is unacknowledged (`terminal::HIGH_WATER`) and reads it again under 128 KiB (`LOW_WATER`), so a flooding program (`yes`, `cat` of a huge file) waits for the screen instead of growing memory in the service, Tauri or the webview, and each terminal's queue holds at most 512 KiB and one read (64 KiB). Tauri forwards terminal output without blocking; the acknowledgements are what bound it.

### Handshake

The first frame from every client is `Hello { protocol, version, role, keep_secs, app_id }`, where `role` is `app` or `hook`. `keep_secs` and `app_id` (26.15) are optional and left out when absent, so protocol 1 stays compatible; only a remote link sends them (see [App disconnect](#app-disconnect-or-sigterm)): `keep_secs` is how long the service keeps its terminals once this app connection ends without `bye {end: true}` (at most 7 days, `daemon::MAX_KEEP`), and `app_id` a random id per app launch (at most 128 bytes, else `Error` and the connection is closed).
- If both `protocol` (`PROTOCOL_VERSION`) and `version` (the `hive` binary version) match, the service answers `Welcome { version, distro }`. `distro` is the service's `WSL_DISTRO_NAME` (null when unset), shown in the app's status bar: the service knows its distribution, so Windows never has to guess it from `wsl.exe`. It was added as an optional field (`#[serde(default)]`), which old decoders ignore and new decoders default, so `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stayed 1.
- Otherwise it answers `VersionMismatch { protocol, version }` with its own values and closes the connection. This is a hard error: the app must block and tell the user.
- Any other first message gets `Error` and the connection is closed.
- **One app at a time.** An app connecting while another is attached gets `Error` ("another app is already connected") and is closed, unless both gave the same `app_id` and both gave `keep_secs` (26.15: a remote link's; one without `keep_secs` is the local app, never taken over): the new connection then takes over, and the old one, stale after a silent drop that sshd has not noticed yet, is closed.

### Message catalog

| Message | Direction | Channel | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `hello` | client → service | 0 | Starts the handshake. `keep_secs` and `app_id` (26.15, optional, a remote link's only): see [Handshake](#handshake). |
| `bye {end}` | app → service | 0 | The app is going (26.15); the service closes the connection. `end: true` ends every terminal at once, as #18; `end: false` keeps them for the connection's `keep_secs`, as a dropped link does (without `keep_secs` both end them). |
| `welcome {version, distro, warning}` | service → client | 0 | Handshake accepted. `warning` (22.1, optional): why the service still uses the pre-22.1 folders (`hive::legacy`), shown in the status bar; null otherwise. |
| `version_mismatch {protocol, version, terminals}` | service → client | 0 | Handshake refused (the service's own values); the connection is closed. `terminals` (26.17, optional): how many terminals the service runs, which restarting it would end; a remote link shows it (see [Remote hosts over ssh](#remote-hosts-over-ssh-2617)). |
| `open_terminal {cwd, cols, rows, account}` | app → service | n ≥ 1 | Start a terminal on channel n. `account` (optional, 12.2): `{config_dir}`, the Claude account it gets, one of the settings' accounts (`config_dir` null: the default one); absent, the account of its project's space (22.5; the default one outside every project). An unknown folder is refused with `error` ("No Claude account has the folder …"). |
| `terminal_opened {worktree, account}` | service → app | n | The terminal is running. `worktree` is the id of the followed worktree holding its cwd (`projects::place`: resolved, so a subfolder or a link counts; the deepest wins), null outside every followed project: the tab shows under that worktree, and under its project once a `projects` list no longer has it (`tabWorktree` in `src/tabs.ts`). `account` is the name of the Claude account it runs as (21.7, `settings::Store::account_name` of its `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; the default account's without one); with more than one account in use by the spaces (22.5) the tab's and its agent's tooltips and the terminal's menus show it (`accountLine` in `src/store.ts`). Missing (an older service): empty. |
| terminal frame | both | n | Keystrokes (app → service) or output (service → app). |
| `resize {cols, rows}` | app → service | n | Resize the PTY. |
| `close_terminal` | app → service | n | End the terminal's processes. |
| `ack {bytes}` | app → service | n | `bytes` more of terminal n's output were written to its screen (see Flow control above); ignored once the terminal is gone. |
| `terminal_exited {code}` | service → app | n | The shell exited; `code` is null when it was killed by a signal. The channel is free again. |
| `terminals_kept {terminals}` | service → app | 0 | 26.16: the first message after `welcome` when a dropped or replaced connection left terminals running (26.15); none otherwise. Each is `{channel, cwd, worktree, account, offset}` (`KeptTerminal`), in channel order: `cwd` what `open_terminal` got, `worktree` and `account` its `terminal_opened`'s, `offset` how many bytes of output it wrote so far (its ring's end). The service sends it nothing until the app's `attach_terminal`. See [Reattach](#reattach-after-a-dropped-link-2616). |
| `attach_terminal {from}` | app → service | n | 26.16: terminal n's output comes to this connection from now on, after what it wrote from offset `from` on (the bytes the app received of it; 0 for one the app does not know). Inside the ring (`from` from its start to its end): only those bytes. Outside it: `terminal_reset`, then the whole ring. Either way in frames of at most 64 KiB, counted as the first output of 9.19's window. A terminal no longer open answers `terminal_exited {code: null}` (its exit went to no app); one without a ring, `error`. |
| `terminal_reset {offset}` | service → app | n | 26.16: the bytes after the `from` of the app's `attach_terminal` are no longer all held, so the app clears that terminal's screen; the output that follows is the whole ring, starting at `offset`. |
| `hook {event, terminal_id, payload, sent_ns}` | `hive hook` → service | 0 | One raw hook call. `sent_ns` is `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` in ns when `hive hook` started (one clock for every process on the machine), so the service applies agent states in the order Claude sent them (see [Agent states](#agent-states)). The service closes the connection once it has handled it. The payload stays in the service: the app gets only what the service makes of it (`agent_detected`, `agent_state`, …). |
| `badge {text}` | `hive badge` → service → app | n | Terminal n's label (`hive badge`); empty clears it. The service drops control and invisible (zero-width, bidi) characters, trims, cuts it at 40 characters (the last one becomes "…") and forwards it only while terminal n is open; `hive badge` sends it on a hook-role connection, which closes after it. The app drops the label when the terminal exits. |
| `statusline_usage {claude_dir, usage, week}` | `hive statusline` → service | 0 | The 5-hour window (`rate_limits.five_hour` of Claude Code's statusline input) of the Claude config folder `claude_dir` (see [Session usage](#session-usage-121)); `usage` is `{used_percentage, resets_at}` (0–100, rounded; Unix epoch seconds). `week` is the 7-day window (`rate_limits.seven_day`, 15.3) in the same form. Each is null or absent when the input has none whole, at least one present (28.1; a report with neither is ignored). The service keeps the latest per folder, also in `<data>/hive/usage.json` (at most 64 folders of at most 4096 bytes; a percentage past 100 is 100; see [Session usage](#session-usage-121)). The connection closes after it. |
| `session_usage {usage, week, limit_at, week_limit_at}` | service → app | 0 | The current space's account's (22.5) 5-hour window (`{used_percentage, resets_at}`), null when none came yet or it is past `resets_at` (see [Session usage](#session-usage-121)); `week`, its 7-day window (15.3), null when none came or past its own `resets_at` (absent from an older service: the app takes it as null); either may come alone (28.1). `limit_at` / `week_limit_at` (17.23): when each window reaches 100% at its pace so far (Unix seconds, rounded down to the minute), null when it would not before its reset (absent from an older service: null). Sent when it changes (a report, its reset, its pace's minute, a space change or a space's account changed; checked every second while the terminals' tick runs, see [Session usage](#session-usage-121)), and right after `welcome` when there is one. |
| `usage_alert {window, threshold, resets_at}` | service → app | 0 | The current account's `window` (`session`: 5-hour, `week`: 7-day) reached `threshold` (80 or 95) percent; `resets_at` is that window's. Once per threshold and window (17.23, see [Session usage](#session-usage-121)). |
| `unhooked_agent` | service → app | n | A `claude` runs in terminal n without sending hook events. |
| `terminal_progress {state, value}` | service → app | n | The progress a program in terminal n reported with OSC 9;4 (17.25): `state` `none`, `normal`, `error`, `indeterminate` or `paused`, `value` 0–100; sent when it changes, in order with the output. See [Shell marks](#shell-marks-and-terminal-alerts-1725). |
| `command_finished {exit, secs}` | service → app | n | A command terminal n's shell marked (OSC 133 `C` … `D;<exit>`) ran `secs` ≥ `notifications.command_secs` and ended while terminal n has no agent and is not in view of the focused window: an inbox item and an OS notification. `exit` null when the shell gave none. |
| `terminal_notification {title, body, notify}` | service → app | n | A program in terminal n (no agent there) asked for a notification, OSC 9 or OSC 777 `notify`, its text cleaned and cut: an inbox item, and an OS notification when `notify` (not in view of the focused window). |
| `agent_detected {id, project, worktree, cwd}` | service → app | n | An agent (`id` = its session id) started in terminal n. `project`/`worktree` are the ids of the followed worktree containing `cwd`, both null outside every followed project. Sent again for every live agent, before its `agent_state`, right after `welcome` and in answer to `resync`. |
| `agent_state {id, state, urgency, pending, interrupted, alert, notify, subagents, activity, since_ms, background}` | service → app | n | The agent's displayed state, its urgency (0 ended … 8 waiting for permission; higher wins), whether it is pending (needs the user), whether it waits for you because the user interrupted it (nothing alerts), its `alert` (`finished`, `action`, `waiting` or null) and whether that alert shows an OS notification (`notify`; see [Notifications](#notifications-hivemd-item-5)), and its live subagents `[{id, agent_type, teammate_name, state, worktree, activity, since_ms}]`, `teammate_name` being an agent team teammate's name once a hook said it, else null, and `worktree` the id of the subagent's own worktree or null; `activity` is what the agent (or subagent) itself is doing, or null, and `since_ms` the wall clock time (ms since the epoch) its displayed state (a subagent's: its state) began (see [Agent states](#agent-states)). `background` (omitted when empty) lists its background commands, running ones oldest first and those that ended in the last 10 s: `[{id, kind, command, description, by, since_ms, ended?}]`, `kind` `shell` or `monitor`, `command` cut at 1000 characters (empty when not known), `description` at 200 or null, `by` the subagent that started it or null, `ended` `{status, exit, ms}` once it ended (see [Background commands](#background-commands-288)). Sent when it changes, after `agent_detected`, and for every live agent right after `welcome`. |
| `background_finished {agent, id, kind, command, description, by, status, exit, secs, alert, notify}` | service → app | n | One background command (`id`, Claude Code's task id) of agent `agent` ended, sent once: `status` `completed`, `failed`, `stopped` or `unknown` (it left `background_tasks` with no notice), `exit` the exit code the notice named or null, `secs` how long it ran as the service saw it. `alert` `failed` (status `failed`, or `exit` not 0) or `quiet`; `notify` true for a failure while the agent is not `watched`. See [Background commands](#background-commands-288). |
| `subagent_worktrees {worktrees}` | service → app | 0 | The worktrees a live subagent works in as its own (see [Agent states](#agent-states) item 7) where no agent runs and no terminal of the human's opened, sorted: the sidebar shows none of them, only the path as its subagent line's tooltip. Sent when the set changes, and right after `welcome` when it is not empty. |
| `agent_title {id, title}` | service → app | n | The agent's session name from its log (the user's, else Claude's, else its first prompt, as the Sessions panel shows it). Sent when first known and whenever it changes, and for every named agent right after `welcome`. |
| `agent_usage {id, context_tokens, context_limit, output_tokens}` | service → app | n | The agent's tokens from its transcript (see [Tokens and context](#tokens-and-context-69)): the last turn's context (input + cache writes + cache reads), the model's context window (see step 3 there) and the session's output so far. Sent when they change and for every agent with a usage right after `welcome`. |
| `agent_removed {id}` | service → app | n | The agent's session ended, or terminal n exited (sent before `terminal_exited`). |
| `list_agents` | `hive agents` → service | 0 | 28.10: every live agent, read only, on a hook-role connection (the one message such a connection gets an answer to); answered by one `agent_listed` per agent, then `agents_listed`. The service copies the agents first (the agents lock is never held while writing) and gives the client 5 s to read them (`LIST_TIME`). See [CLI output](#cli-output-2810). |
| `agent_listed {agent}` | service → `hive agents` | 0 | One live agent (`ListedAgent`), in terminal order (then session id): `{id, terminal, project, worktree, cwd, title, state, pending, since_ms, activity, subagents, usage}`, what `agent_detected`, `agent_state`, `agent_title` and `agent_usage` tell the app (`usage`: `{context_tokens, context_limit, output_tokens}`, null until read). |
| `agents_listed` | service → `hive agents` | 0 | The list is complete; the connection closes. |
| `list_projects` | app → service | 0 | Asks for every project; answered by `spaces`, then `projects`. |
| `projects {projects}` | service → app | 0 | Every project with its worktrees, in the order they were added, each group followed by its repositories (see [Groups](#groups-141); a project carries `group` and `parent`, optional for an older service). Also sent unasked after a `WorktreeCreate` or `WorktreeRemove` hook, or a change of git's worktree registry (9.36) or of a group's folder, when the projects or their worktrees differ from the ones last sent. |
| `add_project {path}` | app → service | 0 | Follow the git repository containing `path` in the current space; a folder outside git holding repositories becomes a group (14.1), one holding none a folder project (23.1, see [Folder projects](#folder-projects-231)). |
| `project_added {project}` | service → app | 0 | The project, with its worktrees, after `spaces` (and, for a group, after `projects`, which brings its repositories). Also the answer when it was already followed in the current space (a group's repository too). |
| `add_project_failed {path, error, message}` | service → app | 0 | `path` was refused. `error` is `empty_path`, `not_absolute`, `not_found`, `not_a_directory`, `not_a_git_repository`, `protected_folder` (23.1: the home folder itself, `/`, Hive's or Claude's folder or one in it, or a folder holding one of these, as a group or a folder project), `in_other_space`, `storage`, or for `create_project` (23.2) `invalid_name`, `name_taken` or `create_failed`; `message` is shown as is. |
| `create_project {parent, name, git}` | app → service | 0 | A new project (23.2, see [New projects](#new-projects-232)): the folder `name` (`create_dir`'s rules) made in `parent` (a Linux path, `dirs`' `linux_path`), `git init -q` in it when `git`, then followed as `add_project` follows it. Answered as `add_project` is, with `project_created` in place of `project_added`; refused with `add_project_failed` (its `path` is `parent` joined with `name`). |
| `project_created {project, path}` | service → app | 0 | The folder `path` was made and `project` (the project that holds it: the new one, or a repository it is in when made without git) follows it, after `spaces` (and `projects` for a folder project). The add-project dialog opens a terminal in `path`. |
| `init_git {id}` | app → service | 0 | `git init` in the folder project `id` (23.1). Nothing answers it: the watch of its folder sees the `.git` made and sends `projects`, with `id` a project. Refused with `error` ("<id> is not a folder project") for any other id, or one whose `.git` is made already; git's own failure is an `error` too. |
| `remove_project {id}` | app → service | 0 | Stop following the project `id` (9.28), refused while a process of one of Hive's terminals works in it or one of its worktrees ("in use by …: close its terminals first"). It leaves its space in `spaces.json`; its settings (`projects.<id>` in `settings.json`) and its worktrees' port blocks (`ports.json`) go too. Nothing on disk is deleted: the repository, its worktrees and Claude's sessions stay. Answered by `spaces`, then `settings` when it had settings (`settings_failed` when they could not be saved), then `project_removed`; or by `remove_project_failed`. A group goes with its repositories, each answered as a project before the group's own; a group's repository alone is refused ("<id> is in the group <group>: remove the group to stop following it"). |
| `project_removed {id}` | service → app | 0 | The project is no longer followed; the app drops its rows, file tabs, panel state and its places in the agents' and tab bar's orders. |
| `remove_project_failed {id, message}` | service → app | 0 | Nothing changed (not a followed project, in use, or `spaces.json` could not be saved); `message` is shown as is. |
| `spaces {spaces[{id, name, projects, groups, env}], current}` | service → app | 0 | Every space (see [Spaces](#spaces-614)) and the current one's id; `groups`, sent only when not empty, are those of `projects` that are groups (14.1). Sent before `projects` in answer to `list_projects`, before `project_added`, and in answer to every space request. |
| `create_space {name, env}` | app → service | 0 | A new, empty space, made current. `env` is `{git_name, git_email, gh_account, claude_account}`, each null or a string, except `gh_account`: null or `{host, login, config_dir}`, `config_dir` its gh config folder (24.13; null or missing: `gh`'s own config). `claude_account` (22.5) is the folder of one of the settings' Claude accounts (null: the default one); another folder is refused ("No Claude account has the folder …"); so is a `gh_account` whose `config_dir` is not one of the settings' `gh.config_dirs` (24.13: "<folder> is not a gh config folder of Settings → Accounts: add it there first"; a blank one is `gh`'s own config). Answered by `spaces` or `space_failed`. |
| `update_space {id, name, env}` | app → service | 0 | Renames a space and replaces its environment. Answered by `spaces` or `space_failed`. |
| `delete_space {id}` | app → service | 0 | Removes a space without projects, never the last one. Answered by `spaces` or `space_failed`. |
| `select_space {id}` | app → service | 0 | Makes `id` the current space. Answered by `spaces` or `space_failed`. |
| `space_failed {message}` | service → app | 0 | A space request refused, nothing changed; shown as is in the space dialog. |
| `list_claude_logins` | app → service | 0 | Whether each Claude account has logged in (21.6, see [Claude accounts](#claude-accounts-122), item 6). Answered by `claude_logins`. The app asks a remote host shown for its own (27.11). |
| `claude_logins {logins[{config_dir, name, logged_in}], claude}` | service → app | 0 | The default account first (`config_dir` null), then the settings' accounts in order, each with its name in the service's settings (27.11); `logged_in` is whether its Claude Code config file has a top-level `oauthAccount` (never the credentials, #45); `claude` whether a `claude` is found on the user's `PATH` (see [The user's `claude`](#the-users-claude)). A remote's reaches the UI as that host's own: the status bar of its spaces shows its account and usage ring from it, never this Mac's; without a `claude` there "No Claude on <host>", with only its default account never logged in "No Claude account on <host>", neither with a ring. |
| `list_gh_accounts {gh_config_dir}` | app → service | 0 | The accounts logged in to `gh`, in `gh_config_dir` when not null (see [Spaces](#spaces-614), item 6). Answered by `gh_accounts`. |
| `switch_gh_account {gh_config_dir, account {host, login}}` | app → service | 0 | Makes `account` `gh`'s own active account (`gh auth switch`); the app asks the human first. Answered by `gh_accounts`, listed again. |
| `gh_accounts {gh_config_dir, accounts[{host, login, active, logged_in}], problem}` | service → app | 0 | The accounts, logins only (never a token), for the `gh_config_dir` asked; `problem` (or null) says why the list is empty or a switch failed: `gh` not installed, no account logged in, an invalid config folder, `gh`'s own error. |
| `get_gh_identity {gh_config_dir, account {host, login}}` | app → service | 0 | The git identity of `account` (21.5): `gh api user` run with its token, as `Gh::run` does (see [Spaces](#spaces-614), item 6). Answered by `gh_identity` or `gh_identity_failed`. |
| `gh_identity {account, name, email}` | service → app | 0 | `name` is the login GitHub answers, `email` its noreply address `<id>+<login>@users.noreply.<host>` (the host without a port); the space dialog fills its empty git name and email with them. |
| `gh_identity_failed {account, message}` | service → app | 0 | Why the identity is not known (no `gh`, no token, an invalid config folder, GitHub's error, an answer without an id and a valid login), naming the login; shown as is in the space dialog. |
| `list_pulls {project, force}` | app → service | 0 | The pull requests of a followed project (see [Pull requests](#pull-requests-931)). Without `force`, a list fetched less than 2 minutes ago (for the project and its space's account) is sent again without asking GitHub; a fetch already running answers instead; while GitHub's rate limit holds, the last list is sent even when forced. Answered by `pulls`. |
| `pulls {project, repo, mine, review, fetched_ms, error}` | service → app | 0 | `repo` is `{name, url, merge_methods, default_merge}` (`merge_methods` among `merge`, `squash`, `rebase`), null when `error`. `mine` (the account's own, any state) and `review` (open ones asking for its review) are at most 30 pull requests each, the most recently updated first: `{number, title, url, state, branch, base, author, review, checks, updated_at, worktree}`, `state` one of `open`, `draft`, `merged`, `closed`; `review` `approved`, `changes_requested`, `review_required` or null; `checks` (the last commit's checks together) `passing`, `failing`, `running`, `skipped` or null; `updated_at` ISO 8601 as GitHub gives it; `worktree` the id of the project's worktree on its branch (same repository only) or null. `fetched_ms` is when GitHub was asked (0: it was not). `error` says why nothing is listed: not a followed project, no remote on the host, `gh`'s message (e.g. not installed), or GitHub's rate limit with the minutes until Hive asks again. |
| `open_pull {project, number}` | app → service | 0 | One pull request's details (`gh pr view`). Answered by `pull`. |
| `pull {project, number, pull, error}` | service → app | 0 | `pull` is `{summary, body, head, additions, deletions, conflicts, notes, checks, files}` or null with `error`: `summary` as in `pulls`, `body` its Markdown (untrusted, cut at 64 KiB), `head` its head commit, `notes` its reviews with a verdict or a text and its comments not hidden, oldest first, the newest 50 (`{author, body, at, review}`, `review` `approved`, `changes_requested`, `commented`, `dismissed` or null for a comment; `body` cut at 16 KiB), `checks` at most 100 `{name, workflow, state, url, run}` (`run` the Actions run id when `url` is a job's page of this very repository, `https://<host>/<owner>/<name>/actions/runs/<run>/job/<job>`, else null, since a check may point anywhere: the app shows it in the Actions view), `files` at most 300 `{path, additions, deletions}`. Links are `https://` only (else empty or null). |
| `act_on_pull {project, number, action}` | app → service | 0 | `action` is `{kind: "ready"}`, `{kind: "close"}`, `{kind: "merge", method, head}` (merged only while `head`, 40 or 64 hex digits, is still its head: `--match-head-commit`) or `{kind: "checkout"}`; the app asks before merging or closing. Answered by `pull_done`, then `pull` and `pulls` again; a checkout by `worktree_created` (the new worktree `.claude/worktrees/pr-<number>`, on the pull request's branch, `pr-<number>-<branch>` for a fork's: see `hive::checkout`) then `pulls`; or by `pull_failed`. |
| `create_pull {worktree, title, body, base, draft, push}` | app → service | 0 | `gh pr create` from the branch of `worktree` (a worktree of a followed project) into `base`: title 1 to 256 characters, body at most 64 KiB, base not starting with `-`. With `push` (27.6; absent or null: no push), the `{remote, merge}` the user confirmed (`push_target`'s `at`), the branch is first pushed as `push` does (refused when its remote or ref changed, or when `merge` is not `refs/heads/<branch>`: the pull request's head would not be it), and a push git refuses is the `pull_failed` ("Could not push <branch>: <why>"). Answered by `pull_done` (then `pulls`) or `pull_failed` with no number. |
| `pull_ended {project, worktree, number, title, state}` | service → app | 0 | 17.27: a `pulls` list from GitHub shows the pull request `number` of the project's worktree `worktree` (its id) `merged` or `closed`; sent before that `pulls`. One per worktree (its most recently updated one), never for the main worktree nor one with another open pull request on its branch; once per pull request, across restarts (`<data>/hive/offered-pulls.json`, mode `0600`, the latest 1000 that fit its 256 KiB read limit; `hive::offers`), and again only after it was reopened. The app keeps it as an inbox item offering to remove the worktree; nothing is removed unless the user goes through the remove dialog. |
| `pull_done {project, number, message}` | service → app | 0 | The action worked; `message` shows as a fading toast (e.g. "Merged pull request #12"; `number` 0 when `gh` printed no link after creating one). |
| `pull_failed {project, number, message}` | service → app | 0 | Nothing changed; `message` is `gh`'s (cut at 4 KiB) or why the request was refused, shown in the view (`number` null: in the "Create pull request" dialog; `project` empty when the worktree is not followed). |
| `list_runs {project, branch, force}` | app → service | 0 | The latest GitHub Actions runs of a followed project (see [Actions](#actions-932)), on `branch` only when not null. Fetched from GitHub as `list_pulls` is (at most once per 2 minutes per project, branch and account unless forced; a fetch running answers instead; GitHub's rate limit holds it even when forced). Answered by `runs`. |
| `runs {project, branch, runs, fetched_ms, error}` | service → app | 0 | `branch` echoes the request. `runs` are at most 30, the newest first: `{id, number, workflow, title, branch, event, state, status, url, created_at, started_at, updated_at, worktree}`: `id` GitHub's run id; `state` as a check's (`passing`, `failing` for `failure`/`timed_out`/`startup_failure`, `running` until completed, `skipped` for any other conclusion such as `cancelled`); `status` GitHub's conclusion once completed, else its status (lowercase letters and `_` only, else empty); `event` likewise; times ISO 8601 as GitHub gives them; `worktree` the id of the project's worktree on its branch, or null. `fetched_ms` and `error` as in `pulls`. |
| `open_run {project, run}` | app → service | 0 | One run's jobs (`gh run view`). Answered by `run`. |
| `run {project, run, detail, error}` | service → app | 0 | `detail` is `{summary, jobs}` or null with `error`: `summary` as in `runs`; `jobs` at most 256 `{id, name, state, status, url, started_at, completed_at, steps}` (`completed_at` is GitHub's zero time, year 1, until it ends), each with at most 100 `steps` `{number, name, state, status}`. |
| `open_job_log {project, job}` | app → service | 0 | The end of job `job`'s log. Answered by `job_log`. |
| `job_log {project, job, log, error}` | service → app | 0 | `log` (or null with `error`, e.g. a log GitHub no longer keeps) is the end of the job's log as text: the last 1 MiB read, each line without GitHub's timestamp, ANSI escapes (CSI, OSC and two-character ones) and other control characters but tabs, a carriage return keeping only the text after it, the runner's `##[group]` prefix and its `##[endgroup]`/`##[start-action`/`##[end-action` lines dropped; cut after the last `##[error]` line when there is one (the failed step's end, not the cleanup after it); then its last whole lines within 64 KiB. |
| `act_on_run {project, run, action, branch}` | app → service | 0 | `action` is `{kind: "rerun", failed}` (`gh run rerun`, with `--failed` when `failed`) or `{kind: "cancel"}` (the app asks first). Answered by `run_done`, then `run` and `runs` for `branch` (the view's filter), forced; or by `run_failed`. |
| `run_done {project, run, message}` | service → app | 0 | The action was accepted; `message` shows as a fading toast ("Re-running the failed jobs", "Re-running every job", "Cancelling the run"). |
| `run_failed {project, run, message}` | service → app | 0 | `gh`'s message (cut at 4 KiB) or why the request was refused, shown in the run's view. |
| `list_workflows {project, worktree}` | app → service | 0 | The workflows of `project` that can be run by hand (27.6): the `.github/workflows/*.yml`/`*.yaml` files of `worktree`'s `HEAD` (a worktree of that project; at most 100 files of 256 KiB, untrusted YAML) whose `on` holds `workflow_dispatch`. Answered by `workflows`. |
| `workflows {project, worktree, workflows, error}` | service → app | 0 | `workflows` = `[{file, name, inputs}]`, sorted by name: `file` its name in `.github/workflows` (`[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, never starting with `-` or `.`), `name` its `name` or its file; `inputs` = `[{name, description, required, default, kind, options}]` (at most 50; `kind` `string`, `boolean`, `choice`, `number` or `environment`; a `choice`'s `options`, at most 100). None before the first commit. `error`: why none could be read. |
| `run_workflow {project, workflow, branch, inputs}` | app → service | 0 | `gh workflow run <workflow> --repo <repo> --ref=<branch>` with one `--raw-field <name>=<value>` per input (`inputs` = `[{name, value}]`, at most 50, names as GitHub allows them, values at most 64 KiB in all and without NUL), as the project's space account. The branch is checked as git checks one. The workflow's runs on that branch from `workflow_dispatch` are listed before and up to five times after (2 s apart): the first one new is its run. Answered by `workflow_started`, then `run` (when found) and `runs` for `branch`, forced; or by `workflow_failed`. |
| `workflow_started {project, branch, run, message}` | service → app | 0 | The workflow started on `branch`; `run` its new run once GitHub listed it (null: not yet, or the runs could not be listed). |
| `workflow_failed {project, message}` | service → app | 0 | `gh`'s message (cut at 4 KiB) or why the request was refused, shown in the dialog. |
| `notice {message}` | service → app | 0 | Something to show as is, as an error toast that stays until dismissed, e.g. a terminal that could not get its space's GitHub account. |
| `list_dirs {path, windows, hidden}` | app → service | 0 | The subfolders of the folder typed in a folder field (`hive::dirs`): `path` is Linux, or Windows (`C:\...`) when `windows`; empty is the home folder (`$HOME`, or `cmd.exe /c echo %USERPROFILE%`). Without a trailing separator, the folder holding the last name is listed. Hidden folders too when `hidden` (default false; the account and `gh` fields set it) or when that last name starts with `.`, as a shell completes. Answered by `dirs`. |
| `dirs {path, windows, linux_path, parent, dirs[{name, git}], error, warning, claude}` | service → app | 0 | `path` echoes the request (the home folder, ending with a separator, for an empty one); `linux_path` is `path` for `add_project`, converted with `wslpath -u` when `windows` by a service in WSL (`null` with the drives a native Windows service lists for a name without a separator); `parent` is the folder above the listed one in the same form (`null` at the top); `dirs` are its subfolders (hidden ones only when asked, see `list_dirs`), links to folders included, sorted ignoring case, at most 1000; `git` when one holds a `.git` entry. `warning` (macOS only, D18.9, `macos::remote`) says why a project in the listed folder (links and `..` resolved, so the folder that holds a name still being typed) would be followed badly: on a network volume (`statfs` without `MNT_LOCAL`, or `smbfs`/`nfs`/`webdav`) or in cloud storage (`~/Library/Mobile Documents`, `~/Library/CloudStorage`), FSEvents misses changes made elsewhere and git can hit its time limit; the dialog shows it under the field and the folder can still be added. `claude` (default false) when `linux_path` holds a `.claude.json` or `settings.json` file: a Claude config folder, said under the new account's field. |
| `create_dir {parent, name}` | app → service | 0 | The folder browser's "New folder" (23.2): the folder `name` made in `parent` (a Linux path, `dirs`' `linux_path`), mode 0755 less the umask, never its parents (`parent` must exist) and never over an existing entry. `name` is one path segment: not empty, at most 255 bytes, no `/`, `\` or other character Windows refuses (`: * ? " < > |`, so `D:x` is never a path on drive D:), no leading `.`, no control character. Answered by `dir_created` or `create_dir_failed`. |
| `dir_created {parent, name}` | service → app | 0 | The folder was made; the field moves into it. |
| `create_dir_failed {parent, name, message}` | service → app | 0 | Why not (the name's rule, a missing parent, "<path> already exists", the system's error), shown under the field. |
| `list_branches {project}` | app → service | 0 | The local and remote branches of a followed project; answered by `branches`. |
| `branches {project, local, remote, current, error}` | service → app | 0 | Short names from `git for-each-ref` (remote `HEAD` symrefs skipped, at most 1 MiB of names). `current` is the branch checked out in the main worktree, shown as "default"; `error` says why they could not be listed. |
| `validate_worktree_name {project, name}` | app → service | 0 | Sent as the user types a new worktree's name. |
| `worktree_name_validated {project, name, folder, branch, error}` | service → app | 0 | The CLI's verdict (`error`, or null) and where the worktree would go (`.claude/worktrees/<name>/`, `worktree-<name>`; `<name>` when empty). Answers may arrive out of order, so the app matches them by name. |
| `create_worktree {project, name, base}` | app → service | 0 | `hive worktree create` in a followed project, from `base` (null: the main worktree's HEAD). |
| `worktree_created {project, path, notes}` | service → app | 0 | The project with its updated worktrees, the new path, and what the CLI prints on stderr (a competing `WorktreeCreate` hook, the files copied from `.worktreeinclude`). |
| `create_worktree_failed {project, name, message}` | service → app | 0 | The CLI's error, shown as is; `name` is `#<number>` for `create_issue_worktree`. |
| `create_issue_worktree {project, number, base}` | app → service | 0 | A worktree for issue `number` of the project's GitHub repository (17.24, `hive::checkout`): its title through `gh issue view` as the space's account, the branch and folder `<number>-<title words>` (`issue-<number>` without words) from `base` (fetched first when remote; null: the main worktree's HEAD), or that branch as it is when it exists. Answered by `worktree_created` or `create_worktree_failed`. |
| `remove_worktree {path, force}` | app → service | 0 | `git worktree remove` of a linked worktree of a followed project (`--force` when `force`), after its archive script (see [Project scripts and ports](#project-scripts-and-ports-68)). Without `force`, one with changes or a process working in it is kept. The branch stays. Answered by `worktree_removed` or `remove_worktree_failed`. |
| `worktree_removed {project, path}` | service → app | 0 | The project with its updated worktrees; `path` is gone. |
| `remove_worktree_failed {path, message}` | service → app | 0 | Nothing removed; `message` is shown as is. |
| `rename_worktree {path, name}` | app → service | 0 | Rename a Claude worktree of a followed project (the rules of a new worktree's name): its folder moves to `.claude/worktrees/<name>` and its branch `worktree-<old>`, while it is still on it, becomes `worktree-<name>` (the folder moves back when that fails). Refused while a process works in it. Answered by `worktree_renamed` or `rename_worktree_failed`. |
| `worktree_renamed {project, from, path}` | service → app | 0 | The project with its updated worktrees; the worktree at `from` is now at `path` (also its new id). |
| `rename_worktree_failed {path, name, message}` | service → app | 0 | Nothing renamed; `message` is shown as is. |
| `create_review {path}` | app → service | 0 | "Review in a new worktree…" (27.4, [Review worktrees](#review-worktrees-274)) of a worktree of a followed project, on a branch: the Claude worktree `review-<name>` on a new branch of that name from the tip of its branch, linked to it as its review. Answered by `worktree_created` (the review's `review_of` set) or `create_worktree_failed` (`name` is `path`; `project` empty when `path` is no followed worktree). |
| `plan_bring_back {path}` | app → service | 0 | What "Bring changes back" of the review `path` would do; nothing changes. Answered by `bring_back_plan`. |
| `bring_back_plan {path, plan, error}` | service → app | 0 | `plan` (`{reviewed, into, base, branch, tip, commits: [{id, summary}], total, fast_forward}`: the reviewed worktree, its branch and that branch's commit, the review's branch and its tip, its commits missing from `into`, newest first, at most 100 listed, `total` counting them all, and whether `into` only moves forward), or `error` (not a review, a side detached). |
| `bring_back {path, into, base, tip}` | app → service | 0 | Sent only on the user's click (#12): brings the review's commits into the reviewed branch, in the reviewed worktree. Refused while either side has changes to tracked files, or once the plan the user saw no longer holds (the reviewed worktree on `into` at `base`, the review's branch at `tip`). A fast-forward (`git merge --ff-only <tip>`) when `fast_forward`, else a merge commit (`git merge --no-ff --no-edit -m "Merge review <branch> into <into>" <tip>`) with the space's git identity (21.5); never a rebase. A merge that stops is undone and its message (stdout and stderr, the last 64 KiB) answered: a conflict with `git merge --abort`; a refusal of git's own changes nothing, so it is left alone (what was staged meanwhile stays); one Hive stopped after 120 s gets `--abort`, or `git reset --merge` before git wrote `MERGE_HEAD`, and counts as done when its commit was made. The reads (`status`, `rev-parse`, `rev-list`, `log`) have git's 10 s limit. Answered by `brought_back` or `bring_back_failed`. |
| `brought_back {project, path, commits, fast_forward}` | service → app | 0 | The project with its updated worktrees (and statuses), the commits brought and whether it was a fast-forward. |
| `bring_back_failed {path, message}` | service → app | 0 | Nothing changed; `message` is shown as is (git's own for a conflict). |
| `discard_review {path, force}` | app → service | 0 | "Discard review": `remove_worktree` of the review `path` (the same checks and archive script; `force` as there), then its `review-…` branch is deleted when every commit of it is on the reviewed branch (`git update-ref -d` while it is still at that commit), else kept.
