4. Outside Tauri (browser, mock transport) only the title bar Close requests a close, and a close that goes through sets `data-closed` on `<html>` for the browser checks.
5. Quitting on macOS (17.7, D17.3): ⌘Q (the app menu's Quit), the Dock's "Quit" and logging out all reach `[NSApp terminate:]`, which quits at once unless the app delegate answers `applicationShouldTerminate:`. tao's delegate has no such method and Tauri sends no `RunEvent::ExitRequested` for them (it sends one only when the last window is gone or for `exit`/`restart`, which never need asking), so `src-tauri/src/macos.rs` adds the method to tao's delegate class (`ask_before_terminating`, through the Objective-C runtime, no crate; a panic or a missing answer quits, a missing class leaves macOS quitting at once). It answers `should_quit` (an app without its `Hive` quits) → `Hive::should_quit`: the app side follows the `agent_state` / `agent_removed` messages and the `confirm_close` setting it relays (`Link::follow`, the at-risk states as the UI's `agentsAtRisk`; cleared on disconnect), and `quit_decision` asks only when a UI is connected, the setting is on and an agent is at risk. Then the quit is cancelled (a logout stops, as for any app that asks first), the window comes up (`bring_up`) and the UI gets `quit_requested`; a UI the message cannot reach is forgotten and the app quits, and a second request within 5 s of the question (`ASK_AGAIN`) quits, so a hung UI never keeps the app; `requestQuit` asks through `confirmClose` and the same dialog, and closes the window (which quits) when it has nothing to ask. `Hive::request_quit` is the same from inside the app (17.13's menu Quit): it quits through `with_exit` (`AppHandle::exit`) when nothing needs asking. The red button keeps quitting through item 1. A click on the Dock icon with the window minimized or hidden (`RunEvent::Reopen` without visible windows) brings it back (`reopen`, called from `main.rs`: Tauri's `Reopen` cannot be built in a test). In the mock transport `quit-app` typed in a terminal sends `quit_requested`.

### The window and its title bar (17.15)
1. The title bar (`src/shell/TitleBar.tsx`) is Tauri's drag region wherever it is empty: the bar itself (`data-tauri-drag-region`) and anything inside the brand (`="deep"`); its buttons are not. On Windows Tauri's double click maximizes. On macOS the native traffic lights sit centred on the bar's middle line, 16.5 px down (the 33 px above its bottom border, where the bar's items sit), 10 px from the left: tao and wry make the title bar view `close.height + y` tall and keep each button where AppKit put it above that view's bottom, so `hive_lib::macos::traffic_lights` reads the close button's frame from a never-shown window with Hive's style at launch and sets `trafficLightPosition` from it (`traffic_light_y`, the top on a half point; 18.5 on macOS 26, a 14 px button 9 px above the bottom; `tauri.macos.conf.json`'s `{x: 10, y: 18.5}` stays when AppKit gives no button). tao and wry reapply it on each redraw and after leaving full screen, with 70 px of room on the left except in full screen (`watchFullscreen`: Tauri's `isFullscreen` on every resize; the page's full screen outside Tauri), where they are hidden. A double click on the empty bar follows the system's "Double-click a window's title bar to" setting: the bar's own mouse-up listener stops Tauri's (which always zooms) and asks the app command `title_bar_action`, which reads `AppleActionOnDoubleClick` with `/usr/bin/defaults read -g` on every click (2 s limit) and answers `zoom` ("Maximize", "Fill", unset or unknown), `minimize` or `none`; the UI then toggles the maximize or minimizes.
2. The window follows Hive's theme (`nativeTheme` in `src/window.ts`, from `App`'s theme effect): `setTheme` light or dark for native parts (none for System, which leaves them, and `prefers-color-scheme`, to the system), and the theme's `--bg` as the window's and the webview's background (`THEME_BACKGROUND`). Both window configs start on One Dark's `#282c33` (`backgroundColor`), so the launch shows no white.
3. Only the app's top document reaches Tauri's IPC (24.14, macOS): WebKit gives every frame the `ipc` message handler, a sandboxed one too, and Tauri counts a registered scheme as the app, leaving the invoke key as the only barrier. `macos::guard_ipc` (Objective-C runtime, from `main.rs`'s `with_webview`) replaces wry's `userContentController:didReceiveScriptMessage:` (its class found by the name part `WryWebViewDelegate`) to drop a message unless it comes from the main frame at an `ipc_from_app` URL (`app_url`'s origins), and the `ipc` scheme handler's `webView:startURLSchemeTask:` to fail (403) a fetch whose `Origin` is not one (`null` for a sandboxed frame, `hive-file:`). Without wry's class it removes the `ipc` message handler, keeping `ipc://`. Tauri's `tracing` feature stays off (0.26.1): it makes `eval_script` wait for the main thread, so a `Channel::send` under the `link` lock deadlocked with a command on the main thread waiting for that lock. Tauri's refusal of a wrong invoke key, which names the right one, is therefore a line on stderr (`app.log`); on macOS the frame gate drops a framed page's call before Tauri sees it. Windows (WebView2) has no frame gate (D17.0).

### Window and single instance (17.18)
1. `tauri-plugin-window-state` saves the window's size, position, maximised and full screen state (`WINDOW_STATE` in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`; not its decorations nor whether it is visible) to `.window-state.json` in the app config folder on a close request and on exit, and restores it when the window is created. The window's `minWidth`/`minHeight` (800×500 in `tauri.conf.json`, 640×500 in `tauri.macos.conf.json` so half-screen tiling fits a 13–14" MacBook, D18.10 a) bound what a tampered file can shrink it to. A position on a display that is gone is dropped (the OS places the window).
2. `tauri-plugin-single-instance`, the first plugin registered: a second launch hands its arguments to the running app and exits before anything else starts; the running app ignores them and brings its window up (`bring_up`: unminimised, shown, focused, as a notification click does). macOS: a Unix socket `/tmp/<identifier>_si.sock` (`.` and `-` as `_`); Linux: a D-Bus name; Windows: a named mutex.
3. Both are keyed on the app's `identifier`. A development build runs as `<identifier>.dev` (`instance_identifier`, set on the context in `main.rs`), so `scripts/mac-dev.sh` neither focuses the installed app nor is focused by it, and keeps its own window state. `scripts/second-launch.sh` (macOS CI) checks that a second launch of a development build ends at once and leaves the first running.
4. On macOS, opening the running app from the Dock, Finder or Spotlight starts no second process: the system activates the running one and Tauri emits `RunEvent::Reopen`, which TODO 17.7 handles (a minimised window comes back only through it).

### The app menu (17.13)
1. On macOS the app has its own menu bar (`src-tauri/src/menu.rs`: `MENUS`, turned into Tauri's menu item for item by `app_menu` in `main.rs`): **Hive** (About, Settings… ⌘,, Check for Updates…, Uninstall Hive… (23.5), Services, Hide ⌘H, Hide Others, Show All, Quit Hive ⌘Q), **Edit** (AppKit's Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste, Select All: WKWebView's ⌘C/⌘V in text fields, the editor and the terminal's paste need them, 17.8), **View** (Toggle Sidebar, Toggle Side Panel View ⇧⌘B, Enter Full Screen, whose change `watchFullscreen` follows), **Window** (Minimize ⌘M, Zoom, Close Tab ⌘W; macOS lists the window there) and **Help** (Hive Help, Report an Issue…; macOS adds its search). Windows and Linux keep no menu.
2. The menu only sends user actions (#37). `menu::on_menu` (Tauri's menu event) answers Quit itself with `Hive::request_quit` (see [Closing the app](#closing-the-app-18) item 5) and sends every other item of Hive's own to the UI as `menu {item}`; AppKit's items act on their own and send nothing. `runMenuItem` (`src/menu.ts`) runs the item's action: Settings, Toggle Side Panel View and Close Tab run their `COMMANDS` entry, Toggle Sidebar hides or shows the left sidebar (`sidebarShown`, not kept between runs), Check for Updates… calls `check_update {manual: true}` (an update already downloaded is offered again; otherwise `update_checked` shows "Hive is up to date." or why the check failed), and the two Help items open their GitHub page. As for the shortcuts, nothing but the update check and the links runs under the connection block or while a dialog is open.
3. An item's key that Hive's own keys handle (⌘,, ⇧⌘B, ⌘W) reaches the page first: WKWebView gives a key equivalent to the page, and only one the page leaves unhandled (not `preventDefault`ed) goes on to the menu, so each runs once. In the mock transport `menu <item>` typed in a terminal sends `menu {item}`.

### Notifications (hive.md item 5)
1. The service decides which change alerts (`states::Agent`): the `agent_state` whose displayed state changed carries `alert`: `finished` when it went from working or with subagents to waiting for you, else `action` when it entered a state that asks for an action (waiting for permission, a plan or an answer, or error: `AgentState::asks_action`), else `waiting` when it entered waiting for you (`Alert::entering`). An agent's first state, the snapshot after `welcome`, an interrupt (`interrupted`: the user caused it) and waiting for you while a subagent is still at work (13.3, [Agent states](#agent-states) item 5) carry none; the next `Stop` of the agent with none at work carries the `finished` held back, even when the displayed state stays waiting for you (a wake that only answers in text sends no other hook). It also decides `notify` (13.4): true when the message has an alert and the agent is not `watched` (its terminal is not the one in view of the focused window: the app's `view {terminal, focused}`). `src/connect.ts` passes every service message to `notify` (`src/notify.ts`), which only presents the alert.
2. Each alert plays a short tone at the `notifications.volume` setting (0 plays none); changes within 500 ms share one tone. Elsewhere it is synthesized with Web Audio (no audio file). On macOS the app plays it (17.19): App Nap throttles a hidden webview, so its own tone could come late or not at all. `transport.playTone` calls the app command `play_tone {volume}`; the app side (`Hive::with_tone`) writes the same beep as a WAV file (`tone_wav`: 880 Hz, 0.2 s, falling from 0.15 to 0.001) once a run into its cache folder (`alert-tone.wav`, through a temporary file renamed over it) and plays it with `/usr/bin/afplay -v <volume/100> <file>` (separate arguments, a thread waiting for it). When the app cannot play it, the webview's tone plays instead.
3. An alert with `notify` is also an OS notification: `transport.showNotification` calls the app command `show_notification {title, body, agent}`, its text from `alertText` (`src/notify.ts`, also the inbox item's): the title is the agent's name (its `agent_title`, else "Claude") and what happened ("fix login finished", "Claude is waiting for permission"), the body its place (`agentPlace`: "space · project · worktree"). An alert in view of the focused window gets no notification (the agent that finished there is not pending either, see [Agent states](#agent-states) item 5); the tone still plays. The app side shows it itself (13.5; `Hive::with_notify`, the platform glue in `src-tauri/src/main.rs`): on Windows a `tauri-winrt-notification` toast, on macOS a `UNUserNotificationCenter` notification (19.10, D19.6: `macos::post`, through the Objective-C runtime) posted under an identifier unique to the run, whose click the center's delegate hands back by that identifier (`macos::receive_clicks`, set once at launch and never released, since the center holds it weakly; `identified_notifier` keeps the click of the last 64, `CLICKS`): nothing waits or polls for a click. One shown while Hive is in front goes to Notification Center without a banner (`willPresentNotification`: the list only, as `NSUserNotification` showed it). Both are silent (the tone is Hive's); the Windows toast shows as the installed app (`notifier`: the `identifier` `com.lucas.hive`, which the NSIS installer gives its Start menu shortcut as the AppUserModelID; a development build borrows PowerShell's). A macOS development build cannot use the center (item 6) and, as Linux (development), shows `tauri-plugin-notification`'s, without clicks. Control characters are dropped from the title and body (a toast's XML refuses them). A click brings the window to the front (unminimized, shown, focused) and sends `notification_clicked {agent}`; the UI goes to the agent as an inbox item does (`goToClicked` in `src/notify.ts` → `goToAgent`, switching space when needed), and a click whose agent is gone only brought the window up. The webview has no notification permission. Outside Tauri nothing is shown: the mock transport keeps the last notification's agent, and `notification-click` typed in a mock terminal stands in for a click on it.
4. Inbox (6.5): every alert (item 2, muted or not) is also kept in the store's `inbox` (`addToInbox`, at most 100, newest first: `{id, agent, state, at, text}`, or a terminal's `{id, terminal, failed, at, text}` (17.25, [Shell marks](#shell-marks-and-terminal-alerts-1725)), text "<name> is waiting for permission" / "finished" / "is waiting for you" / "failed"; an optional `space` is left for 6.14). The title bar bell opens it as a menu (`ContextMenu`): the pending agents in tree order, then the alerts with relative times; an item goes to its agent through `goToAgent` (`src/shortcuts.ts`, shared with F8), an ended agent's item is disabled. A dot shows alerts newer than `inboxSeen`; opening marks them read. It lives in memory only. A `pull_ended` (17.27) is kept there too, without tone, OS notification or badge: `{id, at, text, space, offer: {worktree, number, title, state}}`, text "#12 merged: remove fix-login?" ("its worktree" when the app does not know it), meta the space (when several) and project; a merge icon (merged) or a pull request icon (closed); a click opens the Delete worktree dialog (`openModal("remove-worktree")`, the menu's flow: archive script, `--force` retry), disabled once the worktree is gone.
5. The Dock (17.16, macOS app only; `src/dock.ts`, `src/window.ts`): `followDock` keeps the Dock icon's badge on the bell's count (`attention(s).unseen`, sent when it changes; zero removes it, `setBadgeCount()`), and `connect.ts` passes every message to `bounceFor`, which bounces the Dock once (`requestUserAttention(Informational)`) for an `agent_state` whose `alert` is `action` (the service's classification, item 1; no table of states in the app), still `pending`, while the window is not focused and `notifications.bounce` is on (default on; a Settings checkbox shown on macOS only). Waiting for you (finished, seen or not) never bounces. `attention` (`src/attention.ts`: the pending agents in tree order, the bell's unseen count and the most urgent status) is the one source of "what needs the user" for the Dock and the menu bar icon. Capabilities: `core:window:allow-set-badge-count`, `core:window:allow-request-user-attention`.
6. Permission (17.19, macOS): at launch an installed app (one run from `Hive.app/Contents/MacOS/`, `in_app_bundle`) asks `UNUserNotificationCenter` for permission to show alerts (`src-tauri/src/macos.rs`, through the Objective-C runtime: no crate); macOS asks the user only the first time. A development build cannot ask (the center raises an exception outside an app bundle) and is always `unknown`. The settings' Notifications section sends `check_notifications` when it opens and whenever the window comes back to the front; the app reads the center's `authorizationStatus` (waiting at most 2 s) and answers `notification_permission` (denied → `denied`; authorized, provisional or ephemeral → `granted`; anything else, or no answer → `unknown`), kept as the store's `notificationPermission`. When `denied`, the section says notifications are off and offers "Open System Settings" (`open_notification_settings`: the app opens `x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.Notifications-Settings.extension`, a fixed URL, with the opener plugin). The notifications themselves go through the same center (item 3, 19.10). The mock transport answers `granted`, or `denied` with `?mock=denied`.
7. The menu bar icon (17.22, macOS app only; Tauri's own tray, the `tray-icon` feature): `followTray` (`src/tray.ts`, started in `main.tsx`) sends the app command `set_tray {shown, urgent, agents, usage}` whenever it changes: `shown` is `notifications.menu_bar` (default on; a Settings checkbox shown on macOS only; off removes the icon), `urgent` the most urgent status `[state, pending]` (`attention`), `agents` the pending agents in tree order as `[id, title, state, space]` (the title "Claude" when it has none, the space only when there are several) and `usage` the usage ring's lines (`usageLines`, also the status bar's tooltip). The app side builds everything (`src-tauri/src/tray.rs`, tested): the icon is always the same 36×36 px hexagon outline, a template (the menu bar's own colour) when nothing is more urgent than idle or the most urgent is a seen 🟠, else the same outline in the state's colour (the One Light state tokens; 18.5: only the colour changes, never a fill); the menu lists the pending agents ("fix login — Work · waiting for permission", at most 20, then "N more in Hive"; "No agent needs you" when none), the usage lines, and "Show Hive"; texts lose control characters and are cut at 80 characters; the tooltip counts the agents that need you. The icon is drawn and set only when `urgent` differs from the one shown (19.11); the menu and tooltip are set every time. `main.rs` only hands the view to Tauri's tray (`Hive::with_tray`). Choosing an agent brings the window to the front (as a notification's click) and sends `tray_clicked {agent}`, which goes to the agent as item 3's click does (`goToClicked`); "Show Hive" only brings the window up. The icon lives in the app's process, so it works while Hive is hidden or another app is full screen. In the mock transport `tray-click` typed in a terminal stands in for choosing the first pending agent.

### Permissions of programs in terminals (25.5)
A program in a terminal has Hive.app as its responsible process (the service and its terminals are started by the app, and live as long as its connection), so macOS's privacy checks for it are Hive's. That is what Claude Code's Computer Use needs (Screen Recording, Accessibility).
1. Identity: a release is signed with Hive's self-signed certificate when the `release` environment holds it (`scripts/macos-bundle.sh`: `signing-mode`, `sign-hive`, `check --self-signed`, D17.1), so the designated requirement macOS compares its grants with names the certificate, not a `cdhash`, and survives updates. Without the secrets the release stays ad-hoc with a warning. The first self-signed release says so in its notes (`release-notes`).
2. Settings: on a Mac (`isMac`), the Terminal section sends `check_terminal_permissions` when it opens and whenever the window comes back to the front; the app reads `CGPreflightScreenCaptureAccess` and `AXIsProcessTrusted` (`src-tauri/src/macos.rs`), neither of which prompts, and answers `terminal_permissions`. Both `unknown` shows nothing. Each row's button sends `open_privacy_settings {pane}` (`screen_recording`, `accessibility`): the app opens a fixed `x-apple.systempreferences:` URL with the opener plugin; any other pane is refused. The mock answers `granted`, or Screen Recording `denied` with `?mock=denied`.
3. `Info.plist`: no key. macOS has no usage text for either permission (`tccd` knows none): their dialogs are its own.

### Shell marks and terminal alerts (17.25)
Split (#37): what drives an alert, the inbox or a notification is the service's; what only places something in a terminal's scrollback is xterm's.
1. Service (`hive::osc`, read in the output pump `daemon::terminals::copy`, no lock): a scanner finds `ESC ]` … BEL/ST sequences across reads (CAN/SUB or another escape drop one; a payload over 1 KiB is dropped whole; not UTF-8 is ignored) and keeps OSC 133 `C` (a command starts) and `D[;<exit>]` (it ends), OSC 9 `<text>` (iTerm2's notification; a number first is ConEmu's: only `9;4;<state>[;<percent>]`, progress, is read, `9;9;<cwd>` and the like are ignored) and OSC 777 `notify;<title>;<body>`. Texts lose control and invisible characters and are cut (title 100, body 300 characters); an empty notification is none. The output itself passes through untouched.
2. Per terminal (`osc::Shell`): a `D` counts only after a `C` (a second `C` restarts the clock) and at least 1 s later (no setting alerts sooner, so commands marked in a loop make at most one alert check a second); a terminal's notifications less than 1 s apart are dropped; progress is sent only when it changes, at most once a read (its last change), as `terminal_progress` on the terminal's frames, in order with its output.
3. Alerts (`osc::message`, off the output's path since it takes the agents lock): a command that ran at least `notifications.command_secs` (default 10, 0 never) alerts as `command_finished` when no agent runs in that terminal and it is not the terminal in view of the focused window (`view`); a program's notification goes out as `terminal_notification` when no agent runs there (Claude Code's own OSC 9 would repeat the agent's alerts), `notify` false in view of the focused window.
4. App: `notify` (`src/notify.ts`) presents both as an agent's alert is: a tone, an inbox item `{terminal, failed, at, text}` (the terminal's icon, red when the command failed; it shows that terminal while it is open, `activateTab`), and an OS notification (`command_finished` always, `terminal_notification` when `notify`) whose `agent` is `terminal:<id>`, so a click shows the terminal (`goToClicked`). Texts (`terminalAlertText`): "Command finished in <worktree>" / "Command failed (exit 2) in <worktree>", body "after 1m 15s"; a notification's own title (else the terminal's name) and body. `terminal_progress` sets the terminal's `progress` (cleared by `none` and on exit): a small bar on its tab (`ProgressHint` in `TerminalArea.tsx`: percent, red on error, yellow paused, moving when indeterminate unless reduced motion).
5. xterm (`src/marks.ts`, one `Marks` per terminal, made before any output): an OSC 133 handler (payloads over 256 characters ignored) marks each `A` (prompt start) with an xterm marker (at most 1000, the oldest dropped; markers trimmed from the scrollback leave), and on a `D` that follows a `C` decorates that prompt's line with a 2 px bar (`.shell-mark`, green for 0, red otherwise, grey without a status). On macOS ⌘↑ / ⌘↓ (`MAC_JUMP_KEYS` in `src/terminals.ts`, listed in Settings → Shortcuts) bring the previous / next prompt to the top of the view (from the view's top line); a next prompt on the last screen, or none, scrolls to the end. Windows gets no key (D17.0).
6. Shells: fish 4 emits OSC 133 by itself. For zsh, Hive's generated `.zshrc` (`login::startup_files`, 18.40) adds `preexec` / `precmd` hooks (`add-zsh-hook`, after the user's `.zshrc`) emitting `C` and `D;<exit>` (no `A`: no prompt markers in xterm), unless a user `precmd` / `preexec` hook already emits OSC 133. bash is not marked. The mock transport's shell marks its prompts and commands (`false` fails), and `progress <state> <value>`, `long-command <exit> <secs>` and `osc-notify <title>;<body>` typed in a mock terminal stand in for the service's messages.

### Settings
1. The service owns the settings (#37) in `<config>/hive/settings.json` (Hive's folder, `~/.hive`, 22.1): `terminal {font_family, font_size 8–32, scrollback 1000–100000, cursor_style block|bar|underline, cursor_blink, copy_on_select, option_as_meta off|left|right|both, screen_reader, shell default|cmd|git_bash}` (`option_as_meta`: macOS only, which Option keys send Esc + the key, the others typing accents, D20.1; a pre-20.2 `true` reads as `both`, `false` as `off`; `screen_reader`: xterm's `screenReaderMode`, for VoiceOver) (`shell`: native Windows only, see [Native Windows terminals](#native-windows-terminals-1253); ignored elsewhere), `appearance {theme one-dark|one-light|system, diff_colors standard|color-blind}` (`diff_colors`: 28.6, the diff's green and red or blue and orange), `notifications {volume 0–100, bounce, menu_bar, command_secs 0–3600 (0: never, 17.25)}`, `agents {silence_secs 2–60, confirm_close, keep_awake}`, `worktrees {default_base}` (null: the project's current branch), `projects {<project id>: {scripts {setup, run [{name, command}], archive}}}` (see [Project scripts and ports](#project-scripts-and-ports-68)) and `claude {accounts [{name, config_dir, hive_folder}], account, default_name, share_sessions}` (see [Claude accounts](#claude-accounts-122)), `gh {config_dirs}` (the gh config folders besides `gh`'s own, 24.13: see [Spaces](#spaces-614), item 6), `remote {hosts, keep_minutes 1–10080}` (26.17: the hosts the app links to, at most 32 aliases of `~/.ssh/config`, each unique with no leading `-`, whitespace or control characters and at most 255 bytes; default none and 1440, see [Remote hosts over ssh](#remote-hosts-over-ssh-2617)). Defaults: `"Hive Mono", "Symbols Nerd Font", monospace` (a file still holding the pre-7.11 default `"IBM Plex Mono", monospace` gets it too), 13, 5000, block, no blink, no copy on select, Option not Meta, no screen reader mode, default, one-dark, standard, 100, bounce on, menu bar on, 10 s, 5 s, confirm, keep awake, null. Every key is optional (a missing one takes its default) and unknown keys are ignored. Texts (run script names too) must be non-blank, at most 256 bytes, without control characters; scripts non-blank, at most 16 KiB, without control characters but newlines and tabs; run script names unique per project.
2. At start the service reads the file (at most 256 KiB). A missing file is the defaults. An unreadable or invalid one (bad JSON, a value out of range) is the defaults too: the app gets `settings` with them and then `settings_failed` with the reason, on every connect and `get_settings`, until settings are saved; the file is not touched until then. The warning also goes to stderr (`daemon.log`).
3. `set_settings` replaces the whole settings: checked, written (at most 256 KiB, temporary file renamed over it, mode 0600) and then in use at once (the silence rule reads it on every tick). A refusal changes nothing.
4. The store keeps the last `settings` as `settings` (`DEFAULT_SETTINGS` in `src/store.ts` until they arrive) and a `settings_failed` message as `settingsError` (cleared by the next `settings`), also shown as an error toast. The app applies the `terminal` settings to its terminals, `appearance.theme` as `data-theme` on the root element (One Light's CSS variables in `src/styles.css`, from Zed's One theme; `system` shows One Dark or One Light as `prefers-color-scheme` says, live, `watchColorScheme` → the store's `systemTheme`, `appliedTheme`; the setting is kept in `localStorage` (`hive.theme`), so the store starts on it and `main.tsx` sets `data-theme` before the first render, 18.33), `appearance.diff_colors` as `data-diff-colors` on the root element (the `--diff-*` variables of `src/styles.css`, used by the diff view, the gutter markers and the files and Diff panels; Color-blind's pass WCAG AA in both themes, checked by `src/contrast.test.ts` with `src/contrast.ts`, 28.6), `notifications.volume` to the tone, `notifications.bounce` to the Dock and `notifications.menu_bar` to the menu bar icon (see [Notifications](#notifications-hivemd-item-5) items 5 and 7) and `agents.confirm_close` to the close guard.
5. The settings dialog (`src/shell/SettingsDialog.tsx`, Ctrl+,) shows them by section (Terminal, Appearance, Notifications, Agents, Accounts, Worktrees, Projects, Shortcuts, About), or every field whose label matches the search. The Terminal section's Shell select shows only while the app runs the service natively on Windows (`appMode.mode` `native`, 12.5.4); the Agents section's "Keep the Mac awake while agents work" only on a Mac (`isMac`, 17.17), with a note that the display still sleeps and closing the lid on battery still sleeps the Mac. A change sends `set_settings` with the whole settings (text and number fields once typing pauses for 400 ms), one at a time: a change made while one waits for its answer (`settingsPending`) is sent once it arrives, built on it, so a quick second change cannot undo the first. Run script rows carry a key of their own, so a rename keeps the focus and a late edit finds its row. `settingsError` shows at the top. "Open settings file" sends `open_settings_file`. About shows the app's version (`package.json`), the service's (`welcome`), `diagnostics` (asked when the section opens) and the terminals with an `unhooked_agent` badge.

6. Keeping the Mac awake (17.17): while `agents.keep_awake` is on and any agent is shown working or with subagents (🔵, 🟣: `Agent::works`), the service holds one power assertion, `IOPMAssertionCreateWithName(kIOPMAssertionTypePreventUserIdleSystemSleep)` named "Hive: agents are working" (as `caffeinate -i`: idle system sleep only; the display may sleep), and releases it when none is (`src/power.rs`: the `Power` trait and `Awake`, which takes and releases it once per change). It is checked after every agent change (with `subagent_worktrees`) and on every tick, so a settings change applies within a second; the operating system drops it when the service exits. The FFI is `crates/hive/src/macos.rs` (`macos::power`, plain `extern "C"` against IOKit and CoreFoundation, tested on macOS by `macos.yml`); other systems hold nothing (no Windows equivalent, D17.0). A service started with `HIVE_NO_POWER_ASSERTION` set never takes it (the integration tests' services). Check by hand: `pmset -g assertions` lists `PreventUserIdleSystemSleep` named "Hive: agents are working" under the `hive` process while an agent works.

### Projects
1. After `welcome` (also a replayed one), the app's Rust side sends `list_projects`; the UI's "Refresh worktrees" button sends it again. The service also sends `projects` after each worktree hook (see [Worktree hooks](#worktree-hooks)); any other change of git's worktree registry reaches the app through `hive::registry` (9.36).
2. The service answers `projects`. Each project's worktrees come from `git worktree list --porcelain -z`, bare and prunable entries (the directory is gone) skipped. Git gets 10 s (killed with its children after it, as for [Worktree health](#worktree-health)); a timeout is that project's `error` only (9.13).
3. `add_project {path}` (the add-project dialog): the path must be absolute, an existing directory and inside a git repository with a working tree. It is normalised to the main worktree (the first entry of `git worktree list`), so a subfolder or a linked worktree adds its repository. A new project is appended to the current space in `<data>/hive/spaces.json`; if that write fails the list is unchanged and the answer is `add_project_failed {error: storage}`. A project already in another space is refused (`in_other_space`, "<path> is already in the space <name>").
4. Project requests run on a blocking thread, off the app's frame loop, because git can be slow.
5. Worktree requests name a project by id and are refused ("<id> is not a followed project") for any other.

### Groups (14.1)
A group is a folder outside git holding repositories (an API and its front end): the hierarchy is Group → Project → Worktree → Agent.
1. `add_project` on a folder that is in no git repository (`projects::validate`'s `not_a_git_repository`) makes it a group when some of its direct children are repositories (`projects::scan`: real folders, not links, holding a `.git` folder; UTF-8 names; at most the first 1024 entries read); with none it is a folder project (see [Folder projects](#folder-projects-231)). The folder is resolved. It is kept in its space's `projects`, and in its `groups` (`spaces.json`, `#[serde(default)]`, written only when not empty, so a file of an earlier version loads and saves as it was); its repositories are not kept: they are its folder's, read when first needed and kept until the projects are forgotten (its folder's watch), so one cloned in or deleted joins or leaves on its own. A group is never moved into another, and a change of the spaces that `check` would refuse at the next start is not saved. The current space's projects among them move into it (`Spaces::add_group`); one another space follows stays there, out of the group. Adding one of its repositories again answers it as followed.
2. `projects` lists a group as a project with `group: true` and one worktree, its folder (`main`, no branch, never a status: `health::of` does not ask git), followed by its repositories with `parent` set. The folder is where its own terminals and agents are placed (`projects::place`, the deepest worktree wins, so a repository's own stay its own). Its terminals get the group's space environment and no `HIVE_*` (scripts and ports are not asked for). It is no repository: worktree, file, changes and pull request requests refuse it (`Projects::root`, `projects::followed`, `holding`).
3. `hive::registry` watches each of the current space's group folders too, once and recursively (D19.2; on Linux non-recursively, with each folder directly in one, at most 1024 per group): an entry of the group made, removed or renamed (not a file written), or a `.git` made or removed in one of its folders (a `git init`), lists the projects again (`State::worktrees_changed`, which now also sees a project gone: `known` compares the whole list).
4. `remove_project` on a group: refused while a terminal works in its folder or a worktree of its repositories; the group and its repositories go, their settings and port blocks too, `project_removed` for each repository, then for the group.
5. UI: the sidebar shows a group's row (Phosphor's `Folders`, its "+" a new chat in its folder) with its own agents under it, then its repositories one level in (`.in-group`); collapsed, it shows the most urgent state of every agent inside, its repositories' too. Selecting it shows its terminals; it has no files panel (`panelWorktree`), no pull requests or runs, and its menu only removes it ("Remove group…"); a repository of it has no "Remove project…". F8 and the palette open the group of what they go to.

### Folder projects (23.1)
A folder project is a folder in no git repository that holds none (D22.11): followed so Claude can work in it without git. It is built on the group's model.
1. `add_project` on such a folder (`projects::scan` finds no repository in it, and no `.git` folder of its own that git refuses) follows it as a group with none (`groups` in `spaces.json`, so a file of an earlier version loads unchanged). Refused with `protected_folder`, for a group too (a typo should not follow the whole disk): `/`, the home folder itself, and Hive's folder (the spaces file's, and the settings'), Claude's config folders (`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `~/.claude`) and what they hold, and any folder holding one of these (`Projects::protect`, resolved); a repository in one is still followed. Before its files or `init_git` are acted on, a folder project's folder must still resolve to itself (not swapped for a link) and not be protected.
2. `projects` lists it as a group (`group: true`) with `folder: true`, one worktree (its folder), no repositories. Its terminals and agents are placed in it, with its space's environment and no `HIVE_*`; its sessions show in the Sessions panel. A repository made or cloned in it makes it a group (`folder: false`), one removed a folder project again.
3. Its folder has a files panel: `watch_worktree`, `expand_ignored`, the file requests and `search_files` take it (`Projects::tree`), listed and searched by git as the work tree of an empty bare repository, `<data>/hive/plain.git` (made by `git init --bare` when first needed; git has no listing without a repository; its index is empty, so every file is untracked and `.gitignore` files still apply). `watch_worktree` sends `files` only, never `changes` or a status; `open_file` has no base. `list_changes`, pull requests, runs and worktree requests refuse it as a group's folder.
4. `init_git` runs `git init -q` in it (separate arguments). `hive::registry` watches it as a group's folder, so the `.git` made lists the projects again; before that list `watch_registry` (and `list_projects`) runs `Projects::settle`, which reads the groups' folders again and takes each one that is now a repository out of `groups`: a project of its space like any other, with no re-add. Even before, `projects` already lists it as a repository (`Projects::entries`).
5. UI: its sidebar row has Phosphor's `FolderSimpleDashed`, its "+" a new chat in it, and its own agents under it; selecting it shows its terminals. The files panel shows only Files and Sessions for it (`panelViews`); its menu offers "Initialize git" and "Remove project…"; no new worktree (the shortcut does nothing on it).

### New projects (23.2)

1. The add-project dialog's "New project" mode (D22.12): a parent folder (the folder browser, starting at the last parent a project was created in, kept in the app's local storage, else the home folder), a name, "Initialize git" (on by default) and "Open a terminal running claude" (on by default). It sends `create_project`; the rules are the service's (`dirs::check_name`, `dirs::target`, `dirs::make`, `Projects::create`), whose refusal shows under the name.
2. The service checks the name (one path segment, see `create_dir`), that `parent` is an absolute, existing folder (local on Windows), resolves it, and refuses the new folder with `protected_folder` when 23.1 would refuse following it (in Hive's or Claude's folder, a git repository or not) before anything is made. It makes the folder with `create_dir` semantics (`DirBuilder`, not recursive, 0755 less the umask): a taken name is `name_taken` and nothing is touched. With `git`, `git init -q` (separate arguments) runs in it; its failure is `create_failed` and the folder made stays. Then `Projects::add` follows it: a repository is a project, a plain folder a folder project (or the repository it is in), in the current space.
3. On `project_created`, the dialog remembers the parent, selects `path`, opens a terminal there with `claude` typed when the box is on (`openClaude`, as a new worktree's terminal), and closes.
4. The folder browser's "New folder" button (in the add-project dialog, both modes) asks for a name inline and sends `create_dir`; on `dir_created` the field moves into the new folder.

### New worktree (screens 1c/1d)
1. The dialog opens for a project (the row's "New worktree", or `openModal("new-worktree", id)`), sends `list_branches` and `validate_worktree_name` for the empty name.
2. Every keystroke in the name sends `validate_worktree_name`: the service runs `worktree::check_name` (the rule and the existing-folder check of `hive worktree create`, no git), so the dialog never has its own rule (#33, #37). Create stays disabled until the current name has a clean verdict.
3. The branch list is filtered in the UI (case-insensitive substring) and virtualized (TanStack Virtual); ↑/↓ in the filter move the pick. A pick hidden by the filter gives way to the first branch shown.
4. `create_worktree` runs `worktree::create`, the CLI's code. "From issue" (17.24) swaps the name field for an issue number and sends `create_issue_worktree` with the same base; the service names the branch, so the plan shows none. Its "Start claude" box is not offered: Hive prepares the worktree and the human starts `claude`. On `worktree_created` the UI selects the new worktree, runs the project's setup script, if any, in a terminal tab of its own (`openWith`), opens a terminal tab in it if asked (`openTerminal` of `src/terminals.ts` with its path), and closes the dialog; with notes, the dialog stays open to show them.

The list is `spaces.json`, `{current, spaces: [{id, name, projects, env}]}`, written through a temporary file (mode 0600) renamed over it. Without it, the flat list of earlier versions (`projects.json`, a JSON array of paths) becomes the "Default" space (id `default`), and is left as it is; `spaces.json` is written on the first change. A missing file is an empty list. An unreadable or invalid one (bad JSON, over 1 MiB, or breaking a space rule: no space, an unknown current one, a repeated id, a project in two spaces, a bad name or environment) is moved to `<name>.corrupt` with a warning on stderr (`daemon.log`), and the service starts with an empty list.

### Spaces (6.14)
A space groups projects (work, personal) with an optional identity for the terminals opened in them. Agents of every space stay alive and keep alerting.
1. Rules (`hive::spaces`): a name is trimmed, not blank, at most 64 characters, without control characters. Each `env` value is trimmed and blank means unset; git name and email are at most 256 bytes, the two folders at most 4096 bytes and absolute, all without control characters. A `gh_account` login is 1–39 ASCII letters, digits, `-` or `_`, its host 1–253 letters, digits, `.`, `-` or `:`, neither starting with `-`. On `create_space`/`update_space` the folders must exist as directories (a file read at start only needs the shape, so a folder gone later does not lose the list). New spaces get the ids `space-<n>`, the first free `n`. A project is in one space only. Only a space without projects is deleted, never the last one; deleting the current one makes the first one current. Every change is saved at once; a refused or unsaved one changes nothing (`space_failed`).
2. A terminal opened in a worktree of a project gets its space's `env` as environment entries (never a shell string): `GIT_AUTHOR_NAME` + `GIT_COMMITTER_NAME`, `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL` + `GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL`, `GH_CONFIG_DIR` (its GitHub account's folder, 24.13), only those set (`spaces::vars`, which Hive's own git and `gh` runs get too), plus `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` for its Claude account (22.5, terminals only, when it is one of the accounts: `daemon::terminals`), and with a `gh_account` its token plus `GH_HOST` (item 6). The placement uses `projects::place` on the terminal's `cwd`; outside every project nothing is added. Changes apply to terminals opened afterwards.
3. The Claude account is the space's (22.5, D22.15, reversing 12.2's "independent of the space"; [Claude accounts](#claude-accounts-122)): a terminal gets its **project's** space's, not the current space's, so an agent of another space keeps its own. The terminal keeps the Claude config folder it was opened with, and an agent detected in it takes it: its session name is read from `<folder>/projects`, else from the service's own root (`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/projects`, else `~/.claude/projects`), and its transcript is read when it lies inside that folder or any account's. The Sessions panel (`list_sessions`, `locate_session`, `delete_session`) covers the current space's projects in every account's folder.
4. UI: a select at the left of the sidebar header lists the spaces plus "New space…" and "Edit space…" (the dialog `SpaceDialog`: name, Claude account (a select of the settings' accounts, the default one first and by default; a folder no account has shows and saves as the default one), git name, git email, GitHub account (item 6); "Delete space" only enabled for an empty one). The sidebar shows the current space's projects (`spaceProjects`); the Sessions panel lists again when the space changes. The pending bell, F8, tones and OS notifications cover every space; a notification and each inbox item (pending or kept) name the agent's space when there are several (`agentPlace`, `spaceName` in `src/notify.ts`; `InboxItem.space`), and going to an agent of another space (`goToAgent` in `src/shortcuts.ts`, used by F8 and the inbox) sends `select_space` for it.
5. Removing a project (9.28): "Remove project…" in the project row's menu and in the palette asks through `ConfirmDialog` (`askRemoveProject`: names the project, says its files stay on disk, and warns when one of its file tabs has unsaved edits), then sends `remove_project`. The service refuses it while a process of one of its own terminals (by the terminals' session ids in `/proc`) works in the project or one of its worktrees, then takes it out of its space (`Spaces::remove`, saved as every space change) and drops its settings and port blocks (best effort, after the spaces are saved). Its space may then be deleted if it became empty. Adding the folder again later is a fresh add. The app's `project_removed` drops its rows and sessions, its file tabs, the panel state of its worktrees (files, changes, comments, new folders, search, collapsed folders) and its keys in `hive.agentOrder` and `hive.tabOrder`; `remove_project_failed` shows as an error toast.
6. GitHub account (9.30): a space may name one of the accounts logged in to `gh` (the human has several and switches with `gh auth switch` by hand); Hive uses it without changing `gh`'s active account. `list_gh_accounts` runs `gh auth status` (in the `GH_CONFIG_DIR` asked, checked as a space's folder is): `gh` checks each token with GitHub, so it runs only when the space dialog or the settings' Accounts section opens, or the settings' gh config folders change (`useGhAccounts`, once per folder), never polled. Its lines "✓ Logged in to <host> account <login> (<source>)" / "X Failed to log in to …" and "- Active account: true|false" give the list (at most 32; bad logins or hosts left out); exit 1 with none is "not logged in". When a terminal of the space opens, `gh auth token --hostname <host> --user <login>` (in the account's folder, `spaces::gh_dir`) gives the token, trimmed and kept only when it is 1–1024 printable ASCII characters; it goes into that terminal's environment only (`GH_TOKEN` on github.com and `*.ghe.com`, `GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN` on other hosts, where `gh` ignores `GH_TOKEN`), never to the app, a log or a file. No token (no `gh`, logged out): the terminal opens without it, and the reason (naming the login, never a token) goes to `daemon.log` and to the app as `notice`, so the human knows it acts as `gh`'s active account. `gh_accounts` carries logins only. "Make active in gh…" (shown when the chosen account is listed and not active in its folder) asks through `ConfirmDialog` (its `back` keeps the space dialog open underneath) before `switch_gh_account` in the account's folder.

   **gh config folders (24.13).** The space dialog has no folder field: its select lists "gh's active account", then the accounts of `gh`'s own config, then those of each gh config folder of the settings (`gh.config_dirs`), each named with its folder's last segment ("work (gh-work)"); the space keeps the chosen one with its folder (`gh_account.config_dir`), which its terminals and Hive's `gh` and git runs get as `GH_CONFIG_DIR`. Settings → Accounts has a "GitHub accounts" group beside the Claude accounts, in the same columns: one row per folder (its logins, the folder, "Not logged in" or "Incomplete" with `gh`'s problem as tooltip, remove; `gh`'s own, `~/.config/gh`, first and never removed) and "Add a gh config folder" (`FolderField`, from home, hidden folders shown). A folder is checked as a space's was (absolute, at most 4096 bytes, printable, trailing separators dropped), must exist when added, is kept once, at most 64; `set_settings` refuses to remove one a space's account is in. Migration: a spaces file from before 24.13 has `env.gh_config_dir`; at load its space's `gh_account` takes the folder (a folder that is no absolute path goes), and each distinct folder joins the settings' (`Store::migrate`, with the 12.2 accounts: settings saved first, then `spaces.json` without the field; a failure leaves both for the next start). A space with a folder but no account keeps no `GH_CONFIG_DIR` (its folder is listed in the settings, to pick an account from). For 9.31/9.32, `Gh::run(env, cwd, args, limit)` runs `gh` for a project as its space's terminals would (`Projects::space_env(cwd)` gives the space's environment; `State::gh()` gives `gh` on the user's `PATH`); its error is `gh`'s stderr (never its stdout), fit to show as is. Recordings of the real output (gh 2.46): `scripts/spike/record-gh.sh`. A space with an account but no git name or email (21.5, D22.8) commits as git's own guess while it pushes as the account: the dialog warns ("Commits will use git's own guess") and "Use the GitHub account's" sends `get_gh_identity`; `Gh::identity` runs `gh api user` with the account's token (output capped at 64 KiB, 30 s), reads only `id` and `login` (the login checked as any other) and answers the login and `<id>+<login>@users.noreply.<host>`, which fill the empty fields.

### Claude accounts (12.2)
Several Claude accounts at once, each space using one (22.5, D22.15): an account is a name and a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
1. The service keeps them in the settings (`claude.accounts`, `hive::settings`); each space names its account by folder in `spaces.json` (`env.claude_account`, so a rename keeps it). The default account (no `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`: `~/.claude`, or the service's own) always exists, is not in the list and cannot be removed; its name is `claude.default_name` (21.4), "Default" when missing or empty (the service saves and sends an empty one as "Default"), checked as an account's name. A name is checked as a space's (trimmed, not blank, at most 64 characters, no control characters), unique and not the default account's current name; a folder as a space's were (absolute, at most 4096 bytes, printable) and unique; neither with spaces around it. A folder must exist when `set_settings` adds it (one already there may be gone since). `set_settings` refuses to remove an account a space uses, naming the space. A space's folder no account has (a hand edit) is the default account.
2. A new terminal gets its project's space's account's folder as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (none for the default, or outside every project), or the account `open_terminal` asks for ("Log in…", resuming a session). Open terminals keep theirs; so does the agent detected in one (see [Spaces](#spaces-614) item 3, and `restore_sessions`).
3. The Sessions panel lists the sessions of every account (the default one's projects folder, then each account's), whichever is current; each carries its account's folder (`config_dir`). Resuming or forking one opens its terminal with that account (`resume` in `src/sessions.ts`), and "Copy Resume Command" copies the session's `resume_command`, which the service writes for the shell of Hive's terminals (`sessions::resume_command`), with the account's folder for another account's: `cd '<cwd>' && CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR='<folder>' claude --resume <id>` for POSIX shells and fish (WSL, Linux, macOS) and native Windows' Git Bash; `Set-Location -LiteralPath '<cwd>'; $env:CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR='<folder>'; claude --resume <id>` for PowerShell (each quote mark it reads as one, `'‘’‚‛`, doubled); `cd /d "<cwd>" && set "CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<folder>" && claude --resume <id>` for Command Prompt, null (the item disabled) when a folder holds a `"`, `%`, `!` or control character, which it cannot quote. Claude's records of running `claude`s are read beside each projects folder (`<folder>/sessions/<pid>.json`).
4. Shared sessions (21.10, D22.5, changed by the human 2026-11-03: move, not link): with `claude.share_sessions` on (off by default; the settings' Accounts section), resuming or forking from the history runs as the account of the session's project's space instead (22.5; the history lists the current space's projects, so the current space's account) (a fork of a session running elsewhere stays its own: its log cannot move); "Continue in ▸ <account>" in the session's menu (with more than one account, not while it runs) does it for any other account whatever the setting. When the log is in another account's folder, `resume` first sends `move_session` and waits for its answer: the service (`Sessions::move_to`) moves `<id>.jsonl` and the `<id>/` folder beside it into the target account's `projects/<same project folder>/` (made 0700 when missing; one that is a link refused). Claude Code 2.1.311 refuses to resume a symlinked or hard-linked log and rewrites logs in place (compaction), so a link is not an option (the 21.10 spike). A move is a rename that refuses to replace (`file::rename_new`: `renamex_np(RENAME_EXCL)`, `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)`, `MoveFileExW`), else (another disk, `CrossesDevices`) a copy (at most 4 GiB, 10 000 entries, 8 deep; links refused, sources opened `O_NOFOLLOW`; files 0600, synced; on failure only what it made is removed) then the original removed; never over something there, even something that appeared after the check. The app waits for one move of a session at a time, at most 30 s. `file-history/<id>` stays behind: rewind checkpoints from before the move are lost; tool outputs Claude saved under `<id>/tool-results/` move too, but the log names them by their old absolute path. The list shows one row per session id (the newest log), and each row names the account whose folder holds its log when there is more than one account. Off, a session resumes as its own account, as before. `restore_sessions` keeps each session's terminal account.
5. Migration: spaces used to hold `claude_config_dir` in their `env`. At start, each distinct one in `spaces.json` becomes an account named after its space ("Work", then "Work 2"… when taken), unless an account has that folder; the settings are saved first, then `spaces.json` without the field. A failure (an ignored settings file, which is never replaced; a write that fails) leaves both files as they were and is retried at the next start, so nothing is lost. Then (22.5) the account selected before, `claude.account` in `settings.json` (an account's folder), becomes the `claude_account` of every space without one (a space's own is never replaced, also when a failed settings save makes the next start try again), so nothing changes on upgrade, and the settings are saved without it (`Store::migrate_account`; a `null` or unknown one only leaves the file). The spaces are saved first; a failure leaves the settings file as it was, retried at the next start. An older settings file still loads.
6. UI: the status bar shows the current space's account name, read-only (`SpaceAccount` in `StatusBar.tsx`; hidden while there is only the default account), with its session usage (12.1, which follows the current space's account), the tooltip "Claude account of <space>; change it in the space's settings"; a click opens the space's dialog. The terminals' "Claude account: <name>" (21.7) shows only with more than one account in use by the spaces (`accountLine`). The settings' Accounts section (22.6) has three titled groups, Claude accounts, Sessions and Shared, each with a one-line help (the rest in its tooltip). The accounts are one grid of fixed columns: name (renamed in place, the default one too), folder (cut from the start of the path to whole characters so its last segment stays, the whole path in the tooltip; `FolderCell`), status, "Log in…", remove (×; an empty slot on the default row). "New account" under the list opens the form (a name, its folder made by Hive or an existing one, item 8; Cancel or Add close it); "Log in…" on any of them opens a terminal where a new terminal would open (the files panel's worktree, `panelWorktree`; disabled without one) with `claude` typed in as that account (`openClaude` with `{config_dir}`), and closes the settings: the user types `/login` there. Hive never reads or writes credentials (#45).
7. Not logged in (21.6): `list_claude_logins` answers, per account, whether its Claude Code config file has a top-level `oauthAccount` that is not null (`hive::logins`); the value is skipped unread. The file is the one Claude Code uses: `.config.json` in the account's config folder (its folder; for the default account `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` of the service, else `~/.claude`) when that legacy file exists, else `.claude.json` in the account's folder, and for the default account `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.claude.json`, else `~/.claude.json` (beside `~/.claude`, not in it). A missing, unreadable, invalid or not regular file, or one over 16 MiB, reads as not logged in. The credentials (the Keychain, `.credentials.json`) are never read (#45). The app asks once connected, whenever the accounts change, whenever its window comes back to the front (a `/login` finishes in the browser) and when the settings' Accounts section opens (`useClaudeLogins`); until answered every account counts as logged in. An account not logged in shows a "Not logged in" badge in its row's status column in the settings, and, when it is the current space's (22.5), one small warning mark after its name in the status bar (22.6), whose tooltip is "Not logged in: click to log in" and whose click opens the same log-in terminal as "Log in…" (`useLogIn`; disabled without a worktree).

8. Folders Hive makes (22.3, D22.1): "New account" asks only for a name; `create_account` makes `accounts/<slug>` in Hive's folder (`Paths::accounts_dir`, mode 0700; the slug is the name's letters and digits, lowercase, any run of other characters one `-`: "Work 2" is `work-2`) and saves the account. Refused, with nothing made: a name with no letter or digit, a folder there already, a name or folder another account has. The app then says "<name> is made: log in to it." (its row has the "Log in…"). "Use an existing folder" adds one as before (`FolderField`). A folder's trailing separators go wherever the service takes one (`spaces::trimmed`, 22.2: in `spaces::dir`, so a space's environment as `Spaces::check` keeps it on every read; on every load and save of the settings; in the pre-22.5 selected account and the 12.2 migration's folders): `~/.claude-work/`, as a clicked folder is spelled, and `~/.claude-work` are one folder; a root stays `/`. An account's `hive_folder` is set by the service on every load and save (its folder right in `accounts/`, named as a slug, spelled exactly as Hive makes it: `//work` is not), never taken from the file or the app. Removing one asks whether to delete its folder too (its sessions, settings and skills), saying to run `/logout` first: the credentials Claude Code keeps outside the folder (the macOS Keychain) are never touched (#45). A folder Hive did not make is never deleted, nor one another account also has under another spelling. At every start the service makes Hive's folder and `accounts/` 0700 when missing (also under `HIVE_HOME`); an existing folder keeps its mode. The app sends `create_account` and `remove_account` in turn with its saves (`requestSettings`), so a save queued meanwhile builds on their answer.

### The shared plugin (21.9)
Each account's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` has its own `skills/`, `agents/`, `commands/` and MCP servers; `<data>/hive/shared` is shared by every account (D22.4), as a Claude Code plugin loaded with `--plugin-dir` (Claude Code's plugin docs, checked 2026-11-03: `--plugin-dir <folder>` loads the plugin in place for that session, as `<name>@inline`, with its `skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, `agents/*.md`, `commands/*.md`, `hooks/hooks.json` and `.mcp.json`; every component is named under the manifest's `name`: the skill `/hive-shared:review`, the agent `hive-shared:tester`, the MCP server `plugin:hive-shared:db`; a session-only plugin replaces an installed plugin of the same name).
1. `hive::shared::ensure` makes the folder when missing, at the daemon's start (`wrapper::install`; a failure is logged and the service starts anyway) and on `get_shared` / `open_shared`: `skills/`, `agents/`, `commands/` and `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` with only `name` (`hive-shared`) and `description`, each folder 0700 (it holds MCP servers' tokens; on Unix the folder is set to 0700 each time). What is there already, the manifest too, is left as the user made it.
2. The Unix wrapper adds `--plugin-dir <data>/hive/shared` after `--settings`, while the folder is a folder (the shell's `-d`), to every `claude` of Hive's terminals, whatever the account; a `claude` started inside a hooked one (`HIVE_WRAPPED=1`) gets neither, as it has both already. The native Windows wrapper (`claude.exe`, frozen, D17.0) does not pass it.
3. Settings → Accounts' "Shared" section asks `get_shared` when it opens and lists what `shared` holds (`/<plugin>:<skill>` for skills and commands); "Open folder" sends `open_shared`, whose `editor_target` the app opens once (`openTarget`, key `""`/`shared`). Credentials are never in it: Hive never reads an account's folder for it.

### Pull requests (9.31)

The right panel's "PRs" view shows the pull requests of the shown worktree's project; 9.32's "Actions" view goes beside it and reuses its pieces (`hive::gh::Gh::run`, the `run` helper and `Cache` pattern of `hive::pulls`, the view's refresh).

1. The service finds the project's GitHub repository in its remotes (`git config --get-regexp`), on the space account's host (github.com without an account), and runs `gh` as a terminal of that space would (`Gh::run`: its account's `GH_CONFIG_DIR` and token), with separate arguments only (`--title=` and the like for the human's text, so nothing can pass for an option).
2. The list is one GraphQL request (`crates/hive/src/pulls.graphql`): the repository's merge methods, `author:@me` and `is:open review-requested:@me` searches. It is asked for when the view shows and every 2 minutes while it shows (`PULLS_INTERVAL_MS`; paused while the window is hidden and asked once on its return, D18.11: `useRefresh` in `src/refresh.ts`, which the Actions view uses too; the sidebar's 1 s clock stops while hidden as well), by the sidebar once per project after connecting (its badges), and on demand (Refresh, and after every action). The service fetches at most once per 2 minutes per project and account unless forced; after GitHub's rate limit ("rate limit" in `gh`'s error) it waits 2, 4, 8… up to 30 minutes, even when forced, and the error says when.
3. Details, reviews and comments are Markdown from GitHub: the app renders them with 8.6's `Markdown` (no raw HTML, no images, links through `safeUrl`, opened by `openLink`).
4. Merge and close ask first (8.20's dialog). "Check out in a new worktree" (17.24) makes the worktree `pr-<number>` with 4.x's flow (`.worktreeinclude`, hooks warnings) on the pull request's branch, fetched by Hive's own git rather than `gh pr checkout` (which would move a local branch of the same name, e.g. a fork's `main`): see `hive::checkout`. Nothing is made when `gh`, the branch check or the fetch fails. The app then runs the project's setup script and selects it; no terminal or `claude` is opened.
5. A sidebar worktree row shows `#<number>` (coloured by state) when a list names a pull request on its branch.

### Actions (9.32)

The right panel's "Actions" view, beside "PRs", shows the latest workflow runs of the shown worktree's project, filtered on the worktree's branch at first ("All branches" and the project's other worktrees' branches in its select).

1. `hive::actions` runs `gh` as `hive::pulls` does (the space's account, separate arguments, untrusted JSON with size limits, errors cut at 4 KiB). The list is asked for when the view shows and every 2 minutes while it shows (`PULLS_INTERVAL_MS`), by the sidebar once per project after connecting (every branch, for its badges), and on demand; the service's `Cache` (a second instance of `hive::pulls::Cache`, keyed by project, account and branch) keeps GitHub from being asked more often, and backs off after its rate limit. A run's jobs are asked for when it opens and on demand; while the run is in progress and its jobs show, every 30 s (`RUN_INTERVAL_MS`), paused while the window is hidden (asked once on its return). Nothing uses `gh run watch`.
2. A job that failed offers the end of its log (`job_log`), shown as plain text in a monospace block scrolled to its end; the service has already dropped its escapes and control characters.
3. Re-run (all jobs, or only the failed ones of a failed run) is sent at once; cancel (a run in progress) asks first (8.20's dialog). Finished runs raise no notification (the bell stays for agents).
4. A sidebar worktree row shows its branch's latest run from the all-branches list as one mark (✓ ✗ ● –, coloured like a pull request's checks) after the pull request badge; a click selects the worktree and shows that run. A pull request's check whose page is an Actions job (`run` not null) opens its run in this view instead of GitHub.
5. Not covered: a fork's pull request run whose head branch has the name of a local worktree's branch shows as that worktree's (`gh run list` does not say which repository the branch is in).

### Commit, push and run CI (27.6)

D27.3 (the human, 2026-11-24) changes 9.31 (b): Hive pushes, as a click of the user's. The agent is never asked to commit or push (#52); nothing merges (#12).

1. Staging: the Diff tree's file rows (`changedOnly`) start with a box for each file `changes` gives a `staged` (`no`, `partly` as indeterminate, `yes`); a file only committed since a branch base has none. A click on the box, or Space on the active row, sends `stage_files` for that file (stage unless it is all staged); the commit box's own box stages or unstages every file left to commit. Staging writes the real index (what `git commit` reads); `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0` does not stop it. The files watch sees the index change and sends the new `changes`.
2. Commit: the commit box (`src/shell/CommitBox.tsx`, under the Diff summary) has the message and "Commit", which asks first (8.20's dialog: the files, the branch, the subject) and sends `commit`. The repository's hooks run as git runs them; their output and git's message, as is, show under the box on a failure. The message empties once committed.
3. Push: "Push…" in the commit box and on the worktree's menu opens `PushDialog`, which asks `get_push_target` and says where the branch goes (its upstream, or `origin/<branch>` made its upstream, and the commits to send) before Push. A rejected push shows git's message in the dialog. Credentials are git's and `gh`'s (9.30): the push gets the space's identity, `GH_CONFIG_DIR` and its account's token as its terminals do, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never`.
4. Create pull request (9.31): `NewPullDialog` asks the push target too; when the branch has no upstream, commits not pushed, or an unknown state, "Push … first" shows, ticked, and `create_pull` carries `push`.
5. Run CI: "Run workflow…" (an icon in the Actions view's bar) opens `RunWorkflowDialog`: the workflows `list_workflows` gives (as the shown worktree's `HEAD` has them), the branch (the worktree's), and each input as GitHub's form shows it (a choice as a select, a boolean as a box, else text; required ones must be filled). Run sends `run_workflow`; on `workflow_started` the dialog closes, a toast says so, and the Actions view shows the new run (`openRun`), refreshed as 9.32 does.

### Worktree health
1. `hive::health` reads, per worktree, on a blocking thread and with a 10 s limit per git command (git and its children, in their own process group, are killed after it): `changes` = the entries of `git status --porcelain=v2 -z --untracked-files=all --find-renames` (as `changes` counts them, so linked worktrees inside the main one count as untracked folders unless ignored); `added`/`removed` = their lines against `HEAD` as the Changes panel totals them against `HEAD` (`changes::totals`: `git diff --numstat -z --find-renames --no-ext-diff --no-textconv HEAD --`, plus every line of an untracked text file within the Changes panel's limits, 8 MiB a file and 32 MiB a listing; a binary file adds none; a `git diff` that fails or times out gives 0 lines and keeps the rest of the status; optional in the protocol, 0 from an older service); `last_commit_ms` from `git log -1 --format=%ct HEAD`; for a linked worktree, when the main worktree is on a branch, `ahead`/`behind` from `git rev-list --left-right --count HEAD...refs/heads/<branch> --`, and `merged` = `ahead` is 0 (every commit of its `HEAD` is on that branch, what `git merge-base --is-ancestor` answers, without another git run). The main worktree has `ahead`/`behind` null and `merged` false. Any git failure (no commit yet, the branch is gone, the time limit) makes the status null; `projects` never fails because of it.
2. Every message carrying projects (`projects`, `project_added`, `worktree_created`, `worktree_removed`, `worktree_renamed`, `brought_back`) carries fresh statuses (a list of every space's projects: the current space's only, D19.3; a space switched to gets its statuses as `watch_registry` follows it). The service remembers the last status sent per worktree path (`health::Sent`) and sends `worktree_status` only when one differs: every 30 s for every worktree of the current space, as last listed (the tick lists nothing again; a clean worktree runs no `git diff`, 19.5) (first right when the service starts; `HIVE_HEALTH_INTERVAL_MS` in its environment sets another interval, for the tests), except while the app's `view` says the window is hidden (D18.11): a tick missed then runs as soon as it shows again, and the next one a whole interval later. A window merely out of focus keeps the check, and for the watched worktree after each of its changes (after its `changes`, whose `git` runs it reuses when they were listed against `HEAD`).
3. The sidebar shows the status as muted badges after the worktree's row: `↑ahead`, `↓behind` (each only when not 0), the lines as `+added −removed` in the added/removed colours (`Counts`, as a changed file shows them: a side that is 0 shows nothing; the tooltip "3 changed files, +12 −4 lines"), or `●changes` ("2 changed files") when the changes add no lines (binary or empty files) and "merged", each with a tooltip. The project row's context menu has "Remove merged worktrees…" (`RemoveMergedDialog` in `src/shell/WorktreeMenu.tsx`): it lists the project's linked worktrees that are merged with no changes, as they were when it opened, all checked, and sends a plain `remove_worktree` (never `force`) for each checked one; each shows "Removing…", "Removed" or its `remove_worktree_failed` message (kept by path in `worktreeDialog.removeFailures`). Nothing is ever merged (#12).

### Review worktrees (27.4)
1. A review is a worktree where the user (or an agent they start there: its terminals and agents are ordinary, #52) tries and fixes another worktree's work without touching it (`hive::review`). "Review in a new worktree…" on a worktree's menu (disabled when it is detached) opens a dialog that says what happens and sends `create_review`: the Claude worktree `.claude/worktrees/review-<name>` on a new branch `review-<name>` (the reviewed worktree's sidebar name, lowercased, every other character a `-`, at most 64 bytes of it; `-2`, `-3`, … when the folder or the branch is taken) from the tip of the reviewed branch (`refs/heads/<branch>`), through `worktree::create_on`, as a new worktree is made (its `.worktreeinclude` files copied). Only committed work is in it.
2. The link (which worktree reviews which, and their project) is kept by the service in `reviews.json` next to the spaces file in Hive's folder (22.1; 0600, temporary file + rename, at most 256 links and 1 MiB), never inside `.git` (#56). Every project listing marks a review's worktree with `review_of` (the reviewed worktree's path) while both are listed; when git lists a project afresh (without an error), its links whose review or reviewed worktree is gone are dropped, so a link goes with either worktree however it was removed; a rename moves it. A file that cannot be read is moved aside to `reviews.json.corrupt` and the links start empty (a warning); one that cannot be saved keeps the links for the run (a note on `worktree_created`, a warning otherwise).
3. The sidebar shows a review under the worktree it reviews (after its agents; the reviewed row gets a chevron for it), with a "review" badge. Its menu adds "Bring changes back…" and "Discard review…".
4. "Bring changes back…" (`BringBackDialog`) asks `plan_bring_back` and shows the commits that would go, into which branch, and whether it is a fast-forward or a merge commit; "Bring back" sends `bring_back` with the plan's `into`, `base` and `tip` (#12: only that click merges). Hive merges, never rebases or cherry-picks: the review's commits keep their ids, a fast-forward when the reviewed branch did not move, else one merge commit. `brought_back` closes the dialog with a notice; `bring_back_failed` shows git's message in it.
5. "Discard review…" (`DiscardReviewDialog`) is the delete dialog's (4.4): `discard_review`, then "Discard anyway" (`force`) after a refusal; it says the branch goes only when every commit of it was brought back.

### Project scripts and ports (6.8)
1. A project's scripts live in the settings (`projects.<id>.scripts`), edited in the settings dialog's Projects section (a project Select; setup and archive text areas; run scripts as name + command rows with an Add form). Setup and archive are never read from a repository, so a cloned repository cannot run code on its own; run scripts may also come from it (item 6), and nothing from it runs without a click.
2. Setup: after `worktree_created`, the new worktree dialog opens a terminal in the worktree and types the setup script + Enter into it (`openWith` in `src/terminals.ts`, the mechanism "Start claude" uses), before the dialog's own terminal. Run: the worktree's context menu and the palette have "Run: <name>" per script of the worktree (`scripts`, asked for when the menu or the palette opens; the project's run scripts until it arrives), and the right panel's Scripts tab (23.4, `src/shell/ScriptsView.tsx`) lists them by source with their command, the worktree's port block, and the scripts started from Hive that still run (their terminal open and not exited, not stopped there; "Show the terminal", and "Stop", which types Ctrl+C); its empty state and footer open Settings on the project's scripts (`openModal("settings", project)`). Each opens a terminal there and types the command + Enter (`runScript` in `src/scripts.ts`, which remembers the terminal as `scriptRuns`, UI state). From then on Hive only observes.
3. Ports: the first time a process needs them, a worktree gets a block of 10 ports, the lowest free one of 20000–29999 (below Linux's ephemeral range), kept by worktree path in `<data>/hive/ports.json` (0600, temporary file + rename) so it stays the same across restarts. Blocks of worktrees whose folder is gone are taken back when a new block is given. A file that cannot be read, or a block outside the range, counts as none.
4. Every terminal whose `cwd` lies in a followed worktree gets `HIVE_PORT` (the first port of its block; left out when no block can be saved, with a warning on stderr), `HIVE_WORKTREE_PATH` and `HIVE_ROOT_PATH` (the project's main worktree), as environment entries; the same port also as `PORT` (D22.14), unless the service's own environment has a non-empty `PORT`, which the process then keeps (a `PORT` the user's shell config exports wins in a terminal anyway, set after). The archive script gets the same entries.
5. Archive: `remove_worktree` first checks the worktree (linked, of a followed project; unless `force`, not in use), then the service runs the project's archive script, if any, as `sh -c <script>` in the worktree with the `HIVE_*` entries, stdin from `/dev/null`, stdout and stderr together, in its own process group (on native Windows: see [Native Windows processes](#native-windows-processes-1256a)). After 60 s the group is killed. A failure (exit code not 0, a signal, the time limit) cancels the removal with `remove_worktree_failed` whose message ends with the last 4 KiB of the output ("the archive script failed (exit status: 4):\n…"); with `force` the removal goes on anyway. Once the script ends, whatever it left in its process group is killed (its worktree is about to go); the removal waits at most 0.5 s more for output held by a process that left the group.
6. The repository's scripts (23.4, `hive::scripts::repo`, the human's decision of 2026-11-03 replacing part of #49): from the worktree's root, `package.json`'s `scripts` with a string value (run with the package manager of the first lockfile found: `bun.lock`, `bun.lockb` → `bun`, `pnpm-lock.yaml` → `pnpm`, `yarn.lock` → `yarn`, `package-lock.json` or none → `npm`; `<manager> run <name>`), the tasks of `Taskfile.yml`, else `Taskfile.yaml` (Task; not its `internal: true` ones; `task <name>`), and the targets of the first of `GNUmakefile`, `makefile`, `Makefile` (as `make` looks for them; its `.PHONY` targets when it declares any, else every explicit target; special `.X` targets, patterns, variables, recipes and directives are not; `make <name>`), each in the file's order. Each file's name is matched as the folder lists it (at most 100 000 of the root's entries looked at); a Makefile's `.PHONY` names are a set, so parsing stays linear. Untrusted input: a file is read only when it is a regular file (a link is refused, also one swapped in: opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`, a FIFO with `O_NONBLOCK`; on Windows the link itself is opened and refused), at most 512 KiB; at most 100 scripts of a file; a name is kept only when it can be typed as one word in every shell (letters, digits and `_ - . : + @ /`, starting with a letter, a digit or `_`, at most 100 bytes); each problem is one line of `problems`. The command is built from fixed words and the name, never taken from the file. A settings run script replaces a repository script of the same name. The service remembers the last `scripts` it answered (`State::scripted`, forgotten when an app connects): after every re-list of the watched worktree, when it is that one, and after `set_settings`, it reads them again and sends them when they differ (a worktree no longer followed is forgotten, with nothing sent).
7. The dev server's Preview (24.4): the Scripts tab's ports strip has a Preview toggle (`aria-pressed`) that shows, instead of the scripts, `http://localhost:<port>/` in an `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin allow-forms">` (its own origin, its cookies and API; no top navigation, no popup) under a bar with the port (a `Select` of the block's ten, `$PORT` first) and a reload button (a new frame). The app's CSP has `http://localhost:*` in `frame-src`, and the navigation guard admits a frame at `http://localhost` only at a port of 20000–29999 (`dev_server_url`); it is never an IPC origin (24.14). On macOS (`frame_navigation`) a dev server loads only in a frame already at one, or on its first load (empty or `about:blank`) asked by the app itself (at `app_url`), never in the main frame, from a scheme frame or from a blank frame a page made.

### Files panel watch (3.1, #31)
1. `followPanel` (`src/follow.ts`) keeps the service watching `panelWorktree`: while the files panel is open, the selected worktree (a selected project is its main worktree, which has the same id) or the selected agent's worktree. It sends `watch_worktree` when that changes, `unwatch_worktree` when the panel closes, and `watch_worktree` again after a new `welcome` (a new service has no watch). It also sends `expand_ignored` with the listing's ignored folders (`dir/`) the tree has open, whenever that set changes and after every new watch (which starts with none open). The store keeps the last `files` as `worktreeFiles` (`{path, files, ignored, truncated}`).
