The panel's "All" mode shows it when `path` matches, each file with its status from `changes` and the changed files it no longer lists (deleted ones) merged in, and the ignored entries dimmed (`data-ignored`; `allFiles` and `fileTree` in `src/shell/RightPanel.tsx`, presentation only); a truncated list says so. "Names" searches `files` only (4.10 stays on tracked and untracked files). An open file's tab closes when a new listing no longer has it, in `files` or `ignored`, unless an ignored folder of the listing holds it (it may only have been closed). "Changed", or "All" before the list arrives, shows only `changes`.
2. The service checks the path against the followed projects' worktrees, stops the previous watch task, and starts one (`hive::files::Watcher`, git on a blocking thread). The worktree's git dir comes from `git rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` (a linked worktree's is under the main repository's `.git/worktrees/<name>`).
3. Listing: `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard -z` (at most 32 MiB read; names that are not UTF-8 and nested repositories `dir/` skipped; duplicates from conflicts merged; sorted; capped). Ignored entries (14.2) come from git too: `git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-standard --directory -z`, files and folders (`dir/`, without the files git also names under a folder that holds only ignored ones). Each folder the app sent in `expand_ignored` is listed, parents first, while it is a `dir/` entry of the listing so far (a top-level one from git, or a folder found in an open one): one level (at most 50 001 entries), its folders as `dir/sub/`. Never through a link, race included (15.2): on Unix each part of the path is opened from the worktree's root with `openat` and `O_NOFOLLOW | O_DIRECTORY` and the folder is listed from that descriptor, each entry's type from `fstatat` without following it (a link inside is listed as a file); a link in any part fails the open, and the folder is neither read nor watched. On Windows (no `openat`) the folder's canonical path must be the path itself, checked before the read. Git names a folder only when it tracks nothing inside it, so all it holds is ignored. Once the listing holds more than the cap, no further folder is read. Files and ignored entries share the caps, files first. Every git command Hive runs has `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, so reading never rewrites the index the watcher sees.
4. What counts: the directory of every listed file, every untracked directory (`git ls-files --others --directory`, so a new empty folder counts), every open ignored folder that was listed, at most 8192; never inside a closed ignored folder such as `node_modules` or `target`; closing one stops it counting. How it is watched (`files::Strategy`, 17.6): on Linux and Windows (`Folders`) each of those directories gets a non-recursive watch (an inotify watch costs no descriptor; a recursive one would walk the ignored trees). On macOS (`Root`) one recursive FSEvents watch covers the worktree (a watched path holds a descriptor on itself and on every folder above it, so a watch per directory ran out of descriptors); FSEvents never walks the tree nor follows a link in it. A `Filter`, shared with the watcher's thread and replaced at each re-list before any watch moves, drops every other event before it is queued: a change counts in a counted directory, to what one holds directly, anywhere under an untracked directory, and nowhere outside the worktree. Open ignored folders get a watch of their own under both strategies (on macOS the worktree's covers it, so it adds no descriptor). A watch takes a path, never a descriptor: it is made without following a link at the end of the path (inotify), and a newly watched open folder is opened again as for its listing and compared (device and inode) with the one read; a folder swapped for a link in between loses its new watches at once, by the key the watcher keeps (FSEvents keeps what the path resolved to). In the git dir only `HEAD` and `index` count. Each re-list removes the watches of directories no longer listed before adding new ones (a renamed directory keeps its inotify watch, which must not be reused); a re-list that moves no watch leaves the watcher alone (FSEvents restarts its stream for any batch).
5. Any other event, a queue overflow or a rescan (FSEvents' `MustScanSubDirs`, events dropped) included, starts a burst; 200 ms after its last event (at most 1 s after its first) the worktree is listed again from scratch. `State::worktree_changed` (`hive::daemon`) is the single point that reports the change: it sends `files` when the list differs from the last one sent, then the worktree's `changes` every time (an edit changes the diff, not the list). An `expand_ignored` ends the wait the same way (the watch task follows the open folders through a `tokio::sync::watch` channel), so a burst it cuts short is still covered by the re-list.
6. Known limit (`Folders` only): in a chain of new empty untracked directories (`mkdir -p a/b/c`) only the top one is watched until a file appears; a file created deep inside it later shows up on the next re-list. Under `Root` a change anywhere below it counts.

### Changes
The files panel (screen 1g, "Árvore de arquivos com diff do git").
1. The panel shows the selected worktree (a selected project is its main worktree), the selected agent's worktree, or else the shown terminal's (`panelWorktree` in `src/store.ts`). When it opens and whenever that worktree or its base changes it sends `list_changes {path, base}`; the answer is stored by path in `changes`.
1. Base (9.11): a menu in the summary line (since 27.2, item 6; it was a HEAD | `<branch>` toggle) picks what the worktree is compared with, among others: `HEAD` (what is not committed yet) or `branch`, the merge-base of its `HEAD` and the main worktree's branch (the branch 6.6 counts ahead/behind against, `health::branch`), so an agent's commits stay in view. The pick is UI state (`diffBases` by worktree path, this run only, not remembered); the default is `branch` in a Claude worktree and `HEAD` anywhere else (`diffBase` in `src/store.ts`). The toggle shows when the answer has a `branch` or a `base_error`; with a `base_error` the list is against `HEAD` and the branch button is disabled, its tooltip saying why. The main worktree has no toggle. `followPanel` sends `watch_worktree` again when the base changes, and `followOpenFile` asks for the open file again.
2. The service refuses a path that is not a worktree of a followed project, then runs, on a blocking thread: `git status --porcelain=v2 -z --untracked-files=all --find-renames` and `git diff --numstat -z --find-renames --no-ext-diff --no-textconv <base> --`. Against `HEAD` the base is `HEAD`, or the empty tree (`git hash-object -t tree /dev/null`) before the first commit, so staged, unstaged and untracked changes all count, as `git status` shows them. For `branch` it is `git merge-base HEAD refs/heads/<main branch>` (exit 1, no output: no common commit), and the files are `git diff --raw -z --find-renames <merge-base> --` (committed since it, staged or not: `A` added, `D` deleted, `R` renamed with its source, anything else modified) plus the untracked entries of `git status`: one git run more than against `HEAD`, since the merge-base replaces `rev-parse HEAD`. Without a main branch (the main worktree itself, or a detached one), without a common commit or when `merge-base` fails, it lists against `HEAD` and says why in `base_error`. A detached `HEAD` in the worktree still has a merge-base. The refresh of the watched worktree lists the projects once for both its `changes` and its status, and its status's `changes` count is the entries of the `git status` just run (9.14), not a second one.
3. Status per file against `HEAD`: `?` untracked; `2` (rename) renamed with its source; `1` added when the index says `A`, deleted when either side says `D`, else modified; `u` (unmerged) modified; ignored entries skipped. Line counts come from numstat (`-` = binary = null). An untracked file is counted here: a regular file of at most 8 MiB with no NUL in its first 8000 bytes (git's binary test) has as many lines as newlines, plus one for an unterminated last line; anything else is null. One listing reads at most 32 MiB of untracked files (9.14, default pending the human's review): once a file does not fit in what is left, it and every file after it are null.
4. Limits: each git command's output is read up to 16 MiB (more is an error, and git stops on the broken pipe); file names are kept as bytes to read the file and sent as lossy UTF-8. The files go into the message until their JSON reaches 3 MiB, well under `MAX_PAYLOAD`; the rest are left out with `error` "too many changes: N files not shown", while the totals still count every file.
5. The UI groups the paths into folders (presentation), folders first; a folder shows the strongest status inside (deleted > added > renamed > modified), and a collapsed one a dot of that color (`collapsed["folder:<worktree>/<path>"]`). Letters: M, A (added and untracked), D (struck through), R. The tree is virtualized and is one focusable element: ↑/↓ move, ←/→ collapse and expand, Enter opens a file or toggles a folder. Opening a file sets `openFile {worktree, path}`; `FileView` (in `src/shell/RightPanel.tsx`) shows its header under the tree and, below it, the file (see [Viewer and diff](#viewer-and-diff-33-31)). "All" / "Changed" is `changedOnly`; until the full file list exists (3.1) both show the changed files.
6. More bases (27.2, `docs/spike/diff-bases.md`; the human's answers of 2026-11-26). The summary's base menu (`BaseMenu` in `src/shell/RightPanel.tsx`) lists HEAD, the main worktree's `<branch>` (disabled, with `base_error` as its tooltip, when it fell back), one "Since <agent>'s last turn" per entry of `changes.turns` (a late one says "may miss its first edit") and "A commit…", which sends `list_commits` and opens a searchable dialog (`CommitPicker`: filter on subject, short id and author; ↑/↓, Enter, Esc) of the `commits` answer, the branch's commits under "This branch" first. The trigger names the base shown. The pick stays UI state (`diffBases`), not remembered.
   - **A commit:** `{"commit":{"id"}}`, accepted only as 40 or 64 hex digits and checked with `git rev-parse --verify --quiet --end-of-options <id>^{commit}`; then listed exactly as the branch base, from that commit. A commit that is gone (a rebase) falls back to `HEAD` with `base_error`. `list_commits`: `git log -z --format=%H%x00%h%x00%an%x00%ct%x00%s --max-count=501 HEAD --`, and for `on_branch` `git rev-list --max-count=501 <merge-base>..HEAD --` (the branch base's merge-base; none in the main worktree).
   - **Snapshots (`hive::snapshot`):** at each `UserPromptSubmit` of an agent of Hive's terminals (not a subagent's: those run inside their parent's turn; one in its own worktree has the branch base), in the worktree the agent is in, on a blocking thread off the hook's path (`hive hook` still answers at once): `git add -A` then `git write-tree` with `GIT_INDEX_FILE` = the worktree's snapshot index in Hive's folder (seeded from a copy of the user's index, `git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-path index`, a read) and `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` = Hive's store for the repository (`<data>/snapshots/<FNV-1a of the common dir>/store.git`, a bare repository made by `git init --bare --template=` with the repository's object format, whose `objects/info/alternates` names the repository's `objects`); then `git update-ref refs/turns/<session>/<n> <tree>` there and `turns.json` (0600, at most 1 MiB) with `{session, n, tree, worktree, started_ms, late}`; a turn read back whose session is not a valid id or whose tree is not 40 or 64 hex digits is ignored, and a `turns.json` that cannot be read makes the store start again (its refs would keep objects no turn names). Untracked files go in (large ones too), ignored ones stay out. Every git call of it, and of a listing since a turn, runs with `core.hooksPath` = an empty folder of Hive's, `core.splitIndex=false`, fsmonitor off, and every configured filter driver emptied (`-c filter.<name>.clean= -c filter.<name>.process= -c filter.<name>.required=false`, the names from `git config --name-only --get-regexp ^filter\.`; a name with `=` refuses the snapshot), with a 60 s limit. Since `git add -A` runs `git status` inside each checked-out gitlink of the snapshot index (a submodule, an embedded repository), and so on down, the filter drivers of those repositories are emptied too (their gitlinks from `git ls-files --stage -z`, at most 256 nested repositories, else no snapshot); the `-c` options reach them through `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`. Nothing is created, moved or changed inside `.git` (#56 as the human read it, 2026-11-26: git refreshing the time of an object it meets again is accepted). Session ids are used only when `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`. A `PostToolUse` of `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `Write` or `NotebookEdit` of the session before the tree is written marks the snapshot `late`. Kept: the last 10 turns of a session, at most 7 days, dropped at its `SessionEnd`; a turn of a worktree that is gone is dropped too; past 1 GiB of objects in a store, its oldest snapshots go (one larger than the cap alone is not kept). Dropping deletes the refs (`update-ref --stdin`) and prunes (`git prune --expire=now`, also at most once a minute after a snapshot), which removes the snapshot indexes too (they are seeded again); a store left without turns, or whose repository is gone, is deleted. At start the service sweeps the stores by age. A snapshot that ends updates the watched worktree's `changes`. One `changes` answer reads the kept turns once, for both its base and its `turns`, so a snapshot that ends meanwhile never shows in `turns` with the base fallen back to `head`; a turn dropped after that read (its `SessionEnd`) is checked again once the listing holds the store's lock: it answers `head` with "No snapshot of this agent's last turn in this worktree" and is left out of `turns`.
   - **Since a turn:** `{"turn":{"session"}}`: the session's last kept turn, when it is of this worktree. Under the store's lock: `git add -A` into the worktree's snapshot index (warm), then `git diff-index --cached -z --raw --find-renames <tree>` and `git diff-index --cached -z --numstat --find-renames --no-ext-diff --no-textconv <tree>`, with the same environment; statuses as for a commit (`A`, `D`, `R`, else modified; a file new since the turn is `added`), every line count from numstat. The objects a listing writes go at the next prune, or at once when the listing leaves the store past its cap. No turn, or a listing that fails (a blob the user's `git gc` removed), falls back to `HEAD` with `base_error`. The diff is the worktree's, so what another agent or the user changed since that moment shows too.


### Seen files (27.1)
1. The service keeps, per worktree and diff base (`head`, `branch`, and since 27.2 `turn:<session>` and `commit:<id>`: the base the list was made against, after any fallback), each marked file's diff identity: `<base side> <disk side>`, the base side its blob id in `git diff --raw -z --no-abbrev --find-renames <base> --` (at an agent's turn, in the `git diff-index --cached --raw` of its snapshot, [Changes](#changes) item 6) (a rename's source blob; `-` when not there: added, untracked), the disk side `git hash-object --no-filters --stdin-paths` of a regular file of at most 32 MiB (names on stdin, within git's 10 s limit), `link:<target>` for a symlink (never followed: a link to a FIFO would block git), `stat:<size>:<mtime ns>` for a larger file or a name `--stdin-paths` would read otherwise (a line break, or a leading `"`, which git C-unquotes), `-` when missing. A file is `seen` while its identity is the marked one, else `changed` (also when git cannot tell): unseen again, with a dot "Changed since seen". Only files with a mark are hashed, and only when the worktree has marks for that base.
2. `mark_seen` lists the changes as `list_changes` does, marks the asked paths that are in the list (others ignored) or unmarks them, drops the marks of files no longer in the list, saves, and answers `changes`. `changes` (asked, or after a refresh of the watched worktree) carries each file's `seen`; the 3 MiB budget counts room for it.
3. Store (`hive::worktree_store`, generic, for 27.3 next): `<data>/seen.json` (`{<worktree path>: {<base>: {<path>: <identity>}}}`) in Hive's folder, never inside `.git` (#56), written atomically with mode `0600`, compact JSON of at most 1 MiB: a save over it is refused (`error` "Cannot mark the files as seen: seen.json would be over 1048576 bytes") and a file over it reads as empty. Each save drops the worktrees whose folder is gone and any left empty. A worktree's marks go when Hive removes it (`remove_worktree`) or its project (`remove_project`); one removed outside Hive goes at the next save. `hive uninstall` counts `seen.json` as data.
4. UI (`src/shell/RightPanel.tsx`): in the Diff view, each file row ends with its box (`Seen: <path>`, out of the tab order: the tree has the keys; a click or double click on it opens nothing), with the dot before it when `changed`; the left end of the row stays free. The summary's second line says "k of N seen" (a `changed` file is unseen) and has the "Unseen only" toggle (`unseenOnly`, UI state, this run only: "Every changed file is seen." when it hides them all). The diff's header ends with a "Seen" box for an open file among the changes. The palette's "Mark all as seen" (while a file of the shown worktree is unseen) sends every listed path.

### Review comments (27.3)
1. The service keeps a worktree's comments (`hive::comments`): file, lines, text (`body`), the text of its lines and of up to 3 lines on each side, the author, the time, resolved or not, outdated or not, and the line hashes of its lines and context (`hive::anchor`). Per file with a comment placed on it, it also keeps the file's line hashes when its comments were last placed (only for files of at most 10 000 lines).
2. Every `list_comments`, every change to them and every refresh of the watched worktree (when it has comments) first reads each commented file (as `open_file` checks it: relative, inside the worktree through links, a regular file; at most 2 MiB and 50 000 lines) and moves its comments: a file whose hashes are unchanged is left alone; otherwise the kept hashes are diffed against the file's (bounded Myers), a comment whose lines all stayed together moves with them, and any other one is looked for by its exact lines, the place with the most matching context lines first, then the nearest to where it was (`anchor::relocate`, which never looks for a run of fewer than 3 lines none of which has 8 non-blank characters, like a lone `}`). Not found: `outdated`, still listed at its last place with its last text, and looked for again by its text at each later move (it comes back with a revert). A file deleted outdates its comments; one that cannot be read (too large, not a regular file) leaves them as they were. A file longer than 10 000 lines has no kept hashes: a comment stays while its lines are where they were, else it is looked for by its text.
3. Limits: at most 500 comments a worktree, 4000 characters of text (trimmed, not empty), 1000 lines a comment. Store (`hive::worktree_store`): `<data>/comments.json` (`{<worktree>: {next, comments, files}}`), never inside `.git` (#56), atomic, `0600`, at most 4 MiB (a change over it is refused with its `error`; moves that cannot be saved are still answered, and found again next time). A worktree's comments go with `remove_worktree` and `remove_project`, or at the next save once its folder is gone; ids start again once a worktree has none. `hive uninstall` counts `comments.json` as data.
4. A remote's `comments` (26.19): at most 1000 comments; `code`, `before`, `after` and `author` cut at 1024 characters and `body` at 4096.
5. UI (`src/viewer/review.tsx`, the view in `src/shell/RightPanel.tsx`): "Comment" in the file view or Ctrl+Shift+M (6.7) sends `add_comment` for the selected lines; open comments that are not outdated are marked on their lines in the viewer. The right panel's Comments view lists the shown worktree's comments under Open, Outdated and Resolved, each with its file and lines (a click opens the file there), its code, author and time; Send (one) and "Send all open" paste them into an agent's terminal of the worktree (28.21: only a live agent's, never a plain terminal; with several a menu by the button picks one, the last pick per worktree remembered in `localStorage`, `src/viewer/target.ts`, `SendButton.tsx`) as 6.7 did (`@file#Lx-y — text`, bracketed paste, no Enter), Resolve / Reopen and Delete. The palette's "Send open comments" sends every open comment of the shown worktree that is not outdated.

### Viewer and diff (3.3, #31)
1. `followOpenFile` (`src/follow.ts`) sends `open_file {worktree, path, base}` when `openFile` is set, again whenever a new `changes` for its worktree arrives (the file may have changed with it) or its base changes, and after a new `welcome`. The store keeps the last answer as `file`; `FileView` shows it only while it is the open file.
2. The service checks the worktree against the followed projects and the path on a blocking thread: not empty, at most 4096 bytes, only normal components (no `/` start, `.` or `..`); on disk it is resolved (`canonicalize`, symlinks followed) and must stay inside the worktree's resolved path and be a regular file (never a FIFO or device, which could block). A missing file has no `content`.
3. `base`: the base is resolved as for `list_changes` (the merge-base, a chosen commit, an agent's last turn, or `HEAD`); at a turn the blob comes from the snapshot's tree, read through Hive's store ([Changes](#changes) item 6), and a turn base that cannot be read falls back to `HEAD`. At `HEAD`: nothing before the first commit (`git rev-parse --verify --quiet HEAD`); else the blob of `git --literal-pathspecs ls-tree -l -z <base> -- <path>` (a tree or submodule entry counts as none); a path absent there that is a rename takes its old path's blob: against `HEAD` a staged rename (`git status --porcelain=v2 -z --untracked-files=no --find-renames`), against the merge-base a rename since it (`git diff --raw`, as the list). The blob is read with `git cat-file blob <id>`.
4. Limits: each side is read up to 1 MiB (`TEXT_LIMIT`; the blob size from `ls-tree` is checked first); over it, `too_large`. A side with a NUL in its first 8000 bytes (the rule of [Changes](#changes)) or that is not UTF-8 makes the file `binary`. Both leave both texts null. If the JSON of the answer would still exceed `MAX_PAYLOAD` (escaping can double text), it is sent as `too_large` without texts. Both sides missing is the error "<path> does not exist".
5. `version` = `<byte length>-<64-bit SipHash of the bytes, hex>` (`hive::file::version`, Rust's `DefaultHasher`, stable within one build of the service). A save recomputes it from the file on disk and compares for equality (see [Editing](#editing-35-31)).
6. UI (`src/viewer/`): `CodeView` creates one CodeMirror `EditorView` per open file (keyed by worktree and path), outside React state (#30), through `createViewer` (`editor.ts`), and calls `show({content, original})` with each answer: a new read-only state that keeps the scroll position. A file among the worktree's `changes` shows as `unifiedMergeView` (`@codemirror/merge`, no merge controls, unchanged stretches over 6 lines collapsed to a margin of 3) with `original` = `base` (a deleted file: empty content, all removed; a new one: empty original, all added); any other file shows its text under "No changes in this file.". `binary`, `too_large` and `error` show a message instead ("Binary file not shown.", "File too large to show.", the error). The language comes from the file name through `@codemirror/language-data`, loaded on demand; the look is One Dark with the prototype's surfaces and diff colors.
7. Tables (23.6): a `.csv` or `.tsv` file (`isTable` in `src/store.ts`, by its name) opens as its table; the header's table button (`aria-pressed`) shows its text instead (`asText` in its tab), and back (with unsaved edits, the table shows them: [Editing](#editing-35-31), item 12); Edit shows the text. While the table shows, `followOpenTable` (`src/follow.ts`) sends `open_table {worktree, path}` when it shows, whenever a new `changes` for its worktree arrives and after a new `welcome`; the store keeps the last answer as `table`. The service (`hive::table`, blocking thread) checks the path as `open_file` does and reads the file with the `csv` crate (no header row assumed, rows of any length, RFC 4180 quoting for both, a tab as a TSV's delimiter, each cell lossy UTF-8 so a file in another encoding shows U+FFFD where it is not UTF-8, a UTF-8 BOM dropped): the first 10 000 rows, fewer when their JSON would not fit in one frame with the rest of the answer, and every row counted over at most 64 MiB of the file (past it `partial`); a row over 1 MiB ends the reading before it, also `partial`, and a row whose least JSON (its bytes, two quotes and a comma a cell, its brackets) would not fit is never decoded, so a hostile file costs the service a few MiB at most. The file's size does not matter: one over `TEXT_LIMIT`, or not UTF-8 (`binary` for the text view), still shows its first rows. `TableView` (`src/viewer/TableView.tsx`) shows the first row as the header, kept in view, a column of row numbers, every row padded to the longest one's cells, each column as wide as its longest cell (3–40 characters; a longer cell is cut, its title whole), only the rows in view rendered (`@tanstack/react-virtual`, a fixed 24 px row), "Showing 10,000 of 25,000 rows" ("of more than N" when `partial`) under it when rows were left out, and a tab per sheet when there are several (23.7). Workbooks (23.7): `.xlsx`, `.xls` and `.ods` are table files too (`isWorkbook`), always shown as their table (`tableShown` ignores `asText`; no table button, since a binary file has no text to switch to), read by `hive::workbook`; a broken one shows the service's error in place of the table.

8. Images and PDFs (24.2, 24.3): the service reads the type of the file on disk from its first 4096 bytes, never its name (`hive::file::media_type`: PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, PDF by their signatures; SVG when its first element, past a BOM, white space, the XML declaration, comments and a doctype, is `<svg`), and an image of at most 20 MiB (`SERVE_LIMIT`) or a PDF of at most 100 MiB (`PDF_LIMIT`) gets `media {mime, token, version}` in its `file` answer, whatever `binary` and `too_large` say; over the cap it shows the text view's notice and "Open in external editor" opens it. `token` names the worktree for the whole service run (`Projects::token`: 128 random bits from `RandomState`, issued only for a followed worktree, checked followed again on every read), so a page shown through the scheme can never name another worktree by its path; `version` is the file's size and modification time in nanoseconds (`stamp`; hashing up to 100 MiB on every read would cost too much). The Tauri pump adds `media.url`: `hive-file://localhost/<token>/<path>?v=<version>` on macOS, `http://hive-file.localhost/…` on Windows, every part percent-encoded but the unreserved characters (`hive_lib::scheme`), so a changed file gets a new URL. `main.rs` registers the `hive-file` scheme (`register_asynchronous_uri_scheme_protocol`); its handler (`Hive::serve_file`) decodes the token and the path and pulls the whole file with `read_file` in reads of 1 MiB, at most 101 (the PDF cap), each answered by a `file_range` and a data frame (raw bytes, no base64); a read whose `version` differs from the first one's, or that returns nothing before `size`, fails the answer ("<path> changed while it was read"). The answer is `200` with the service's type and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, or `404` with the error as text; a `Range` request still gets the whole file. Every answer has its type's CSP (`scheme::policy`, the one place 24.4 adds HTML's): a PDF `default-src 'none'` (a sandbox would blank the WebView's viewer); anything else, the 404 included, `default-src 'none'; img-src data:; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; sandbox`, so a file shown as a document (an SVG a PDF links to, opened in its frame) runs no script and reaches nothing: in the scheme's origin it could read the token from its URL and fetch the worktree's files. Known limit: the navigation guard cannot tell frames apart (Tauri's `on_navigation` gets only the URL), so the main frame may also navigate to the scheme, replacing the UI with the file (IPC stays refused, 24.14). The app's CSP admits the scheme in `img-src` and `frame-src`, and the navigation guard lets a frame load it (`scheme::file_url`, the scheme's own origin only); it is never an IPC origin (`ipc_from_app`, 24.14). The viewer (`MediaView`, `src/viewer/MediaView.tsx`) shows `media` instead of the text or the diff: an image as an `<img>` (an SVG too, so its scripts never run), fitted to the view; a PDF in a plain `<iframe>`, not sandboxed (WebKit draws no PDF in a sandboxed frame; the human, 24.1), drawn by the WebView's own viewer. Edit still shows an SVG's text. The browser mock answers a `.png` and a `.pdf` with `data:` URLs.

9. HTML pages (24.4): no bytes tell a page, a style sheet or a script apart, so the scheme serves them by name when the bytes are no image or PDF (`hive::file::text_type`): `.html` / `.htm` as `text/html; charset=utf-8` (`hive_protocol::HTML_TYPE`), `.css` as `text/css`, `.js` / `.mjs` as `text/javascript` (UTF-8), each under `SERVE_LIMIT` (20 MiB); anything else stays `application/octet-stream`, which `nosniff` keeps from running as a script or style sheet. Only a page gets `media` in its `file` answer, and keeps its text: the viewer shows the text, and the header's eye button ("Preview the page", `aria-pressed`, the tab's `rendered` as Markdown's) swaps it for `PagePreview`, an `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts">` without `allow-same-origin` (origin `null`) of `media.url`, so its relative files load through the scheme from the file on disk (unsaved edits are not in it). A page's answer has its own CSP (`scheme::policy`, exact type only, the human at 24.1): `default-src 'none'`; scripts and styles from the scheme and inline (`'unsafe-inline'`); images from the scheme and `data:`, fonts from the scheme, the scheme being this platform's origin only (`hive-file:` on macOS, where `http://hive-file.localhost` is a loopback host that would carry a request out; `http://hive-file.localhost` on Windows); `connect-src 'none'`, `form-action 'none'` and `sandbox allow-scripts`, so even loaded in the main frame it runs in origin `null`. Its scripts thus never fetch (not the network, not the worktree's files), never post a form, never navigate the app or open a window, and reach no IPC (24.14); a file it loads as a script or style sheet runs as one, a cross-origin image is never readable. A module script (`type="module"`) does not load: the scheme sends no CORS header. Since a page's scripts can ask for many files, at most `SERVING` (4) scheme answers are read at once (`Hive::serving`, a semaphore) and `QUEUED` (32) more wait (`Hive::waiting`); past them a request is answered `503` at once (WebKit never cancels a scheme request, so an endless queue would stall every image), and a `read_file` the service does not answer within 10 s (`READ_WAIT`) fails and is forgotten. On macOS two more guards (`main.rs`'s `with_webview`): WebRTC is off for the whole webview (`macos::disable_peer_connections`, WebKit's private `_peerConnectionEnabled` preference: no CSP covers `RTCPeerConnection`, whose STUN packets a sandboxed frame did send in a real `WKWebView`; Hive has no use for it), and frames' navigations are checked by frame (`macos::guard_frames` replaces wry's `webView:decidePolicyForNavigationAction:decisionHandler:`, then hands what it admits to wry's, which still asks the navigation guard): `frame_navigation` keeps a frame now at the scheme, or a navigation a scheme frame asks for, in the scheme, so a page cannot move its frame to a dev server serving the worktree and run there without the scheme's CSP. Windows (D17.0, not done): wry checks only the main frame's navigations there (frames need `FrameNavigationStarting`), WebRTC stays on (a WebView2 switch), and init scripts reach subframes. The browser mock gives a `.html` its text and the Windows scheme URL under the token `mock`, which `e2e/html-preview.e2e.ts` serves with routes and the CSP read from `scheme.rs`.

### Editing (3.5, #31)
1. Which views are editable: a file without changes opens (from the tree) as editable text; a changed file opens as its read-only diff (#31: the diff stays read-only) and the header's "Edit" switches it to editable text, "Diff" back (disabled while there are unsaved edits). No LSP.
2. The buffer (`src/viewer/buffer.ts`, pure; kept in the store as `edit`, so it survives the panel closing) holds the text being edited, the text on disk it is based on and its `version`. The CodeMirror view (`createEditor` in `editor.ts`) is created once per open file outside React state (#30); it splits lines on "\n" only, so a "\r" is text and saves unchanged. Undo, the standard keys and Tab indenting come from `@codemirror/commands` (Esc, then Tab, leaves the editor).
3. Save: Ctrl+S while the editor has the focus (a CodeMirror key, so the terminal never loses it and no app-wide shortcut is taken, #35) or the header's Save sends `save_file` with the buffer's base `version`; one save at a time. A save the app cannot send (not connected, over the frame limit) fails at once with the reason. `file_saved` makes the sent text the new base; the header's dot marks unsaved edits.
4. The service (blocking thread) checks the worktree and the path as `open_file` does. An existing file is written through its resolved path (a symlink inside the worktree stays a symlink); a new one only in a folder that resolves inside the worktree. `content` over 1 MiB is `too_large`. The text goes to `.<name>.hive-<pid>-<n>.tmp` in the same folder (created 0600, then given the file's permission bits, 0644 for a new file), is synced, the version on disk is checked, and the temporary file is renamed over the file; the folder is then synced. Any failure removes the temporary file. Known limit: a write between the version check and the rename is lost (agents take no lock, so none would help).
5. Every new `file` answer for the open file (see [Viewer and diff](#viewer-and-diff-33-31)) updates the buffer: text equal to the buffer's makes it clean at that version; text equal to its base changes nothing (e.g. a new service build with new version tokens); a clean buffer reloads (keeping scroll and selection); a dirty one keeps the edits and shows the conflict banner "Changed on disk." (or "Deleted on disk."): Reload takes the disk's text; Keep mine takes the disk's version as the base, so the next save overwrites it (a deleted file is created again); View diff shows the edits against the disk as a read-only unified diff. A `save_failed` conflict asks for the file again, which brings the banner.
6. File tabs (8.21): every opened file has its own tab (`openFiles` in the store); `openFile` is the one whose view shows, with its live `editing` / `edit`, and switching tabs puts them back into the file's tab, with its view's selection and scroll (`snapshot` / `restoreView` in `editor.ts`, taken when the view goes). Closing a tab with unsaved edits asks through the Hive dialog (`askDiscard`, 8.20); a file renamed or moved (`file_renamed`) keeps its tab; a file that a new listing of its worktree no longer holds (deleted) closes its tab, asking first when it has unsaved edits: in one question, which grows with the files deleted while it is open, and goes back to the dialog it was asked over. The tab bar mixes terminals and files in `tabOrder` (`barItems`): the order they opened, as dragged (`useReorder`, `moveTab`), kept in `localStorage` under `hive.tabOrder` by `barKey` (a file by worktree and path, a terminal by its Claude session while one runs, else by its channel, which is not kept for the next run). Split (Ctrl+Shift+D), closing a tab and selecting a place follow that order. Split pairs (6.11, 20.5, 25.1): `splits` in the store, any two tabs of a worktree as `PaneId`s (a terminal's id, a file's `fileKey`), a tab in one pair at most (`splitOf`); showing either tab shows its pair (`shownSplit`, `shownPanes`). The focused file pane is the open file, with the live `editing` / `edit` / `file` / `table`; a file pane without the focus reads what its tab keeps (`paneState`, `FileTab.text`, `FileTab.table`) and takes the focus on a pointer down or focus in it (`focusFilePane`); `asideFile` is what the followers ask for beside the open file, and its answers go to its tab (`reduce`). A preview tab entering a pair is kept. A pair's tabs sit side by side in the bar, left first (`setSplit` moves the right one there, `moveTab` moves a pair together), drawn as one group (`data-pair`, `data-split` view / paired). A bar tab dropped on a pane takes it, on a tab alone makes a pair on that side (`dropOnPane`; `replacePane`, `swapSides` back the menus). Pairs are not kept across a reload. Known limit: closing the app does not ask.
7. Change markers (14.4, `src/viewer/gutterMarkers.ts`): the editor marks the buffer's lines added, modified and deleted against the `base` of the file's last `file` answer (`HEAD`, or the merge-base the Changes panel compares with), in their own gutter left of the line numbers, coloured with the `--git-added`, `--git-modified` and `--git-deleted` tokens; a deletion is a short bar on the top edge of the line the removed lines sat before. `Chunk.build` / `Chunk.updateB` (`@codemirror/merge`) keep the chunks current as the buffer is typed, diffing whole lines (`lineDiff`, 25.7: a diff of characters is quadratic in the changed characters, seconds on the main thread for a large file of long lines); a new base (a commit, the merge-base moving) rebuilds them (`Editor.setBase`). No base (a new or untracked file, binary) draws none, and "View diff" hides them while it shows. Clicking a marker does nothing.
8. "Open in external editor", the file menu's "Open with Default App" and "Reveal in Finder" / "Show in Explorer", the worktree menu's "Reveal in Finder": `open_in_editor`; the service resolves the file as `open_file` does and runs `wslpath -w <path>` (separate arguments). With `reveal`, any file or folder inside the worktree is accepted; to open, only a regular file that is not executable (any exec bit), not a type the system would run, install or follow (`.app`, `.command`, `.bat`, `.exe`, `.js`, `.lnk`, `.ps1`, `.vbs`, `.webloc` and similar: a fixed list in `hive::file`), and not inside a bundle (`Foo.app/…`), so images and PDFs open in Preview (D19.1). `openTarget` (`src/viewer/external.ts`) opens the answer's path with the Windows default app for the file through the app command `open_path {path, reveal}` (9.10): the app side opens (or, `reveal`, shows in the Explorer or the Finder) only a `windows_path` the service just sent in `editor_target` or `session_located`, each once (the 16 newest wait), and never opens one `editor_target` sent with `reveal`, with the opener plugin's Rust API (to open on macOS, `hive_lib::mac_open`: `/usr/bin/open -- <path>`, then `open -t -- <path>`, the text editor, when that fails, as for a `.jsonl` no app claims; both failing reads "No app opens <name>", D21.1); the webview's capability has no opener permission at all: links go through the app command `open_url` (26.19, see [Remote hosts over ssh](#remote-hosts-over-ssh-2617) item 7). A session's working folder (`locate_session`, target `folder`: the untrusted `cwd` of its log) is checked as a worktree's folder is: a real folder, no name Windows would change, no bundle. Outside Tauri nothing opens and the file view (or, from a menu, a toast) says so.
9. New file and rename (7.4): a right click on the files tree (or the Menu key on its active row) opens its menu: "New File…" in the folder, the file's folder, or the root below the rows; "Rename…" on a folder or a file still on disk (9.1: "Rename folder", showing the folder it is in). The dialog (`src/shell/FileMenu.tsx`, `openFileDialog(target, kind)`) sends `create_file` / `rename_file` and shows `file_op_failed`'s message under the field.
10. Move and new folder (8.3): "New Folder…" in the same menu opens the dialog as `kind` `"folder"`, which sends `create_folder`; `folder_created` keeps the path in the store's `newFolders` (for the window's life) so the Files tree shows it while empty. A file or a folder of the Files tree (not a deleted file; not the Diff tree) is dragged with native HTML5 drag and drop (`useFileDrag` in `RightPanel.tsx`): dropped on a folder, on a file (its folder) or below the rows (the root), it sends `move_file`, unless that is its own folder; a folder is never dropped into itself or its own folders (no drop line there). The target folder is outlined, and a closed folder hovered for 600 ms (`HOVER_OPEN_MS`) opens (9.1); the drag closes it again when it leaves that folder (its row and the rows inside it), leaves the tree or ends without a drop, never closing a folder open before the drag; a drop opens its folder and that folder's parents so the moved entry shows. On `file_renamed`, the open tabs under the path, the open state of its folders (`collapsed`, both trees), `newFolders` and the bar's order follow, and the Files tree makes the moved entry its active row once listed (`movedRow`). The window turns Tauri's native drag and drop off (`dragDropEnabled: false`), which would take the WebView's HTML5 drag and drop (on Windows, and on macOS too: see [Terminals in the UI](#terminals-in-the-ui-xtermjs) item 4 for Finder drops on a terminal, 18.1). A drag nothing takes (a file or link from outside, dropped anywhere else) is refused on the window (`installShortcuts`), so the WebView never opens it in place of the app. `create_file`, `rename_file`, `move_file` and `create_folder` refuse any folder that resolves inside the repository's `.git` ("Hive does not change what is inside .git"): a file moved there could become a hook.
11. Delete (9.29): "Delete…" in the same menu (a folder or a file still on disk), or the Delete key on the tree's active row (also ⌘⌫ on macOS, D18.10c), asks through `ConfirmDialog` (`askDelete` in `FileMenu.tsx`, Cancel focused): the text names the entry, for a folder how many files of the tree's listing it holds, and says it moves to the Trash on macOS, elsewhere that nothing goes to a trash (git brings back tracked files; untracked and ignored ones are lost). Delete sends `delete_file`; `file_deleted` drops the entry from the listing and `newFolders` at once and closes the tabs under it (`closeDeleted` in `src/reduce.ts`, shared with a deletion seen in a new listing: the clean ones at once, those with unsaved edits after one "Discard changes?" question). One entry at a time.
12. A table edited in place (25.4): a CSV or TSV table whose sheet is whole (`rowsNote` null: not `partial`, every row sent) and whose file has text (`file` answered with `content`) can be edited. A cell is selected by a click (the arrows move it) and edited by a double click or Enter, in a text area over the rows below it (⇧Enter adds a line, Enter or leaving it keeps the text, Tab too and selects the next cell, Escape drops it; the row being edited stays rendered, `rangeExtractor`); its context menu (`ContextMenu`, also the Menu key at the selected cell) inserts and deletes rows and columns; an empty file offers "Add a row". Otherwise the table is read-only, with a note under it (`NOT_WHOLE`, or "this file has no text to edit"); a workbook says nothing and never edits. The first edit starts the buffer as Edit does (`editOpenTable`). Each edit goes to `tableAsks` in the store and `followTableEdits` (`src/follow.ts`) sends the first, one at a time, as `edit_table` with the buffer's text; `table_edited` makes its `content` the buffer's text (same tab, dirty mark, Save, `save_file` with the version check) and its `sheet` the table, or shows its `error` under the header and drops the rest of the file's line and a save waiting on it. An answer applies only to the text its ask was sent from (`tableSentFrom`): a buffer changed meanwhile (a reload, a keystroke) keeps what it has, the file's other asks go, and a notice says so; with an ask on its way a new text on disk shows the conflict banner rather than reloading (`fromDisk`'s `pending`). `saveOpenFile` (`src/viewer/save.ts`) waits for that file's asks only (`saveAfterAsks`, the file), then saves that file's buffer, so ⌘S with a cell open (`EditableTable`, which closes it first) saves its text. While the buffer has unsaved edits a `table` answer from disk is ignored (`reduce.ts`), and the table is asked from the buffer (`edit_table` with no edit) when it starts showing, so the text view can go back to it; a buffer clean again asks the disk. In a pair (25.1) a click or a key first gives the pane the focus, so an edit acts on the open file; the other pane's table, from either answer, stays in its tab. The service (`hive::table_edit`, blocking thread) checks the worktree is followed, the extension says the delimiter, the text is at most 1 MiB (`TEXT_LIMIT`), before and after the edit, its sheet is whole, and that the cell or place is in the table; `scan` walks the text by the `csv` reader's rules (a test holds them to it), giving each row its cells' byte ranges and line ending, and the edit is a list of splices: every byte outside them stays, so an untouched cell keeps its needless quotes, a row its line ending, a blank line its place. A cell written is quoted only when it holds the delimiter, a quote or a line break (RFC 4180, `encode`). A new row has the longest row's cells and the file's first line ending (`\n` if none), and a final line with no ending stays so; a removed one takes its line ending along. A new column reaches the rows that reach it, a shorter row is left alone (the cells it lacks already read as empty); a removed one takes one delimiter along. A row left with one empty cell is written `""`: unquoted it would be a blank line, which is no row. Past a quote never closed nothing can be added: a splice at that row's end would land inside the quote, so it is refused (`UNCLOSED`). The answer's rows share the frame with its text; an edit whose table would not fit is refused.
13. A Markdown block edited in its preview (25.4, `src/viewer/blockEdit.tsx`; HTML has no such edit, its preview being a sandboxed page from disk): a click on a top-level block (`[data-line]`, not on a link, button, form control or `summary`, nor ending a text selection) starts the buffer as Edit does and swaps the block for a text area with its source: lines `data-line` to `data-line-end`, which `markLines` sets from the block's source position, so unrendered lines around it (link definitions, comments) are not in it. The block is hidden and the editor inserted next to it in a portal, leaving the rendered tree alone; each keystroke reaches the buffer at once (`withBlock`: only those lines of the `Text` change, so every other byte stays; a block with CRLF lines shows `\n` and writes CRLF), so the dirty mark and Save work as for the text, while the preview keeps rendering the text it had. Escape or a click outside (which opens no other block) closes it, keeping the edit, and renders the new text; a text from elsewhere (a reload) closes it too. A file with a `\r` alone is refused with a notice: the parser counts it as a line ending and the buffer does not, so a block's lines would not be the buffer's. Ctrl+S saves and shows the text (11.2) unless a block was edited (typed in, not only opened) in this view, when it stays. The previews show the conflict banner and a save's error (`BufferNotes`), without View diff, which only the text can show.

### Daemon start
1. Prepare the runtime dir (0700, owned by the user). A socket path `bind` cannot take (104 bytes or more on macOS, 108 on Linux) is refused first, naming the path and `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (17.9).
2. Take the lockfile with an exclusive `flock`. If it is already held, exit with an error.
3. Install the wrapper and the hooks settings. Then clean what the agent's browser (24.6, 25.2; removed in 26.11) left in an install that had it (`hive::browser_cleanup`): `<data>/hive/bin/hive-browser.json`, which an older wrapper passed to `claude` with `--mcp-config`; each browser an older service left running, its process group killed only while its leader still runs with that profile's `--user-data-dir` (never group 0 or 1); and their profiles, `<runtime>/browsers/`. Its old `browser` setting is no longer read and goes from the file at the next save. A `claude` started before the update still runs `hive browser-mcp`, which fails as an unknown command (exit 2, nothing on stdout); its tools' hooks are tools like any other.
4. Remove any stale socket, bind it and set it to 0600.
5. Serve. Every 2 s (`daemon::claim`, 17.9) the service checks that the runtime dir, the lockfile and the socket are still the ones it made (same device and inode): one removed or replaced (macOS cleans old temporary files; a user may delete the folder) is made again, the lock taken and the socket bound anew, so neither a second daemon nor a bridge's new one can start beside it. A folder that cannot be made, a lock that cannot be opened or a socket that cannot be bound is logged and tried again at the next check. Only when another daemon took the new lock meanwhile, or the folder is no longer safe (`check_runtime`), does this one stop as on SIGTERM, leaving another's socket in place.

### Native Windows service (12.5.2)
The same daemon, bridge, hook and badge, over a named pipe instead of the socket (`hive::windows`):

- **Paths.** Data in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\hive` (else `%USERPROFILE%\AppData\Local`, else the temporary folder), the lock and `daemon.log` in its `run` folder (created with the folder's inherited ACL: the user's local app data), settings in `%APPDATA%\hive`.
- **Pipe name** (12.5.7 follow-up). `\\.\pipe\hive-<SID>-<random>`: the SID (`S-1-5-21-…`) of the user the process runs as (its token's `TokenUser`), and 16 hex digits from `RandomState` (keyed from the system's random source), new for every service, so no other local user can create it first. The service writes it, once it holds the lock and has created the pipe, to the runtime folder's `hive.sock` (the user's local app data ACL), which it removes when it ends. Clients read it from there: at most 256 bytes, and only `\\.\pipe\hive-` followed by letters, digits and `-` (else "<file> does not name a hive pipe"). A missing file, or one a service that did not end well left (its pipe is gone), fails at once (`NotFound`), and the bridge starts a new service, which writes it again. The runtime folder plays the part of `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` on Unix: one service per user and data folder, so a test's service (temporary `LOCALAPPDATA`) never meets the real one.
- **Server.** The first instance is created with `first_pipe_instance(true)`, so a pipe of that name already there makes the daemon exit with the error; `reject_remote_clients(true)`; a security descriptor `O:<SID>D:P(A;;GA;;;<SID>)`: the user as owner (an elevated process would otherwise make the administrators the owner), a protected DACL with all access for the user only. Each connected instance is handed to the connection task and a new one waits for the next client; the lock (`hive.lock`, `File::try_lock`, retried for 5 s) still keeps one service per data folder.
- **Client** (`Paths::connect`, used by the bridge, `hive hook`, `hive badge`, `hive worktree hook-*`): opens the pipe, retrying every 10 ms while every instance is busy (`ERROR_PIPE_BUSY`), for at most 2 s; then the pipe's owner (`GetSecurityInfo`) must be the same SID, else "refusing a hive pipe of another user (<SID>)". This replaces the socket's `check_peer`. It checks the pipe object, not the serving process (`GetNamedPipeServerProcessId`, the plan's first idea): a pipe instance handed to another process keeps its creator's process id, which one of the user's own processes could later reuse, while another user cannot make an object the user's without a privilege (security review).
- **Network and device paths** (12.5.7 follow-up). A path from the app (the folder browser's, `add_project`'s, a Claude or GitHub CLI config folder, a terminal's `cwd`) must start with a drive (`C:`, `\\?\C:`; `std::path::Prefix::Disk`/`VerbatimDisk`), checked before anything opens it (`windows::local`): a UNC path (`\\host\share`, `\\?\UNC\…`) would make Windows send the user's NTLM credentials to that host, and a device path (`\\.\…`, `\\?\…`) is none of their folders. Refused with "<path>: network and device paths are not supported: use a drive path such as C:\…" (`dirs`' `error`, `add_project_failed` `not_found`, the space or settings error, or `error` on the terminal's channel). Worktree ids, files and sessions are only ever those of followed projects, so they need no check of their own.
- **Daemon life.** The bridge starts `hive daemon` with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP | CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB`, again without the breakaway when its job forbids it (cargo's does), stderr to `daemon.log`. `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` gives the service a console of its own without a window, which every console program it runs (git, `gh`, scripts, and what they run) shares; without a console (`DETACHED_PROCESS`, before 16.4) Windows opened a console window for each of them. A terminal's shell gets its pseudoconsole instead. Since any of those programs can send Ctrl+Break to that console or to the service's process group (`GenerateConsoleCtrlEvent`; its own process group turns off Ctrl+C only), the service first installs a console handler that ignores every event (`windows::ignore_ctrl`, the `claude` wrapper's too; a failed install stops the start, in `daemon.log`). A program that reads `CONIN$` directly (e.g. a credential prompt from git or a script) now waits on the hidden console until its time limit, where it used to show a window. Nothing signals a detached daemon: it ends when the app connection ends (the bridge exits when the app closes its stdin, or when the app kills it).
- **Clock.** `sent_ns` is the performance counter (`QueryPerformanceCounter` / `QueryPerformanceFrequency`, in ns), one clock for every process on the machine like `CLOCK_MONOTONIC`.
- **Files** (12.5.6b). A folder moves with `MoveFileExW` without `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING` (an existing target fails, as `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)`); a file save still renames a temporary file over the file, and when another program keeps the file open without sharing it (a sharing or lock violation on the version check's open, access denied on the rename; also what a file Hive may not write gets) it answers `save_failed` `io` "<name> is open in another program, or cannot be replaced: close it there and save again (…)", the file untouched and the temporary file removed. The editor target, the settings file and a session's log or folder are the paths themselves (no `wslpath`); nothing is refused for its permission bits (Windows has none). Resolved paths lose `\\?\` (`\\?\UNC\` becomes `\\`) before git gets them or they are compared; the paths git prints (worktrees) use `\`, while the relative paths the file operations answer keep `/`, as git lists them. Counting an untracked file's lines opens it plainly (no FIFO on Windows). The folder browser: see `hive::dirs`. The watcher (`notify`, `ReadDirectoryChangesW`) watches the same folders as on Linux; Windows also reports a folder as modified for a write anywhere under it (an ignored tree too), so a folder's own change (a modify event on a folder, also a mode change on Unix) counts for nothing: what it holds has its own watch or is ignored.
- **Hooks, the `claude` wrapper and the statusline:** see [Claude Code on native Windows (12.5.5)](#claude-code-on-native-windows-1255).
- **Tested** by `windows.yml`: `hive::windows`'s unit tests (the pipe, busy and missing service, the SID, the clock, the detached start) and the integration test `tests/integration/windows.rs` (the bridge starts the service, the app's handshake, terminals (see below), a hook-role connection's report reaches the app, `hive hook` exits 0 quietly, and the service ends once the app leaves).

### Native Windows terminals (12.5.3)
The same `open_terminal`, input, `resize`, `ack` flow control and `close_terminal` as on Unix ([Terminal open and close](#terminal-open-and-close)), on a pseudoconsole (`hive::windows::terminal`; the portable parts, tested on every OS, are `terminal::conpty`):

- **Shell** (`terminal.shell` in the settings, read when a terminal opens): `default` is `pwsh.exe` when it is on the service's `PATH`, else `powershell.exe`, started with `-NoExit -Command <FOLLOW>` (`conpty::FOLLOW`: once the profile ran, its prompt, the user's wrapped, first sets the process's working folder to its file-system location, which `cd` alone never moves; see [Native Windows processes](#native-windows-processes-1256a)); `cmd` is `cmd.exe`; `git_bash` is `<Git>\bin\bash.exe --rcfile <data>/shell/bashrc -i` (12.5.5; the rc path in Git Bash's form, `/c/…`), `<Git>` being two folders above the first `git.exe` on `PATH` that has one (`<Git>\cmd\git.exe`), else the terminal does not open ("Git Bash was not found: no git.exe on PATH"). The rc file is macOS's bash one (`terminal::login`): it runs `/etc/profile` and the first of `~/.bash_profile`, `~/.bash_login`, `~/.profile`, as a login bash does, then puts `$HIVE_BIN_DIR` first on `PATH` and unsets it; a login bash (`--login`, 12.5.3) put Git's own folders, and whatever the user's profile adds, before Hive's bin folder. An unknown value makes the settings file invalid (serde), so it is checked in Rust.
- **Start.** Two named pipes `\\.\pipe\hive-console-<pid>-<n>` (created as the service's pipe is, only this user can open them): the console host writes the output to one and reads the typing from the other. `CreatePseudoConsole` of the terminal's size, then `CreateProcessW` with the pseudoconsole attribute, `CREATE_SUSPENDED` and invalid standard handles (the service's own are its log), in the terminal's `cwd`. The shell joins a new job with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` (so nothing it started outlives the service), then resumes. Its process id is the terminal's session id.
- **Environment:** the service's, then `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID`, `TERM=xterm-256color` and the terminal's own (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` of its account, its space's, its worktree's `HIVE_*`), names compared ignoring case, then Hive's bin folder first on `PATH`, and `HIVE_BIN_DIR` (that folder in Git Bash's form, for its rc file).
- **Resize:** `ResizePseudoConsole`, only while the console is open.
- **End.** The shell's exit is watched by a thread waiting on its process handle. Ending a terminal (its tab closed, its shell exited, the app gone) closes its pseudoconsole (`ClosePseudoConsole`, on a thread of its own: it waits for the console host, which may wait for its output to be read), which sends its programs `CTRL_CLOSE_EVENT` as SIGHUP does; after at most 2 s with processes still in the job, `TerminateJobObject` kills them with exit code 9. A shell that exits leaves the console open (its output never ends by itself), so the pump's 200 ms drain always runs before it closes.
- **Unhooked `claude`:** a process is in a terminal's session when it is in that terminal's job (`JobObjectBasicProcessIdList`, at most 1024 per terminal); see [Native Windows processes](#native-windows-processes-1256a).

### Native Windows processes (12.5.6a)
What the service runs and watches outside its terminals (`hive::windows`):

- **Process table** (`windows::list`, `sysinfo`, a Windows-only dependency): every process, named by its file without `.exe` (`claude.exe` is `claude`), with its process id as its `pgrp` and, when it is in a terminal's job, that terminal's session (else 0). A terminal's processes also get their command line read (one read of the process's memory each, so only theirs): a `node` running `…\@anthropic-ai\claude-code\cli.js` is npm's `claude` and counts as `claude` (`terminal::conpty::comm`). A process's arguments and open files are not read (`procs::claude_sessions` then knows a `claude`'s session only from Claude's own record).
- **Working folders** (`windows::inside`, for the worktree busy and held checks of `projects`, `file` and `daemon::app`): every process's working folder is read at once (a process's own, from its memory); it and the folder are resolved (`paths::canonical`: 8.3 short names, junctions, links and `subst` drives, no `\\?\`; as they are when that fails), and one is inside the folder when its names start with the folder's, compared ignoring ASCII case. Command Prompt's `cd` moves its working folder and PowerShell's follows its location at each prompt (`conpty::FOLLOW`), but the msys `cd` of Git Bash moves neither its own nor its launcher's: a Git Bash terminal counts where it started, and a program it starts where that program runs. So, as on Unix, a worktree some process works in is not removed, renamed or moved without `force`: Windows would refuse to delete a process's working folder only after `git worktree remove` had deleted the files in it. A process Windows does not let the user read (another user's, a protected one) has no folder.
- **Time limits** (git, `gh`, project scripts): the program joins a new job (`windows::Job`, `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, as a terminal's) right after it starts, so everything it starts is in it too; the limit ends the whole job with `TerminateJobObject` (exit code 9), as the process group is killed on Unix, and so does the end of an archive script. A program that cannot join a job is killed (it would run without a limit). Dropping the job, once the program was waited for, also ends what it left running. (Not started suspended in it as a terminal's shell is: `std::process::Command` cannot resume a process; a program starts nothing in the microseconds before it joins.)
- **Archive scripts** run in the terminals' shell (`terminal.shell`, found as for a terminal): PowerShell `-NoProfile -Command <script>`, Command Prompt `/D /S /C "<script>"` (the line as it is: `cmd` has quoting rules of its own; `/D` skips its AutoRun; a script of more than one line is refused, which cancels the removal: `cmd` would run its first line only), Git Bash `-c <script>`; Git Bash missing fails the script ("Git Bash was not found: no git.exe on PATH"). Same 60 s limit, environment and output as on Unix; a failed script's message says `exit code: N`, Rust's wording on Windows.
- **`gh`** is looked for as `gh.exe` only in the absolute folders of the user's `PATH` (`windows::on_path`): given a bare name, Windows would also find it beside Hive, in its system folders and on the service's own `PATH`.

### App mode (12.5.4)
On Windows the service runs in WSL or on Windows itself, one at a time (human answers 2026-09-28). The app's Rust side keeps the choice, since it is needed before any service starts (`hive_lib::Modes`, `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`):

- **Always on Windows** (12.5.7): `main.rs` builds `Modes` on every Windows start (none on macOS, which sends no `app_mode` and shows no choice). Without WSL the service runs on Windows without asking; with WSL the first run asks; the settings switch it.
- **WSL.** `wsl.exe -l -q` (`CREATE_NO_WINDOW`) run once at start, unless Windows is saved (WSL was there when it was chosen), and killed after 3 s: WSL has a distribution when it succeeds and prints a letter or digit (it prints UTF-16, or UTF-8 with `WSL_UTF8=1`; a missing WSL or distribution fails or prints nothing). Without one the service runs on Windows, never asking, and the settings offer no switch, and `set_mode` refuses `wsl`. A listing killed at its time limit leaves WSL unknown, counted as there: the saved mode starts, else the first run asks, so a slow WSL never moves a WSL user to Windows.
- **Store.** The file `mode` in the app's config folder (`%APPDATA%\com.lucas.hive\mode`): `wsl` or `native`. None (or anything else) with WSL present: the first run, which asks.
- **Connect.** `connect` sends `app_mode` first. With no mode yet it starts nothing: the UI blocks the workspace with "Where should Hive run?" (`ModeChoice` in `src/shell/ConnectionBlock.tsx`; WSL or Windows). Otherwise it starts that mode's bridge (see [Bridge start](#bridge-start)).
- **Choose.** The command `set_mode {mode}` saves the file (a failure is an error toast, nothing changes), then, in the background, ends the connection as the app's exit does (`shutdown`: every terminal exits, `disconnected`) and connects the same UI again (`app_mode`, then the new service's `welcome`, `settings`, `spaces`, `projects`). The first-run dialog and the settings' "Service" select (Terminal section, shown only when WSL is there; asks "Run Hive on Windows?" first) both use it.
- **Separate data.** Each mode is its own service with its own data folder, so projects, spaces and settings are kept per mode (no migration).
- **UI.** The status bar shows "Windows" in native mode (title "Service connection"); the connection dialog drops "in WSL" and stops the old service with `taskkill /F /IM hive.exe` instead of `pkill`. The add-project dialog (`AddProjectDialog.tsx`) has no WSL/Windows select in native mode (no distribution) and treats every path as a Windows one (12.5.7): the text after the last `\` or `/` filters the listed folders, a clicked one is joined with `\`, and a drive letter lists the drives (`hive::dirs`); it sends `list_dirs` with `windows` false, since the service takes its own paths as they are.
- **Bundle.** The Windows installer carries the Linux `hive` and `hive.exe` (`tauri.release.conf.json`); `release.yml` and ci.yml's `windows` job build `hive.exe` on the Windows runner (static CRT), and ci.yml uploads the installer (`hive-windows`, 7 days, no updater artifacts).

### Claude Code on native Windows (spike, 12.5.2)
From Anthropic's documentation (code.claude.com/docs: hooks, statusline, settings, env-vars, setup; 2026-09-28), for 12.5.5:

- **Hooks, exec form** (`args` set, which `hive-hooks.json` uses): no shell on any platform. `command` is resolved as an executable (a bare name on `PATH`, or a path) and spawned directly with `args` as the argument vector, each element one argument exactly as written. An absolute path with spaces such as `C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe` is one valid executable (no quoting, backslashes kept). On Windows `command` must be a real executable (`.exe`): `.cmd`/`.bat` shims cannot be spawned without a shell. So `"command": "<...>\\hive.exe", "args": ["hook", "<Event>"]` works as is, and the `claude` wrapper must be an `.exe` (as planned: a copy of `hive.exe` named `claude.exe`). The `shell` field is ignored with `args`.
- **Hooks, shell form** (no `args`): `sh -c` on macOS and Linux; on Windows Git Bash when Git for Windows is installed (found automatically, or `CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH`, which must name `bash.exe`/`sh.exe`), else PowerShell; `"shell": "bash"` or `"powershell"` chooses. PowerShell is started with `-ExecutionPolicy Bypass` unless `CLAUDE_CODE_POWERSHELL_RESPECT_EXECUTION_POLICY=1`.
- **`statusLine`**: only a shell `command` (no `args`, no exec form, no `shell` field). On Windows it runs through Git Bash when installed, else PowerShell. Git Bash eats unquoted backslashes, so the docs write paths with forward slashes (`~` also expands to the Windows home). Hive's current `'<hive>' statusline` (sh single quotes) works in Git Bash, even with backslashes and spaces, but not in PowerShell, where a quoted path followed by a word is a parse error (it needs `& '<path>' statusline`, which bash refuses). A form both accept: forward slashes and no character that needs quoting; for a path with spaces (a user name with a space), its 8.3 short name (`GetShortPathNameW`, when the volume has them), else the form of the shell Claude Code will pick (Git Bash found: sh quoting; else `& '...'`). When `hive statusline` runs the user's own statusline on Windows, it must use that same shell choice.
- **Environment**: hook and statusline processes inherit Claude Code's environment (so `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID` from a Hive terminal reaches them), plus `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` for every hook (and `CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` for some events). `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is not set by Claude Code: it is inherited when the user or Hive set it; without it the config folder is `%USERPROFILE%\.claude` (settings in `%USERPROFILE%\.claude\settings.json`). The input's paths (`cwd`, `transcript_path`) are Windows paths.

### Claude Code on native Windows (12.5.5)
Hooks, the `claude` wrapper and the statusline, built on the spike above (`hive::windows::claude`):

- **Copies in the bin folder.** The bridge copies the running `hive.exe` (the installed one, or `HIVE_BRIDGE`'s) to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\hive\bin\hive.exe` and starts the service from that copy; the service's `wrapper::install` copies itself to `bin\hive.exe` again when it runs from elsewhere (a hand-started daemon) and to `bin\claude.exe`. So nothing Hive runs for long, the service, its hooks or its statusline, keeps the installed `hive.exe` busy, and the app's installer can replace it (the bridge itself ends with the app). A copy is made beside the file (`<name>.<pid>-<ns>.tmp`) and renamed over it; a program running from the file cannot be replaced but can be renamed, so it is first moved aside (`<name>.<pid>-<ns>.old`). Each install removes what was moved aside earlier, unless it still runs (then the next one tries again).
- **Hooks.** `hive-hooks.json` runs `bin\hive.exe` in exec form (a real `.exe`, no shell), as on Unix.
- **The wrapper** is `bin\claude.exe`, first on the terminals' `PATH`: `hive` started under the name `claude` (its own file's, `current_exe`) is the wrapper instead of the CLI. It looks for the real `claude` on its `PATH` as `wrapper::real_claude` does (below), outside its own folder, however that folder is written (compared resolved); none: `wrapper::NO_CLAUDE` ("hive: no claude found in PATH: Claude Code is not installed on this host." and how to install it, see [The user's `claude`](#the-users-claude)), exit 127. Unless `HIVE_WRAPPED` is 1 (a `claude` started from inside a hooked one: its hooks are in place already, so it runs as it is), it sets `HIVE_WRAPPED=1` and passes `--settings <data>\hive-hooks.json` before the user's arguments. The real `claude` inherits its streams; the wrapper waits and exits with its code (126 when it cannot start; a code over 255, such as the `NTSTATUS` of a program ended by Ctrl+C, is 1), returned from `main`: `process::exit` is `ExitProcess` on Windows, which skips what runs at exit (the coverage profile). A console's Ctrl+C and Ctrl+Break reach every program on it: the wrapper handles both by doing nothing (a handler, not the inherited ignore flag), so `claude` decides and the wrapper outlives it.
- **The real `claude`** (`wrapper::real_claude`, also Diagnostics): each absolute `PATH` folder but the bin folder, `claude` with each extension of `PATHEXT` in its order (else `.COM;.EXE;.BAT;.CMD`), the first file found. The native installer's is `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\claude.exe`; an npm install is `claude.cmd`, which runs `node.exe` (`std::process::Command` runs a `.cmd` through `cmd.exe`, quoting its arguments).
- **What the unhooked-`claude` watcher sees** (processes named `claude`, from the terminal jobs): a native `claude.exe`, and the wrapper `claude.exe` itself while it waits, so a `claude` started through the wrapper is seen whatever its install. An npm `claude` is `node.exe` running Claude Code's `cli.js`, which the watcher also counts as `claude` (12.5.6a, `conpty::comm`).
- **The user's `PATH`** (`wrapper::user_path`) is the service's own (inherited from the app, the user's: no shell is asked), then `%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin` and `%USERPROFILE%\.claude\local`.
- **The Claude folder** is `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, else `%USERPROFILE%\.claude` (`sessions::claude_dir`, `sessions::root`: the home is `dirs::HOME`, `USERPROFILE` on Windows, never Git Bash's `HOME`).
- **`hive worktree hook-create`/`hook-remove`** run as on Unix (paths with `\`, 12.5.6b), reached from the fake `claude`'s hooks in the Windows integration test.
- **`statusLine` command** (`statusline_command`): the `bin\hive.exe` path with `/` between names and ` statusline` after it, bare when every character is a letter, a digit or one of `/._:-~` (so both Git Bash and PowerShell read it as one word); else its 8.3 short name (`GetShortPathNameW`) when that one is bare (a user name with a space); else quoted for the shell Claude Code will pick, as the service sees it: `'…'` (sh quoting) when Git Bash is found, else `& '…'` (PowerShell, `'` doubled).
- **The statusline's shell** (`statusline_shell`), for the user's own statusline, as Claude Code picks it: Git Bash (`CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH`, else the `bin\bash.exe` of the first `git.exe` on `PATH`, as for terminals) with `-c`, else PowerShell (`pwsh` when on `PATH`, else Windows PowerShell) with `-NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command`. Time and output limits as on Unix. It joins a `windows::Job` (12.5.6a) right after it starts (`statusline_job`; one it cannot join is killed), dropped as its run ends: late or in time, it and whatever it started end then (unlike Unix, nothing it leaves in the background stays), and when Claude Code cancels a run by killing `hive statusline` (no signal on Windows), the job goes with it. A `taskkill /T` would miss the process Git Bash is forking at that moment, which then waits 5 minutes holding the output pipe.
- **Tested** on the Windows runner: `windows::claude`'s unit tests (the copies moved aside while running, the command, the short name, the shell, the wrapper against a `claude.cmd`), `statusline`'s tests through Git Bash, and the integration test: `tests/fixtures/fake_claude.rs` (never the real `claude`; std only, built by the test with `rustc`, since `cargo llvm-cov` builds no examples) as a `claude.exe` behind the wrapper gets `--settings`, its `SessionStart` hook reaches the app as an agent, its `WorktreeCreate`/`WorktreeRemove` hooks make and remove a worktree; the `statusLine` command run by PowerShell and by Git Bash prints the user's statusline and reports the window; Git Bash puts the bin folder first after the user's `.bash_profile`.

### Handshake
See [Handshake](#handshake). A refused client is not the app, so the daemon keeps waiting for one.

### Terminal open and close
1. `open_terminal` on channel n.
2. The service spawns `fish -C 'set -gx PATH <bin> $PATH'` (on macOS and on a remote service, the user's login shell, see `hive::terminal`) on a new PTY. The shell is the session leader, and the environment (the service's, without `HIVE_WRAPPED` and `HIVE_STATUSLINE`, `wrapper::GUARDS`: a service started from a hooked `claude` or a statusline never hands its terminals those guards, 28.1) has `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID=n` and `TERM=xterm-256color`, plus, in a followed worktree, `HIVE_PORT`, `PORT`, `HIVE_WORKTREE_PATH` and `HIVE_ROOT_PATH` (see [Project scripts and ports](#project-scripts-and-ports-68)).
3. The service replies `terminal_opened` with the worktree it placed the cwd in.
4. A pump task copies PTY output into terminal frames. There is no scrollback on the service side. Typing and output never wait for another task (9.13): input goes through a per-terminal queue found under a short std lock, and the pump records the time of the last output in an atomic shared with the registry, so neither waits while agents are placed or their logs read. The pump waits only for the app's acknowledgements of its own output (`terminal::Output`, a count shared with `ack` through a `watch`, found under the same std lock as the input queue; see Flow control in [Wire protocol](#wire-protocol)); input never waits for them.
5. When fish exits, the pump ends the terminal's other process groups as on `close_terminal` and sends `terminal_exited {code}`. If another process keeps the PTY open (a disowned job, a `setsid` child), the output still gets 200 ms first (9.15).
6. `close_terminal` ends the terminal's session (see below). The exit is reported by the pump.

### Line provenance (27.5)
Design and decisions: `docs/spike/line-provenance.md` (approved 2026-11-26 with every recommendation). Advisory only: what Hive recorded, never proof, never input to anything that acts.
1. `hive hook PostToolUse` turns a file edit into `hive_edit {file, kind: "edit"|"create"|"update"|"notebook", before, lines, whole, added, user_modified}`: `added` holds one `[start (1-based), count, hashes]` per run of `+` lines of the `structuredPatch` (hunks whose lines disagree with their counts are left out; at most 4096 hunks and 200 000 patch lines), hashes packed as base64 of little-endian `u64`s, empty for a run over 5000 lines or past 40 000 hashed lines in all (the range is kept); `whole`: a new file, or a `Write` with no patch (Q5), whose run is its whole content; `before`: the digest of `originalFile`'s line hashes; `lines`: the line count after the edit when known. A `NotebookEdit` is a file-level record (no lines).
2. `hive::anchor` (shared with 27.3's anchored comments): `line_hash` (FNV-1a 64 of the line without `\n`/trailing `\r`, its lowest bit set when the line has at least 8 non-blank characters), `hashes(text, most)`, `digest`, `diff(old, new) -> LineMap` (Myers over hashes after the common start and end, at most 1000 edits, past which every line in between counts as replaced; `LineMap::line`, `range -> Kept | Partly | Gone`), `Anchor::at(file, range, context)`, `relocate(anchor, file, hint)` / `relocate_scored` (an exact run of the anchor's lines, ranked by matching context lines counted outward, then by distance from `hint`, then the earlier place; `None` when gone, *trivial* (under 3 lines with none significant) or past 1 000 000 line comparisons (`RELOCATE_WORK`), so a file of identical lines cannot make it quadratic), `pack`/`unpack`.
3. The service (`hive::provenance`, on each hook call, off the frame loop) checks `hive_edit` again (an absolute path of at most 4096 bytes, a known kind, at most 4096 runs in order and never overlapping, within 50 000 lines, at most 40 000 hashed lines, a `whole` edit one run, hashes that unpack to their count, else the run keeps its range only), places the file in the deepest followed worktree (`projects::place`, groups left out), never inside `.git`, and reads it through `file::open_inside` (a regular file inside the worktree; at most 2 MiB and 50 000 lines, else a file-level record only). It keeps an edit record (session, subagent, tool, `tool_use_id`, time, path, runs) and the file's line map: lines the diff from the stored map keeps keep their origin, the others are unknown (no map yet: `before`); each run is claimed where its hashes are at its line, else nearest to it (`relocate`, for at most 256 runs of the edit), else its changed lines stay unknown; a `whole` run claims, through a diff, only lines changed since the map; unknown lines holding again one of the runs of the file's 50 latest records (at most 256 runs) are that edit's (never a trivial run). A claim whose hashes are not in the file labels nothing.
4.
