Re-sync: at each edit; at each `PostToolUse` or `Stop` of an agent, the followed files of its `cwd`'s worktree whose size or modification time changed (a `stat` each, at most once a second per worktree), changed lines `unknown`; Hive's editor's `save_file` (changed lines are the user's, `editor@<ms>`, and the file starts being followed); before each live lookup.
5. `who_wrote`: on the worktree side, a line whose origin is an edit or the editor is answered live. Otherwise (unknown, before, no map, the base side) `git blame --porcelain -L n,n [<base commit>] -- <path>` (10 s limit) gives the commit C, the path and line in C and the line's text (hashed, dropped); uncommitted is `unknown`. `git ls-tree`/`cat-file` of C's file (≤ 2 MiB) gives the line with 3 lines of context; the repository's records for that path, C's path, or the path C renamed it from (`git diff-tree -M`), made before C's committer time plus 5 minutes, that hold the line's hash in a run are ranked by `relocate_scored`'s context score, then the newest: the best is a probable answer with C, else `unknown` with C. A session whose log is gone shows `deleted` (Q7: records outlive a deleted session).
6. Store: `<Hive folder>/provenance/<16 hex of the project id>.json` (folder 0700, files 0600, `write_atomic`), written at most every 2 s (`keep_provenance`) and at shutdown, loaded the first time a project is needed; a malformed file (or one naming another project) is renamed `.json.bad` and Hive starts empty; on load, maps whose runs do not cover their lines exactly, paths that are not plain relative ones (or in `.git`), invalid session ids and runs outside 50 000 lines are dropped, and the next id is above every kept one. Caps (Q6, pending): records older than 90 days or past 20 000 per repository go (oldest first); past 8 MiB a repository's line maps untouched longest go (a tenth per pass), then the oldest records (a tenth per pass); past 64 MiB in all, the least recently written files go. A worktree removed through Hive drops its maps (its records stay, for lookups through a commit); one gone when loaded too; a project removed drops its file.
7. `list_session_files`: the session's files from every followed repository's records (`edits`, `lines_now` and `first_line` from the current maps, `last_ms`), the latest first, at most 500. `sessions` carries each session's `files_changed`.
8. App (`src/provenance.ts`, `src/viewer/lineAuthor.ts`, `src/shell/LineAuthor.tsx`): a right-click in the gutter of the viewer, the editor or the diff (`lineMenu`, a `contextmenu` listener on CodeMirror's wrapper taking only `.cm-gutters`; the text keeps its native menu) opens `LineMenu` with "Who wrote this line?"; the palette has the same command for the focused view's cursor line (`trackView`/`cursorAsk`), no shortcut (Q8). A line of the unified diff's removed-lines widget asks the `base` side at its original line, any other the `worktree` side; `who_wrote` carries the panel's diff base (`transport.whoWrote`, app command `who_wrote {host?, …}`, hosted). `authorAnswered` (`reduce.ts`) ignores an answer for another line; a live session answer opens the right panel's Sessions tab with `sessionFocus` (the row highlighted and scrolled to, its edit line "Edit · 3h ago · lines 120–122 · you changed it before accepting", its "N files" open with the file highlighted; a session not listed for the shown worktree in a "From <worktree>" section above the list); any other answer shows `LinePopover` by the line: "Probably <session>, committed in <short id> · <time>" with "Show session", "You, in Hive's editor · <time>", or the unknown sentence with the blamed commit. A session row with `files_changed` has an "N files" toggle that sends `list_session_files` (`transport.listSessionFiles`); a file opens in the editor at `first_line`. `line_author` and `session_files` are in `REMOTE_TO_UI` (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`) and `REMOTE_TYPES` (`src/hosts.ts`); the store's `lineMenu`, `provenance`, `sessionFocus`, `openSessionFiles` and `sessionFiles` are per host (`HOST_FIELDS`). The mock answers `src/auth/session.ts`: lines 39–41 a live session ("Fix the login redirect", from another worktree in `feat-checkout`), line 2 the editor, line 1 a probable subagent's, the base side a probable session, other lines unknown with `MOCK_BLAME`'s commit; `session_files` for two fake sessions.

### Hook event
1. Claude Code, started through the wrapper with `--settings <data>/hive/hive-hooks.json`, runs `<abs path>/hive hook <Event>` in exec form with a 1 s timeout.
2. `hive hook` stamps `sent_ns` (`CLOCK_MONOTONIC`, before reading anything), then reads stdin: at most 512 KiB (a `PostToolUse` 32 MiB, 27.5), invalid JSON kept as a string. Past the limit the payload becomes `{hive_error, session_id?, agent_id?, tool_name?}`, the ids from a bounded scan of its first 4 KiB, so the agent's state still follows the event.
   - A `PostToolUse` is then slimmed (`hive::slim`, 27.5): only the fields the service reads stay (`session_id`, `transcript_path`, `cwd`, `permission_mode`, `hook_event_name`, `agent_id`, `agent_type` (64 characters at most), `teammate_name`, `tool_name`, `background_tasks`, `tool_use_id` when it is `[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, and `tool_response`'s `backgroundTaskId`/`taskId`/`agentId`; for a Bash call whose `tool_input.run_in_background` is `true`, `tool_input`'s `command` (1000 characters at most) and `description` (200) with `run_in_background`, and nothing else of any call's `tool_input`, 28.8); an `Edit`/`MultiEdit`/`Write`/`NotebookEdit` also gets `hive_edit` (see [Line provenance](#line-provenance-275)). `tool_response`'s ids are kept only as strings of at most 256 bytes. A slimmed payload over 512 KiB loses `hive_edit`; still over, it keeps only `session_id`, `agent_id` and `tool_name` with `hive_error`. The over-limit marker's head scan also keeps `agent_id`. The file's text (`originalFile`, `content`, the patch) never crosses the socket.
3. If `--record` is given, it appends a JSONL line (the raw payload, before slimming).
4. It connects and sends `hello` and `hook` within 200 ms. It prints nothing and exits 0.
5. The service translates the call with the `ClaudeCode` adapter. A `SessionStart` marks the terminal's `claude` as hooked.
6. Agents (see [Agent detection](#agent-detection)) are updated.
7. The service closes the hook connection. Nothing of the event itself goes to the app.

### Badge (6.12)
1. `hive badge <text…>` (words joined with spaces) or `hive badge --clear` reads `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID` (a number from 1; otherwise an error and exit 2).
2. It connects, sends `hello` (role `hook`) and `badge {text}` on channel `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID` within 200 ms; on failure it prints `hive: cannot reach the Hive service: …` and exits 1.
3. The service cleans the text (control and invisible characters dropped, trimmed, at most 40 characters) and forwards `badge` to the app on that channel if the terminal is open.
4. The app shows a non-empty label as a muted pill on the terminal's tab and on its agent's row; `terminal_exited` clears it.

### Session usage (12.1)
1. `hive-hooks.json` sets `statusLine` to `'<abs path>/hive' statusline` (a shell command, as Claude Code runs a statusline; on Windows a form Git Bash and PowerShell both read, see [Claude Code on native Windows (12.5.5)](#claude-code-on-native-windows-1255)). `--settings` outranks the user's and the project's settings, so it replaces their `statusLine` in Hive's terminals only; managed settings still outrank it.
2. `hive statusline` reads Claude Code's JSON on stdin (untrusted; at most 512 KiB parsed, as a hook's). Its key is the Claude config folder it runs with: `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, else `$HOME/.claude` (`%USERPROFILE%\.claude` on Windows; `sessions::claude_dir`, written one way: an explicit `~/.claude` is the same key). With a whole `rate_limits.five_hour` or `rate_limits.seven_day` (`used_percentage` a number, rounded into 0–100; `resets_at` whole seconds), it sends `hello` (role `hook`) and `statusline_usage` within 200 ms, errors ignored, the 5-hour one as its `usage`, the 7-day one as its `week` (15.3), either alone (28.1; Claude Code's docs: each window may be absent on its own).
3. At the same time it runs the user's own statusline: `statusLine.command` (with `type: "command"`) from `.claude/settings.local.json`, else `.claude/settings.json` under the input's `workspace.project_dir` (else its `cwd`, else the process's folder), else the config folder's `settings.json`. It runs with `sh -c` (on Windows the shell Claude Code picks: Git Bash, else PowerShell), in the same folder and environment plus `HIVE_STATUSLINE=1`, gets the same input (all of it, even past the parse limit) and has 10 s; its stdout is printed unchanged (nothing when over 64 KiB or late) and its exit code is Hive's (1 after a signal). With no user statusline, or when `HIVE_STATUSLINE` is set (a statusline that runs Hive's own), it prints nothing and exits 0. It runs in its own process group: late, or cancelled by a SIGTERM (Claude Code cancelling a run, which then prints nothing), the group is killed (on Windows its job, see 12.5.5); one that ended in time keeps what it left in the background. Settings files that are not regular files (a FIFO, a device) are never opened.
4. The service keeps the latest windows per folder (a report replaces both: a window absent from it disappears, 28.1), and writes them to `<data>/hive/usage.json` when they change (21.8; mode `0600`, atomic; a JSON object of folder → `{usage, week}`). A new service loads them at start (at most 2 MiB read, more than the most it writes; bounded as reports; an unreadable or corrupt file has none), so a window still before its reset shows as soon as the app connects; one past its reset is kept but not shown. A file that cannot be written only logs a warning. The current account is the current space's (22.5, `env.claude_account`, see [Claude accounts](#claude-accounts-122)), else the one the service's terminals get (`State::account`, the one place it is chosen). It sends `session_usage` when the current account's windows differ from the ones the app has: after a report and a settings change (an account switch), every second while the terminals' tick runs (the 5-hour `resets_at` passed: `usage` null; the 7-day one passed: `week` null; each on its own, 28.1), and to a new app. The tick (`watch_terminals`, 19.3) runs while a terminal is open or the app has a window (`State::ticking`) and sleeps otherwise until a terminal opens or `session_usage` is sent; it lists processes only when a terminal printed since the last tick (a `claude` that starts or exits prints) or an unhooked `claude` may still be warned about (`Watch::pending`).
5. The status bar shows a ring filled to the 5-hour percentage while there is a window (15.3; the warning colour from 80%), else to the 7-day one when it came alone (28.1); with none while the service is connected, the track alone, dimmed (`data-empty`), with the tooltip and accessible name "No usage yet: it appears once an agent of this account answers" (21.8); nothing while it is not connected. Its tooltip (on hover and keyboard focus) and accessible name read "Session N% · resets HH:MM" (local time) and "Week N% · resets Ddd HH:MM", each only for a window the service sent (28.1; the menu bar's usage lines are the same, 17.22). It updates only while a `claude` runs in a Hive terminal: a chat or a `claude` outside Hive feeds nothing.
6. **Pace (17.23).** A window began its length before its reset (5 h, 7 days); its pace is the share used over the time since then, so at `now` it reaches 100% at `start + (now − start) × 100 / used` (`daemon::usage::limit_at`, rounded down to the minute so `session_usage` changes at most once a minute). None when that is not before its reset (it lasts), nothing or all of it is used, or `now` is before its start. Idle, the forecast moves later every minute until the window lasts; a new window starts afresh. The tooltip adds "At this pace: limit at HH:MM" under the session's line and "At this pace: limit Ddd HH:MM" under the week's, only when there is one.
7. **Alerts (17.23).** When the current account's live window (as sent in `session_usage`) is at 80% or more, the service sends `usage_alert` for the highest threshold reached (80, 95) that this window has not alerted for: once per threshold and window (a jump from below 80 to 95 sends 95 alone; the record of a window ends at its `resets_at`, so the next window alerts again), kept per Claude config folder in memory (a new app does not alert again; a window loaded from `usage.json`, 21.8, counts as having alerted for the thresholds it already reached). The app (`notify`, text from `src/usage.ts`) adds an inbox item ("Session usage at 80%", "Weekly usage at 95%"; `agent` and `state` null, a gauge icon in `--state-permission`; choosing it only closes the inbox; the bell's badge still counts pending agents only) and shows an OS notification with that title and "Resets HH:MM" (the week's "Resets Ddd HH:MM") through `show_notification` with an empty `agent` (a click only brings the window up), whether or not the window is focused; no tone.

### Agent detection
1. A `SessionStart` with a `session_id`, no `agent_id` (a subagent belongs to its agent, see [Agent states](#agent-states)) and a `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID` naming an open terminal registers the agent: session id → terminal.
2. The service places it by the payload's `cwd`, never the terminal's (#19): the followed worktree whose path contains `cwd`, by whole path components, the deepest one winning (Claude worktrees live inside the main one). Placement runs git (`projects.list()`) on a blocking thread, and is done once; a project followed later does not move an agent already detected.
3. It sends `agent_detected` on the terminal's channel. The agents lock is held while placing, so a `SessionEnd` or the terminal's exit arriving meanwhile is handled after the announcement (other terminals are not blocked). An agent outside every followed project is sent with a null `project` and `worktree`; the UI does not show it.
4. A `SessionEnd` for that session (after its `agent_state` `ended`), or the terminal's exit, removes it with `agent_removed`. A `/clear` is a `SessionEnd` plus a `SessionStart` with a new session id, in either order.
5. Channel n messages go through the app's Rust side like other terminal messages, so a reloaded UI never sees agents of terminals it did not open. On `disconnected` the UI drops every agent.
6. The sidebar shows each agent as a "Claude" row (idle icon) under its worktree; clicking it shows its terminal's tab (`activateTab`).

### Agent states
`hive::states`, from `docs/hive.md` "Mapeamento de estados". The UI only renders `agent_state`.
1. A detected agent starts idle. Each later hook event with its `session_id` sets the state of the agent, or of the subagent named by `agent_id`: `SessionStart` idle; `UserPromptSubmit`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PostToolUseFailure` (never error), `SubagentStart`, `PreCompact` and `Notification` `elicitation_complete`/`elicitation_response`/`quota_auto_resume_fired` working; `PermissionRequest` and `Notification` `permission_prompt` waiting for permission; `PreToolUse`/`PermissionRequest` of `AskUserQuestion`, `Elicitation` and `Notification` `elicitation_dialog`/`elicitation_url_dialog`/`agent_needs_input` waiting for your answer; `PreToolUse`/`PermissionRequest` of `ExitPlanMode` waiting for plan approval; `Stop` and `Notification` `idle_prompt`/`quota_auto_resume_stale`/`quota_auto_resume_disabled` waiting for you; `StopFailure` error; `SessionEnd` ended. `Notification` `permission_prompt` (Claude Code's reminder ~6 s into any dialog) leaves waiting for your answer or plan approval as it is. Anything else leaves the state as it is.
   - Compaction: the agent's own `PreCompact` shows working with the activity "Compacting" and remembers its state and activity; its `PostCompact` gives them back (another event in between forgets them). The `SessionStart` with `source: "compact"` of a detected session leaves the agent as it is (state, subagents, tokens); any other `SessionStart` of it detects it again.
   - A `Stop` whose `background_tasks` is not empty keeps waiting for you with the activity "N background tasks"; a `UserPromptSubmit` clears the activity.
   - Order (9.17): each hook call comes on its own connection, so a large payload still being read can arrive after a later call. The agent keeps the last `sent_ns` applied to itself and to each live subagent (dropped when the subagent leaves); an event of the same one with an older `sent_ns` changes neither its state nor its activity (nor wakes a subagent waiting on a background task), but is still forwarded and still counts for its worktree, its background launches, a subagent leaving and the silence timer. An equal `sent_ns` applies.
2. A subagent joins the list on its first event with a state and leaves it on `SubagentStop` (or a `SessionEnd` carrying its `agent_id`), unless it ended its turn to wait on a background task it launched: the task ids come from its `PostToolUse` `tool_response` (`backgroundTaskId` for Bash `run_in_background`, `taskId` for `Monitor`, `agentId` for `Agent`), and Claude Code lists the session's running ones as `background_tasks` on every `Stop`/`SubagentStop`. While one of its launches is in that list it stays listed as working (silence does not change it) until it is woken (`SubagentStart` again); it leaves when a later list has none of them, when its own worktree is removed, or with its agent. A subagent whose run ends on an API error (a `StopFailure` carrying its `agent_id`) gets no `SubagentStop` (27.7): it leaves on that failure, error or not, and the agent's own state is not changed by it; one with a launch recorded waits as above until a list without it. A teammate's `StopFailure` makes it an error and keeps it listed. At most 32 are kept per agent (idle teammates count), and ids or types over 256 bytes are ignored, so a message always fits in a frame.
   - Agent team teammates (26.10, D20.3): an in-process teammate's hooks carry its lead's `session_id` and its own `agent_id`, so it is a subagent of the lead. `TeammateIdle` makes it idle and moves it to the end of the list, joining the list if it was not on it, and marks it a teammate until it leaves: its `SubagentStop` then makes it idle instead of removing it, and its next `SubagentStart` makes it work again. It leaves on a `SessionEnd` carrying its `agent_id`, or with its lead. So `SubagentStop` then `TeammateIdle`, `TeammateIdle` then `SubagentStop` and `TeammateIdle` alone end the same; which of them Claude Code sends is not documented (a recording is still owed, `TODO.md` 26.10). A `TeammateIdle` without an `agent_id` (the docs say it may be absent) changes nothing. An idle teammate is not at work: it does not make its lead "with subagents", holds back no alert, and rule 2 leaves it alone. Its name, `teammate_name` from any of its hooks, loses control and invisible characters and is cut at 64 characters. The shared task list (`TaskCreated`, `TaskCompleted`) is not observed.
3. Rule 1: `state` is the most urgent of the agent's own state, its subagents' states and "with subagents" when any subagent is live and not idle. Urgency, highest first: `waiting_permission`, `waiting_plan`, `waiting_answer`, `error`, `waiting_you`, `with_subagents`, `working`, `idle`, `ended`.
4. Rule 2 (interrupts; Esc/Ctrl+C fire no `Stop`, nor does declining a dialog): every second (the unhooked-claude tick) the service reads the new lines of the transcript of each agent that is working, with subagents or on a dialog (`transcript::Usage`, the same bounded reader as its tokens; the lines already there when the agent was detected are read first and never count). When the last main-conversation message read is `[Request interrupted by user…]` or a declined tool call (Claude Code's `toolUseResult` "User rejected tool use" on a record with a failed `tool_result`; declining with feedback writes another marker and Claude goes on; the result's text is the tool's, so it is never trusted), the agent and each subagent working or on a dialog (not those waiting on a background task) go to waiting for you, `interrupted` and not pending, until the displayed state changes. Fallback: when its terminal has printed nothing for `agents.silence_secs` (5 s by default, see [Settings](#settings)), counted from the later of the last output and the last hook event, the agent and each subagent that is working go to waiting for you. The three dialog states never decay with silence (a dialog is static on screen). Output alone never moves a state back; only hook events do.
5. Pending (the "N pending" counter and F8): waiting for permission, a plan or an answer, error and waiting for you. `urgency` is the state's rank in the order above; the app shows a collapsed project or worktree with the state of highest `urgency` inside (a pending one first at equal urgency) and counts agents with `pending`, so it keeps no table of its own. Except (hive.md items 4 and 5, 15.6): an agent waiting for you while its terminal is the one in `view` and the window has the focus is seen, so not pending, until its displayed state changes again (looking away later keeps it seen): whether it got there in view, or was already there when its terminal came into view (`State::view` → `Agent::watch` sends its `agent_state` at once). Dialogs and error stay pending in view. The app draws a waiting for you that is not pending (seen, interrupted, or 13.3's below) muted: its icon and state name in `--text-3`, its title in `--text-2`, in the sidebar row, a collapsed node's rollup and its tab (`muted` in `src/store.ts`, `StateIcon`'s `muted`). Likewise (13.3) an agent whose displayed state becomes waiting for you while a subagent is still listed under it in its own turn (one kept listed only by its own background launch, 5.10, does not count) has not finished: not pending and no alert, until its displayed state changes again; Claude Code wakes it when a background subagent ends, and the turn that ends with none left alerts `finished`.
6. `agent_state` is sent on the agent's terminal channel only when the message changes.
7. A subagent's own worktree (#22), sent as its `worktree` (the worktree's id, i.e. its path). Linked by a `worktree_created` carrying the subagent's `agent_id` (the path the hook created), or else by the subagent's own events: the first time each new `cwd` of a subagent without a worktree is seen, the service places it like an agent (`projects::place`, git on a blocking thread) and links the worktree found when it is not the agent's own. Only when the agent itself was placed; paths over 4096 bytes are ignored. A `worktree_removed` of that path unlinks it; the subagent leaving drops it. The sidebar gives that worktree no row (9.35), its path is the subagent line's tooltip, unless an agent runs in it or a terminal of the human's opened in it (the worktree `projects::place` gave the terminal at `open_terminal`, until it exits): `subagent_worktrees` lists those it hides. To confirm by spike 1.12: whether `WorktreeCreate` carries `agent_id`, and that a subagent's `cwd` is its worktree.
8. Activity and time: an event with an `activity` sets it for the agent, or for the subagent that fired it; `Stop`, `StopFailure`, `SessionEnd` and (for a subagent) `SubagentStop` and `TeammateIdle` clear it, and so does rule 2 for the agent or subagent it interrupts. `since_ms` is when the displayed state (a subagent's: its own state) last changed, on the wall clock: the daemon gives each agent the wall time it was detected and later times are counted from it with the monotonic clock. The sidebar shows the time in the state ("12s", "3m", "1h", from `since_ms`, one shared 1 s timer) and the activity, muted, after the state's name.
9. Subagents in the sidebar (9.35): each live subagent is one line under its agent, smaller and dimmer than an agent row: its state's icon (same colour and shape, smaller), its `teammate_name` or else its `agent_type`, its activity (cut with an ellipsis) and the time in its state. The line is not a control: nothing to click, select or focus, no hover state and no context menu. There is no subagent conversation view (6.10 was dropped).

### Background commands (28.8)
`hive::background`, from `docs/spike/background-commands.md` (the human approved its recommendations, 2026-11-27). Each agent keeps its own and its subagents' `run_in_background` Bash commands and `Monitor` watches, sent in `agent_state`'s `background`. Every field comes from a hook payload: ids over 256 bytes are ignored, text is cleaned of control and invisible characters (`adapter::clip`) and cut, and at most 32 are kept per agent (a new one past them takes the oldest ended one's place, else is ignored).
1. Start: the `PostToolUse` of a Bash call with `tool_response.backgroundTaskId` (its `tool_input.command` and `description`, kept by `hive::slim`), or of `Monitor` with `taskId` (its input fields wait for the recording), adds it, with the subagent whose `agent_id` the payload carries. An event older than the last one applied (9.17) still counts for it.
2. `background_tasks` on every `Stop`/`SubagentStop` (the session's running tasks): a `shell` or `monitor` entry not known yet is added from its `command`/`description` (its subagent unknown); a running command first seen before that list (by `sent_ns`) and missing from it has ended, status `unknown`. A list older than the last one applied is ignored.
3. End: `background_finished` is sent once per command, after the `agent_state` that lists it as ended. `alert` is `failed` for status `failed` or an exit code other than 0 (`BackgroundAlert::of`), else `quiet` (`stopped` and `unknown` never fail); `notify` is a failure while the agent is not `watched`. Claude Code's task notice in the transcript, which carries `completed`/`failed`/`stopped` and the exit code in its summary (`background::exit_code`), ends a command through `Commands::end`; reading it waits for the human's recording of its shape (28.8c), so until then a command ends at the next list, quiet.
4. An ended command stays listed 10 s (the agents' tick drops it, with a new `agent_state`). The agent's own `SessionEnd` drops them all with no message (Claude Code stops them at exit); a subagent leaving keeps its commands until a list without them.
5. App: after the agent's subagent lines, one quiet line per command (`BackgroundLine` in `src/shell/Sidebar.tsx`): a terminal icon, the subagent's teammate name or type, the description (else the command line, else "Background command" / "Monitor"), the time it has run, and the full command line as its tooltip; ended, struck through with its result ("done", "stopped", "failed", "exit 3"), red when it failed. No control: the user stops a task in Claude Code. `notify` (`src/notify.ts`) makes `background_finished` an inbox item on the agent's terminal (`{terminal, failed}`, "Background command failed (exit 3): Build"); only a failure plays the tone, and shows an OS notification when `notify` (named by its description only, never its command line, which may hold a secret; body "<terminal> · after 12s"), since Claude's wake-up turn alerts `finished` as usual. In the mock transport `bg-start <id> <command>` and `bg-end <id> <status> [exit]` typed in an agent's terminal stand in for them.

### Transcripts
1. The agent's `SessionStart` `transcript_path` is kept on its `states::Agent` (`transcript`) when it is an absolute `.jsonl` path of at most 4096 bytes; its tokens (6.9) and interrupts (rule 2) are read from there.
2. Each read canonicalizes it (links resolved) and reads it only when it is a regular file inside a Claude projects folder: its terminal's account's, the default one's (`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/projects`, else `~/.claude/projects`) or any other account's (12.2, see [Claude accounts](#claude-accounts-122)).
3. The first read takes the last 8 MiB (a line cut at the start does not parse and is skipped), then each read takes the whole lines added since (a line still being written waits; a transcript that shrank is read again from its start).

### Tokens and context (6.9)
From the transcripts only: no 5 h or weekly limits, and the user's statusline is never touched.
1. An agent's `PostToolUse`, `Stop`, `SubagentStop` or `TeammateIdle` (its subagents' too) marks its usage due; the next tick of the unhooked-claude timer (every second) reads the whole lines its transcript (`transcript_path`, checks and 8 MiB bounds in [Transcripts](#transcripts)) gained since the last read. So a transcript is read at most once a second per agent, and only the last 8 MiB of a long one count at first.
2. Each `assistant` record outside a sidechain with a `message.usage` counts: the context is its `input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens` (records with no context, such as API errors, are skipped); `output_tokens` add up, once per `message.id` (Claude repeats a message's usage on each of its content blocks; the last one wins). A field that is missing, not a number or over 100 million counts as 0.
3. The context window (8.1): 1M when the transcript's model (`message.model` of the last counted record, at most 128 bytes) ends in `[1m]` or starts with `claude-opus-5`, `claude-sonnet-5`, `claude-fable-5` or `claude-mythos-5`; else 200k. Whatever it is, at least 1M once the context passed 200k. The transcript never carries the `[1m]` suffix and the hooks and `<Claude config folder>/sessions/<pid>.json` carry no model (Claude Code 2.1.283), so an agent on a 4.x model asked for as `[1m]` shows 200k until its context passed 200k.
4. `agent_usage` is sent on the agent's channel when context, window or output changed. The sidebar shows "ctx 42%" after the agent's state line (rounded down).
5. Sessions panel: `sessions` entries carry `context_tokens` and `output_tokens`, counted the same way while summarizing the log (cached with the summary).
6. Sessions panel, `running` (8.16): a session runs only when a `claude` is known to run its id: the agents of Hive's terminals (their hooks name the session), and each process named `claude`, from, in order, Claude's own record `<Claude config folder>/sessions/<pid>.json` (its `sessionId`, when its `pid` is that process's), its arguments in `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` (`--session-id <id>`, else `--resume`/`-r <id>` without `--fork-session`), or an open `<id>.jsonl` in `/proc/<pid>/fd`. Ids must be UUIDs; the record and the command line are read up to 64 KiB, at most 4096 open files per process. A process none of these name (a bare `claude`, `--continue`) marks nothing. On macOS the arguments come from the `KERN_PROCARGS2` sysctl (read up to 1 MiB, arguments and environment together) and the open files from `proc_pidinfo(PROC_PIDLISTFDS)` (17.4). The app shows a session of its own terminal as "Show its terminal"; one running outside Hive has Resume and Delete disabled, without text, and "Continue in New Session" enabled.
7. Sessions panel, reading the logs (9.12): each log is read once. The service keeps, per log, how far it read (the end of its last whole line) and the summary so far; a log that grew is read from there, one that shrank or has another inode from its start, one whose inode, size and modification time are unchanged not at all. A last line without its newline counts in the summary and is read again once whole. The same summaries serve the list, `locate_session`, `delete_session` and the agents' names. The list takes the logs by modification time and reads only as many as it lists (500, plus one to tell `truncated`).

### Uninstall (23.5)
1. **Hive → Uninstall Hive…** in the app menu (macOS) and "Uninstall Hive…" in the palette (macOS) open `UninstallDialog` (`src/shell/UninstallDialog.tsx`), which asks the service `list_uninstall` on opening and on each box: "Keep my settings, spaces and accounts" (`keep_data`, off) and, when Hive made account folders, "Also remove the Claude account folders Hive made" (`accounts`, off; disabled while keeping the data). It lists exactly the paths of `uninstall_list`, says how many terminals and agents uninstalling closes, as quitting does, and that `~/.claude`, `~/.claude.json`, the projects, their worktrees and branches stay, with `/logout` to run first to remove Claude Code's login.
2. `hive::uninstall::plan` builds the list from the user's `HOME` and the service's `Paths`, only what exists, each once: the data (unless kept): every entry of Hive's folder but `bin/`, `shell/`, `hive-hooks.json` and `accounts/`, the folders of before 22.1, and on macOS `~/Library/Application Support/com.lucas.hive`, `~/Library/WebKit/com.lucas.hive` and `~/Library/Preferences/com.lucas.hive.plist`; then the account folders (each entry of `accounts/`, only when asked and the data is not kept); then the program: `bin/`, `shell/`, `hive-hooks.json`, and `~/.local/bin/hive` only when it is a link to Hive's `bin/hive` (or the old folder's); then the caches: `~/Library/Caches/com.lucas.hive`, `~/Library/Logs/Hive`, `<logs>` and the runtime folder; the app bundle last. Only what Hive writes in its folder goes (`settings.json`, `spaces.json`, `projects.json`, `ports.json`, `offered-pulls.json`, `usage.json`, `open-sessions.json`, `shared/`, `accounts/`, the program, and their `<name>.*` files such as `spaces.json.corrupt`); anything else stays, and so the folder. A folder is Hive's only when it is `~/.hive` or holds `hive-hooks.json` (a `HIVE_HOME`), and never when it is the home or holds it, links resolved; nothing named `.claude` or `.claude.json` is ever listed. Once Hive is uninstalled the service ignores the hook connections still open, and the app side drops a downloaded update, so quitting does not install it.
3. On `uninstall` the service stops serving as on a disconnect (the socket goes first, so nothing puts back the runtime folder), does not save the open sessions, ends every terminal, then removes each item in order through `uninstall::Remove` (`System`: `macos::trash` on macOS, so a mistake can be undone; deleted on Linux); one gone meanwhile is skipped. Hive's folder and its `accounts/` are then removed only if empty. It answers `uninstalled` and waits for the app to disconnect; the UI quits (closing the window), or first lists what failed.
4. `hive uninstall [--keep-data] [--accounts] [--yes]` does the same from a terminal, for when the app no longer opens (in WSL: the service's side there; native Windows keeps the installer's uninstaller): refused while a service holds the lock ("Hive is running: quit it first…"), else it holds the lock, lists the paths, asks `Uninstall Hive? [y/N]` unless `--yes`, and removes them; the app bundle it removes is `Hive.app` in `/Applications`, else `~/Applications` (`HIVE_APPLICATIONS`, an absolute folder, replaces both: the tests' own). It exits non-zero on a "no" or a failure.

### App disconnect (or SIGTERM)
The local app's connection (no `keep_secs`, #18), a `bye {end: true}`, a SIGTERM, and a remote one's after its keep time with no app back:

1. The accept loop stops and the watcher stops.
2. For every terminal, every process group in its PTY session gets SIGHUP.
3. The service waits until no process in those sessions is alive, for at most 2 s, then sends SIGKILL to what is left.
4. The socket is removed and the daemon exits 0.

fish job control puts each job in its own process group, which is why the service ends the groups of the whole session and not only fish's group. Processes that left the session with `setsid` or `nohup` survive (risk 9).

**A remote link that drops (26.15).** An app connection whose `hello` gave `keep_secs` that ends without `bye {end: true}` (a dropped link, `bye {end: false}`, or the app gone without a word) leaves the service running:

- Every terminal is kept (the service runs no chat yet, #40). Each terminal's output already fills its ring (`hive::ring`, the last 1 MiB, the oldest bytes dropped) while attached; detached from the app that went (`Output::detach`), it only fills the ring, so its PTY is **never paused** while no app is attached. 9.19's window applies again once an app attaches it (26.16).
- Hooks, states, transcripts and worktrees are processed as while an app is there; what would go to the app is dropped.
- The service ends as above once `keep_secs` passed with no app attached (`daemon::serve`'s deadline, checked again when it fires: an app that came back meanwhile cancels it, and its own drop starts a new one). `open-sessions.json` is written then, as always.
- A new connection of the same `app_id` takes over a stale one (see [Handshake](#handshake)); its terminals stay detached, writing into their rings, until the app attaches them (below).

### Reattach after a dropped link (26.16)
Q9 of `docs/spike/ssh.md`: the ring is replayed and the program redraws itself; the service runs no terminal emulator.

1. **Offsets.** A kept terminal's offset is the count of bytes of output it ever wrote (`Ring::end`, a `u64`); a terminal without a ring (the local app's) has none and is never reattached. The app's Rust side counts, per terminal, the bytes of output it passed to the UI (`Term::received` in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`).
2. **`terminals_kept`.** An app connection accepted while terminals with a ring are open gets `terminals_kept` first after `welcome` (`State::terminals_kept`, from `State::kept`: each one's `cwd`, `worktree` and `account`, kept when it opened, and its ring's end).
3. **The app answers** (`Link::kept` in `src-tauri/src/lib.rs`), on every link, a remote's messages carrying its `host` (26.17). The service is not trusted: `terminals_kept` is taken only as the first frame after the connection's `welcome` (any frame, data or terminal output included, closes that window; a second `welcome` on the same connection is ignored), on channel 0; at most 256 terminals (`MAX_KEPT`), each on a channel from 1 to 2^20 (`MAX_KEPT_CHANNEL`) given once, with `cwd`, `worktree` and `account` of at most 4096 bytes (`MAX_KEPT_TEXT`); the rest is left out. `received` counts only bytes sent to a UI (saturating), so a terminal waiting for one is replayed from 0:
   - a terminal it has with a UI output (a link that dropped and came back, 26.17): `attach_terminal {from: received}`, and `terminal_reattached {channel}` to the UI;
   - a terminal waiting for the UI's `attach_terminal` is not told again;
   - each terminal the link kept across the drop (`Link::unconfirmed`, taken at `welcome`) that the service does not list ended meanwhile: `terminal_exited {code: null}` (`Link::confirmed`); so too all of them when the window closes on another frame (the service sends `terminals_kept` only when it has some).
   - one it does not have (the app restarted): a `Term` with no output yet, and `terminal_kept {channel, cwd, worktree, account}` to the UI, `channel` being the UI's id for it (`Link::kept_id`): for a remote, always the next id of every link's, never its channel number (26.18: a remote's could use up the ids), its frames keeping their channel; for the local service, its own channel while that is past every terminal id any link gave (the shared counter then moves up to it, so new terminals' ids stay above the kept ones), else the next id. The UI opens its tab under its worktree (`reattachTerminal` in `src/terminals.ts`) and calls `attach_terminal {id, onData}`; for such a terminal that command sends `attach_terminal {from: received}` (0) instead of 18.21's `ack`.
4. **Replay** (`State::attach` → `terminal::Output::reattach`, under the output's one lock, so nothing the program writes meanwhile is lost or sent twice; an `attach_terminal` for one already going to the same connection does nothing, no replay and no answer; another connection's, a takeover's that comes before the replaced connection detached, takes it with one replay, and that late `detach` leaves it alone, since it only detaches its own queue): `from` inside the ring (`Ring::since`) gets only the bytes after it; otherwise `terminal_reset {offset}` (the ring's start) comes first, then the whole ring. Then the output goes to the new connection under 9.19's window, the replay counting as its first unacknowledged bytes.
5. **Reset in the app.** `terminal_reset` sets `received` to its `offset` and writes `ESC c` (RIS, `CLEAR_SCREEN`) into the terminal's own output `Channel`, so xterm.js clears the screen and scrollback in order with the ring that follows; the UI gets no separate message.
6. **Redraw.** The UI resizes the terminal one column wider and back (`redrawTerminal`; also after `reattachTerminal`'s attach), so a full-screen program (Claude Code) gets SIGWINCH twice and draws its screen again over the replayed bytes.
7. A kept terminal that ended before its attach: its `terminal_exited` reaches the app if it was connected then; one asked for afterwards answers `terminal_exited {code: null}`.
8. **Chats (#40):** the in-app chat was removed in 9.2; a chat is a terminal running `claude`, reattached as any other. There is no separate chat stream to replay.

### The user's `claude`
Diagnostics names the `claude` the wrapper would run (`wrapper::real_claude` on the user's `PATH`), and `claude_logins` says whether there is one (27.11). Without one the wrapper says Claude Code is not installed on this host and to install it there to run agents, with the official native installer's command as text (`wrapper::NO_CLAUDE`: `curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.sh | bash`; on Windows `irm https://claude.ai/install.ps1 | iex`), and exits 127; Hive never downloads or runs it (#52). The user's `PATH` (`wrapper::user_path`) is the one their terminals get, since the service started through `wsl.exe` lacks what their shell config adds (e.g. `~/.local/bin`): the service runs the terminals' shell once (`fish -c` on WSL, `$SHELL -l -c` on macOS and on a remote Linux service (26.14), whose own `PATH` is sshd's minimal one, with `/bin/sh` on Linux when `$SHELL` is unset or cannot run, `/bin/zsh` on macOS when it is unset; none on native Windows, where the service's own `PATH` is the user's) with a fixed `printf '%s\n' "$PATH"`, stdin and stderr closed, and takes the last line it prints when it exits successfully within 5 s after at most 64 KiB (else the service's own `PATH`), then appends `~/.local/bin` and `~/.claude/local`, where Claude Code installs itself; empty entries are dropped. It is asked for in the background the first time it is needed, never at start (9.20): Diagnostics and `gh` (a space's GitHub account, the pull requests and Actions views) wait for that answer. It is asked again, in the background, whenever no `claude` is found on it.

### Unhooked claude
1. Every 1 s the watcher lists `/proc`.
2. A terminal whose session contains a process named `claude`, with no `SessionStart` from that terminal for 5 s, gets one `unhooked_agent`.
3. The state resets when that `claude` exits.

### Worktree create (CLI and `WorktreeCreate` hook mode)
1. Find the main repository root, even from inside a linked worktree: the first entry of `git worktree list --porcelain -z`. A bare repository is refused.
2. Validate the name with `^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9._-]*$`, the same rule as the UI. Refuse an existing directory. An existing `worktree-<name>` branch is refused by git itself, and a `--base` starting with `-` is refused.
3. Collect the `.worktreeinclude` files before creating anything. `git ls-files --others --ignored --exclude-from=.worktreeinclude` finds the matches and `git check-ignore` keeps only the gitignored ones.
4. Run `git worktree add -b worktree-<name> .claude/worktrees/<name> <base>`. The base is a local or remote branch, or HEAD. A remote base (`<remote>/<branch>` for a remote `git remote` lists, the longest that fits; never one whose branch has a `:`) is fetched first (13.2, `worktree::fetch_base`, before `create`): `git fetch --no-tags -- <remote> refs/heads/<branch>` with `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0` and `GCM_INTERACTIVE=never` (both added to `WSLENV`, so Git Credential Manager under WSL never asks either) within 30 s; from the app with its project's space environment (`spaces::vars` and the GitHub account's token) and before the worktree-change turn, from the CLI with the terminal's own. A failed fetch only adds the note "could not fetch <base>: <git's first line>; created from the last fetched copy". A local base and no base (so the hook) fetch nothing.
5. Copy the included regular files. Existing paths are never overwritten and symlinks are never followed. Each file is a copy-on-write clone where the filesystem allows (28.9, `hive::cow`: macOS `fclonefileat` on APFS, Linux's `FICLONE` on Btrfs/XFS), else a plain copy of its bytes; the source is opened with no link in any part of its path (`file::PlainFiles`, so a folder swapped for a link after git listed it fails the create instead of reading from outside the project) and must be a regular file, the destination is always a new file (`O_EXCL`).
6. Print only the path. In hook mode, any failure exits non-zero with an empty stdout.

`create` returns the path and its notes, which the CLI prints on stderr as `hive: <note>` and the service sends to the app: a warning when the project has its own `WorktreeCreate` hook in `.claude/settings{,.local}.json`, and how many `.worktreeinclude` files were copied. `remove` and `hook-remove` run `git worktree remove` without `--force` and keep the branch. `hook-remove` only removes paths directly under `<repo>/.claude/worktrees/`. Both hooks are registered in `hive-hooks.json` (see below).

### Worktree hooks
1. Claude Code, through the wrapper's settings, runs `<abs path>/hive worktree hook-create` for `WorktreeCreate` (60 s timeout: `git worktree add` plus the `.worktreeinclude` copy) and `hive worktree hook-remove` for `WorktreeRemove` (10 s timeout), in exec form. The hook input is at most 64 KiB.
2. Each does its work as above. Only on success, it then forwards the call to the service like `hive hook` (same `hello` + `hook`, 200 ms, `HIVE_TERMINAL_ID`), as event `WorktreeCreate` with the created path added as `worktree_path`, or `WorktreeRemove` unchanged. Forwarding never changes stdout nor the exit code.
3. The `ClaudeCode` adapter maps them to `worktree_created {name, path}` and `worktree_removed {path}`, keeping `session_id`, `cwd` and `agent_id`/`agent_type` (a subagent's worktree). They do not change agent states.
4. On either one the service lists the projects again off the frame loop and sends `projects`, so a `claude -w` or subagent worktree appears, and a removed one disappears, without "Refresh worktrees". It does so after updating the agent states, so `projects` follows any `agent_state` the call caused. Any other `git worktree add/remove/prune` (an agent's Bash, `hive worktree remove`, a terminal outside Hive) reaches the app through `hive::registry`. `State::worktrees_changed` is the single point for both: it sends `projects` only when some project's worktrees differ from what the app was last sent (every reply carrying projects records them), and runs in turn with the app's own create and remove (and with `list_projects`, `add_project` and `remove_project`, so a list it made earlier never follows theirs), so each change reaches the app once. A worktree folder deleted by hand leaves git's registry alone: it disappears on the next list (git marks it prunable, and prunable worktrees are hidden).
5. The store replaces its list on every `projects`. A selected worktree that is no longer listed leaves its project selected (nothing, if the project went too), and `worktree:<id>` collapsed keys of unlisted worktrees are dropped. Its terminal tabs stay open. An agent placed in it keeps its `worktree` id, so, like an agent outside every project, it is no longer shown in the tree but still counts as pending and F8 still reaches its tab.

## Files written

| Path | Mode | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `<runtime>/hive.sock` | 0600 | Service socket. |
| `<runtime>/hive.lock` | 0600 | Single-instance lock; holds the holder's process id (26.17, for `hive bridge --replace`). |
| `<data>/hive/bin/hive` on a remote host | 0755, folder 0700 | Written by the macOS app's upload (26.17, [Remote hosts over ssh](#remote-hosts-over-ssh-2617) item 3): the bundled `hive-linux-<cpu>`, first as `hive.new` (umask 077), renamed over `hive` once its size, SHA-256 and `--version` are checked; a failed check removes `hive.new`. |
| `<logs>/daemon.log` | 0600 | stderr of a daemon started by the bridge; the previous start's as `daemon.log.1`. `<logs>` is `~/Library/Logs/Hive` on macOS (D18.6) when `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` is unset, else the runtime dir. |
| `~/Library/Logs/Hive/app.log` | 0600 | macOS: the app's stderr and panics (`app.dev.log` for a development build); the previous launch's as `app.log.1`. |
| `<data>/hive/bin/claude` | 0755 | Wrapper: finds the real `claude` on `PATH` (skipping the bin dir and itself). If `HIVE_WRAPPED` is unset it exports it and adds `--settings`, and `--plugin-dir <data>/hive/shared` while that is a folder (21.9); otherwise it runs the real claude unchanged. On Windows `claude.exe` and `hive.exe`, copies of `hive` (12.5.5). |
| `<data>/hive/shared/` | 0700 | The plugin every Claude account shares (21.9, see [The shared plugin](#the-shared-plugin-219)): made when missing with `skills/`, `agents/`, `commands/` and `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (0644, never overwritten); the rest is the user's. |
| `<data>/hive/spaces.json` | 0600 | The spaces, their projects and environments, and the current space (see [Spaces](#spaces-614)). |
| `<data>/hive/projects.json` | 0600 | The followed projects' paths before spaces (read only while `spaces.json` is missing). |
| `<data>/hive/ports.json` | 0600 | JSON object: worktree path → first port of its block of 10. |
| `<config>/hive/settings.json` | 0600 | The user's settings (see [Settings](#settings)); written only when the app saves them. |
| `<data>/hive/hive-hooks.json` | 0644 | One exec-form hook per observed event (16 events, `hive hook <Event>`, 1 s) plus `WorktreeCreate` → `hive worktree hook-create` (60 s) and `WorktreeRemove` → `hive worktree hook-remove` (10 s), and `statusLine` → `'<hive>' statusline` (12.1). On Windows the hooks run `bin\hive.exe` and `statusLine` has no quotes when it can (12.5.5). |
| `<app config>/.window-state.json` | 0644 | The window's size, position, maximised and full screen (`tauri-plugin-window-state`, 17.18); `<app config>` is the OS's config folder plus the app's `identifier` (`~/Library/Application Support/com.lucas.hive` on macOS). |
| `/tmp/<identifier>_si.sock` | umask | macOS: `tauri-plugin-single-instance`'s socket while the app runs (17.18). `/tmp` is shared by every local user and the plugin fixes the path: another user who binds it first makes every launch hand over to them and exit silently. The fix is to delete the file and launch again. |

`<data>/hive` and `<config>/hive` are both Hive's folder on Unix (22.1, D22.16): `$HIVE_HOME`, else `~/.hive` (mode 0700); the XDG data and config folders no longer move it. On Unix a `XDG_*`, `HOME` or `TMPDIR` that is not an absolute path counts as unset (the XDG spec; a relative one would resolve against a terminal's folder). `<runtime>` is `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR/hive`; when that is unset, on macOS the user's own temporary folder's `hive` (`confstr(_CS_DARWIN_USER_TEMP_DIR)`, the `$TMPDIR` launchd gives the user's processes, so a `TMPDIR` a shell changes does not hide the service from `hive hook`; `$TMPDIR` itself only if the system does not answer, and only when it is a folder of the user's that others cannot write to, else `/tmp/hive-<uid>`; D17.5, 17.9), elsewhere `/tmp/hive-<uid>`. Nobody else can create a folder in the user's temporary folder, which closes risk 9.7 on macOS. On native Windows (12.5.2) `<runtime>` is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\hive\run` and `<data>/hive` is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\hive`; there is no socket file (the service's named pipe instead) and no mode: files get their folder's ACL.

## Run and test

```
export PATH=$HOME/.cargo/bin:$PATH
cargo build -p hive                         # target/debug/hive
cargo test --workspace                      # unit + integration (tests/integration)
cargo llvm-cov --workspace --fail-under-lines 100
cargo mutants --gitignore true --in-diff <(git diff main -- '*.rs')   # needs cargo-nextest; TMPDIR on disk: /tmp is a small tmpfs
cargo bench -p hive --bench hook_latency    # p99 target < 20 ms
cargo bench -p hive-protocol                # codec throughput (criterion)
cd crates/hive-protocol && cargo +nightly fuzz run --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu decode
```

Integration tests run the real `hive` binary with a temporary `HOME` and `XDG_*` (`tests/integration/common.rs`).
- fish runs with that temporary config.
- A stand-in `claude` is built with `cc` from `tests/fixtures/fake_claude.c` (macOS 26 kills a copied system binary run from another folder), so the real Claude Code never runs.
- When a test ends, every process still carrying the test's `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` is killed, so failing tests do not leak processes.
- `llvm-cov` records a spawned `hive` only if it exits normally, so tests stop daemons with SIGTERM or by closing the app connection.

### Frontend without Tauri

Outside Tauri (a plain browser, `bun run dev`, Playwright) or with `?mock` in the URL, `src/transport/mock.ts` stands in for the service (a dynamic import, loaded only then: the startup bundle does not carry it, checked by `src/transport/bundle.test.ts` on a `vite build`): it answers `welcome` (distribution "Ubuntu"), `settings` (the defaults; `set_settings` keeps new ones in memory, unchecked), `spaces` (one "Default" space holding the initial projects; space requests follow the service's rules in memory, without the folder checks, and `list_sessions` lists the current space's sessions, sent again unasked after `delete_session` as the service does) and `projects` (two of three fake repositories under `/home/user`; `?mock=empty` starts with none, and `add_project` accepts only the fake paths, `/home/user/work` among them, a group of two repositories (14.1); `list_dirs` answers folders under `/mnt/share/` with a network volume's `warning`, and hidden `/home/user/.claude-work` (a Claude config folder) and `/home/user/.config/gh-work`; branches, name checks and new worktrees follow the CLI's wording, with a long remote branch list in `shop`; `list_changes` answers `MOCK_CHANGES`, sample changes after screen 1g, against the base asked (the same files either way; the main worktree falls back to `HEAD` as the service does), and `open_file` a sample text shaped by the file's status there, `MOCK_TEXTS` for `src/auth/session.ts`, a `.png` or a `.pdf` as binary with its `media` from a `data:` URL, 24.2), or with `?mock=mismatch` / `?mock=disconnected` a `version_mismatch` / `disconnected` (as an installed app, `bundled` true) instead; `?mock=choose` plays the first run on Windows with WSL: `app_mode` with no mode, and the rest once `set_mode` picks one (native: `welcome` without a distribution, and `list_dirs` answered as a native service does: Windows paths as typed, `C:` for a name without a separator); and each terminal prints `mock$ `, echoes input, repeats the line on Enter and exits on `exit`; `cd <dir>` moves it and `claude` sends `agent_detected` placed at the worktree whose path is exactly that directory, then `agent_state` idle; every later line sets that agent working (`agent_removed` when the terminal exits); `worktree-remove <name>` stands in for a `WorktreeRemove` hook, dropping that Claude worktree and sending `projects`; `hive badge <text>` / `hive badge --clear` sends `badge`. `?mock=states` adds, without terminals, agents in every state, two of them with subagents, one subagent owning a worktree (`tests-login` in `shop`, no row: its path is the subagent line's tooltip), with fake activities, times and context (`agent_usage`); the fake sessions carry made-up token totals. `?mock=load` replays a recording into every terminal (see Load test below). `bun run e2e` needs `libnss3` and `libnspr4`; without root, extract them with `apt-get download` + `dpkg -x` and point `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` at them.

### Load test (1.11, #28)

`e2e/load.e2e.ts` (Playwright project `load`, run after the other specs, alone) opens 20 terminals with `?mock=load`: the mock replays a recording into each one through the real path (transport `onData` → terminal manager → xterm.js), at recorded timing, starting 0.5 s after the terminal opens plus 100 ms per terminal id. After a 3 s warm-up it types into the focused terminal for 12 s (10 keys/s) and measures:
- **input latency**: key `timeStamp` → the echo parsed (the mock appends `OSC 7777` to every echo in this mode) → the next animation frame;
- **frames**: every `requestAnimationFrame` interval; **long tasks**: `PerformanceObserver("longtask")`;
- **dropped output**: after the replays end, every hidden terminal's buffer must equal a fresh xterm of the same size fed the whole recording at once.

Pass: input p95 < 50 ms (typing starts to feel laggy past ~50 ms; native terminals sit at 10–40 ms) and p99 < 100 ms (RAIL response budget), frame p95 < 33.3 ms (30 fps), no long task ≥ 100 ms, nothing dropped. Results go to `target/e2e/load.json`.

**Recording.** `src/transport/replay.ts` generates a deterministic ~19 s Claude Code-like session: three turns of prompt, spinner with shimmer (one truecolor per character) + todo list + input box redrawn at 12.5 Hz the way Ink does it (DEC 2026 synchronized output, erase-line + cursor-up over the whole dynamic region), streamed answer redrawn every 33 ms, and an 80-line diff with truecolor backgrounds flushed in 4 KiB chunks. 449 KB, ~23 KB/s per terminal, ~460 KB/s for 20. To use a real capture instead, record one with asciinema (v2 or v3 cast, `asciinema rec --command claude target/claude.cast`), put it under the repo so Vite serves it, and run with `HIVE_LOAD_CAST=/target/claude.cast` (or open `/?mock=load&cast=/target/claude.cast`); only output events are replayed, and the end-of-recording check is skipped.

**GPU.** The spec launches Chromium with ANGLE on the system GL (`--use-gl=angle --use-angle=gl`), and inside WSL sets `GALLIUM_DRIVER=d3d12` with `/usr/lib/wsl/lib`, so WebGL runs on the Windows GPU. Chromium's default SwiftShader saturates the main thread with a single replaying terminal (~11 fps) and says nothing about a real GPU; Mesa's llvmpipe (no GPU) competes with the page for CPU and fails the thresholds.

**Result (2026-09-23, Chromium headless on Linux/WSL2, Intel Iris Xe through D3D12, machine otherwise idle, three runs):** input p50 28.5–30.3 ms, p95 36.4–38.0 ms, p99 38.1–43.9 ms, max 39–64 ms; frame p50 and p95 16.7 ms (60 fps), max 33–117 ms; no long task; nothing dropped. Inside the full `bun run e2e`: p95 38.7 ms, p99 62.5 ms, max 175 ms. **Pass.** While another agent was compiling Rust (load average 8–16 on 12 cores) the same test failed (input p95 70–1400 ms, with stalls of 0.4–2 s that had no long task, so outside the page), so timings are only meaningful on a quiet machine.

**Waste found (not fixed):** a hidden terminal loses its WebGL addon and xterm.js falls back to its DOM renderer. On every scroll xterm refreshes the selection, and `RenderService.handleSelectionChanged` makes the DOM renderer rebuild every row even though rendering is paused; in a `display: none` pane each glyph width measures 0, is never cached, and is measured again (`WidthCache._measure`, a forced layout). With 20 terminals this was ~35% of the main thread in a CPU profile. It still passes, but it is margin lost on slower machines; disabling that call for hidden terminals (xterm internals) removed it entirely in an experiment.

**This is not WebView2 on Windows.** To re-run in the real app: start it with `WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS=--remote-debugging-port=9222` and point the spec at it with Playwright's `chromium.connectOverCDP("http://localhost:9222")` after navigating the window to `?mock=load` (the transport honours `?mock` inside Tauri); or, by hand, open `?mock=load` in the debug build's DevTools and run the same probes.

### Windows app during development

The code and every build stay in WSL (TODO 1.0, human decision 2026-09-23). `scripts/win-dev.sh`:
1. cross-compiles `hive-app` for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` with `cargo xwin`, with a static CRT because a stock Windows has no VC++ redistributable;
2. copies the `.exe` to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\hive-dev`;
3. builds `target/debug/hive` and exports `HIVE_BRIDGE` (that path) and `HIVE_WSL_DISTRO` through `WSLENV`, so the app's bridge runs the development build;
4. starts Vite in WSL on port 1420. The debug build loads `devUrl`, which Windows reaches through WSL localhost forwarding, so hot reload works;
5. opens the app through PowerShell, because launching a Windows `.exe` straight from WSL interop fails.

With WSL there, the first run asks where the service runs: WSL runs the development build above. Native mode runs a Windows `hive.exe` (`native_bridge`); the development build bundles none, so `HIVE_BRIDGE` must then be the Windows path of one built with `cargo xwin build -p hive --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` (`win-dev.sh` exports the Linux build, for WSL mode). The settings' Service select switches back.

One-time setup: `sudo apt install clang lld llvm`, `rustup target add x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`, `cargo install cargo-xwin --locked`, `bun install`. The first build downloads the MSVC CRT and Windows SDK into `~/.cache/cargo-xwin`. Release bundles (MSI/NSIS) are not covered yet.
